OBJECTIVE: Each of these 46 Open findings of `SECURITY_FINDINGS.md` MUST be fixed under the constraints below, through its own `CRITICAL Directive:` and in this order: DEP-08, DEP-09, DEP-10, SEC-01, SEC-02, SEC-20, DEP-11, DEP-12, SEC-04, DEP-13, DEP-14, DEP-15, DEP-16, SEC-10, SEC-26, SEC-27, SEC-33, SEC-35, SEC-36, SEC-41, SEC-08, SEC-13, SEC-42, SEC-07, SEC-23, SEC-06, SEC-09, SEC-17, SEC-29, SEC-34, SEC-05, DEP-19, SEC-38, SEC-16, SEC-30, DEP-17, SEC-11, SEC-31, SEC-12, SEC-22, SEC-28, SEC-40, SEC-43, SEC-15, SEC-25, SEC-39.

CONSTRAINTS:
- Validate fixes only through `mvn clean install` and in-JVM unit or integration tests that use mocked requests or MockMvc.
- Never start services or run exploits. Never send requests to any host other than the declared package registries (Maven Central, Shibboleth, JBoss, npm registry), the Node.js distribution host `nodejs.org` and scanner database sources.
- Preserve all REST endpoints, `user.mode` values, existing property keys and module boundaries.
- Make minimal changes only, and add each regression test to the existing test module of the class it covers.
- The validation command is `mvn clean install -P '!itest' -Dsurefire.excludesFile=<path> -Dinvoker.skip=true`. The excludes file MUST sit outside the working tree and list exactly `**/MailConfigValidatorTest.java`. Every directive MUST use this command for build validation: gate 1 counts the tests it runs, and it runs the gate 2 regression tests. The SEC-20 commented-lines diff check of gate 2 and every gate 3 check MUST use only the checks their own `PASS GATES:` bullets prescribe, NEVER this command.
- The `itest` profile, which holds the ITEST suites and both archetype builds (`pom.xml:1833-1836`), and `MailConfigValidatorTest` MUST NOT run, because they start Kafka, Jetty, identity and storage containers or an in-JVM SMTP server.
- The Invoker builds of `Util/openl-maven-plugin` MUST NOT run, because they start Jetty: `Util/openl-maven-plugin/pom.xml:118-163` runs the fixture projects under `Util/openl-maven-plugin/it/` with `clean verify` inside `mvn clean install`, their `verify` phase runs `openl:verify` (`Util/openl-maven-plugin/resources/META-INF/plexus/components.xml:39-41`), and `openl:verify` starts Jetty and requests its readiness endpoint (`Util/openl-maven-plugin/src/org/openl/rules/maven/VerifyMojo.java:108-110`, `Util/openl-maven-plugin/src/org/openl/rules/maven/AppServer.java:55-77`). `-P '!itest'` and the excludes file do not stop them, because `Util/openl-maven-plugin` is a default module (`pom.xml:66`) and Invoker builds are not Surefire tests. `-Dinvoker.skip=true` skips both Invoker goals, as the root `skipTests` profile does (`pom.xml:1817`), and the Surefire tests of `Util/openl-maven-plugin` still run.
- `-DskipTests`, `-o`, `npm_config_offline`, the `npm.*.skip` properties (`pom.xml:226-228`), `skip.installnodenpm` and `skip.npm` MUST NOT be used. The Vitest suites of `STUDIO/studio-ui` MUST run, so the SEC-04 UI changes are validated.
- Maven artifacts MUST come only from the declared repositories: Maven Central, `shibboleth-releases` and `jboss-releases`. npm 12.0.2 (`pom.xml:225`) and every npm package MUST come only from the npm registry `registry.npmjs.org`.
- The build MUST use the Node v24.21.0 archive (`pom.xml:224`) from the `frontend-maven-plugin` cache in the local Maven repository (`com/github/eirslett/node/`) whenever that cache holds it. When the cache lacks it, the plugin downloads it from `nodejs.org`, its default download root. The build MUST NOT set `nodeDownloadRoot` and MUST NEVER fetch Node.js from any other host.
- Preflight: before every run of the validation command, the run MUST confirm from the POMs that none sets a `skipInvocation` or `skipInstallation` element, because a POM value overrides `-Dinvoker.skip=true`, and that no module the command builds uses `openl` packaging, because its `verify` phase runs `openl:verify`; the fixture projects under `Util/openl-maven-plugin/it/` are not modules. When either check fails, the validation command MUST NOT run: before the first directive that refusal is the baseline failure, inside a directive it fails that directive's gate, and the Project Guide names the POM line that caused it.
- Baseline: before the first directive, the preflight and the validation command MUST run once on the unchanged tree. When either fails, no directive changes code: every directive is recorded as failed with the baseline error, and the Project Guide lists the failure and what unblocks it, for example a Node v24.21.0 archive missing from the plugin cache whose download from `nodejs.org` failed.
- Version lookups read only the `maven-metadata.xml` of a declared Maven repository and `npm view <package> versions` against `registry.npmjs.org`.
- Target publication preflight: every version a Dependency upgrade directive names, whether a register first fixed version or a benchmark, is scanner data, not registry evidence. Before a directive writes a version into `pom.xml` or `STUDIO/studio-ui/package.json`, the run MUST confirm that the version lookup for that package lists it, and the run MUST accept a candidate only after the directive's scoped gate-3 check (the Dependency-Check check for a Maven unit, the scoped `npm audit` check for an npm unit) reports none of the unit's owned advisories against it. A named version that the lookup does not list, or that the scoped check still reports, is skipped, and the target becomes the lowest later release the lookup lists that passes the scoped check and the directive's own conditions. NEVER pin a version the lookup does not list. NEVER replace a register value with a value from any other source: a later listed release becomes the target and never rewrites the register's fixed version. NEVER describe the selected release as free of advisories beyond the owned IDs its scoped check covers. The Project Guide records, for each Dependency upgrade directive, every candidate checked with whether the lookup listed it and what the scoped check reported, and the release selected.
- Migration notes: the Default-changing directives create `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`, and the remediation run is the only run that creates it. The first Default-changing directive that runs writes the file and its header; every Default-changing directive adds one entry stating what flipping its default breaks and how users upgrade.
- Switch pattern for every Default-changing directive except SEC-20: each new property goes into the named `openl-default.properties` beside its neighbours, with a comment in the neighbouring style, and its default MUST preserve the audited runtime behavior exactly. Names use dotted lowercase segments with kebab-case words, booleans read `allow-...` or `....enabled`, and every key MUST match `openl.config.key-pattern.allowed` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:51`).
- Startup WARN: the named owner class logs one `WARN` line for each insecure condition of the effective configuration, so a partially hardened configuration still warns. Each line names the finding ID, the property and the recommended secure value. `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfig.java:37-60` is the conditional-warning pattern: it logs its warning (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfig.java:57-58`) only while the insecure value is selected. That warning names the property and its insecure value but neither a finding ID nor a secure value, so every new line adds both.
- Regression tests follow `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfigTest.java`: every property gets a test at its default and at its secure value, and a finding with two properties also gets one partially hardened test that sets one property secure, keeps the other at its default and asserts the remaining `WARN`. When a finding's two properties belong to OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services (SEC-02, SEC-11, SEC-16), whose modules do not depend on each other, the partially hardened state MUST be proven per application, as that directive's `Tests:` states. Test classes and methods stay package-private JUnit 5.
- Switch value validation: the class that reads a switch property MUST parse and validate the value once, when it first reads it and before any use, and MUST refuse a value outside the domain its directive's `Required fix:` states with an explicit error whose message names the property (both properties for a rule between two), the rejected value and the accepted values, as `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfig.java:43-45` does for `security.password.encoder`. The refusal fails OpenL Studio or OpenL Rule Services startup, or for a Kafka switch the deployment of the Kafka-published service, and NEVER clamps the value, ignores it or replaces it with the default. Surrounding whitespace is trimmed before parsing, and an unset property takes its shipped default. A numeric switch is a decimal integer of the Java type its directive names: a blank, non-integer or out-of-range value is refused, and zero and negative values are accepted only where the directive gives them a meaning. Every documented default and sentinel stays valid with its current meaning. Each directive's `Tests:` cover its refused values, and an unset property that behaves as its shipped default, beside its default and secure states; `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfigTest.java:61-67` is the pattern for a refused-value test.
- Boolean switch grammar: every boolean switch property a directive adds accepts exactly `true` or `false`, trimmed and compared case-insensitively; the class that reads it MUST refuse every other value, a blank one included, with the property-naming error and at the failure point of the rule above, and an unset property takes its shipped default. That class MUST read the raw string and parse it itself, NEVER through Spring's conversion, because `StringToBooleanConverter` in `spring-core` 6.2.19 (`pom.xml:90`) and `CustomBooleanEditor` in `spring-beans` also accept `on`, `yes` and `1` as `true` and `off`, `no` and `0` as `false`, and neither refuses a blank value with the property-naming error. The one exception is `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled`, whose blank value means its default `false` and whose other refused values fail repository creation, as the SEC-22 directive states, matching how `RepositoryInstatiator.setParams` skips blank values (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:117-119`). This grammar and its tests apply to every boolean switch whether or not its directive's `Tests:` names them: each boolean switch's tests MUST prove that `yes` and a blank value are refused with the property-naming error, except that a blank `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled` behaves as its default, and that an unset property behaves as its shipped default.
- Any code comment a fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT. NEVER log a password, token secret, private key or session identifier.
- A class, test class or test root labelled new does not exist at the audited commit: the directive or hand-off item that introduces it MUST create it at the named path, and a new test MUST sit in the test tree of the module that owns the code it covers.

FAILURE HANDLING:
- When a directive fails its gate after 3 attempts with the same error, revert that directive's changes, record the failure, and continue with the next directive.
- A failed directive never blocks the others.
- Gate scope: a directive's gate covers only its own finding IDs, and for a Dependency upgrade only the occurrences its upgrade unit owns. A finding owned by another directive NEVER fails, and NEVER reverts, a directive.
- A revert MUST undo only the reverted directive's own edits; edits that other directives made to a shared file stay in place.

PASS GATES:
- The build passes with 100% of existing tests passing.
- Each code or switch fix has at least 1 regression test per property state.
- OWASP Dependency-Check reports zero CVSS ≥ 7.0 findings that have an available fixed version; findings with no fixed version are documented as "no fix available" with the upstream tracking link.
- Gate 1 counts the tests the validation command runs. The ITEST suites, the archetype builds, the `Util/openl-maven-plugin` Invoker builds and `MailConfigValidatorTest` are reported as not run, NEVER as passed.
- Gate 2: SEC-20 has no property state, so its gate is the commented-lines diff check of its directive.
- Gate 3 collection: Dependency-Check MUST run through the `owasp` profile as a direct goal, `mvn -B -Powasp org.owasp:dependency-check-maven:13.0.0:aggregate`, with `-Dowasp.failBuildOnCVSS=11 -DversionCheckEnabled=false -DskipTestScope=false -DassemblyAnalyzerEnabled=false -DcentralAnalyzerEnabled=false -DossIndexAnalyzerEnabled=false -DnodeAuditAnalyzerEnabled=false -DyarnAuditAnalyzerEnabled=false -DpnpmAuditAnalyzerEnabled=false "-DnvdDatafeedUrl=https://nvd.nist.gov/feeds/json/cve/2.0/nvdcve-2.0-{0}.json.gz"`, `-DdataDirectory=<path>` outside the working tree, and `-Dodc.outputDirectory=<path>` with `-Dformats=JSON` so the JSON report is written outside the working tree. Collection MUST NOT pass `-P '!itest'` or `-DskipTests`, so the ITEST modules that own DEP-08 and DEP-12 occurrences stay in scope; as a direct goal it runs no test.
- Gate 3 database update: the first collection run MUST use `-DautoUpdate=true`, reaching only `nvd.nist.gov`, `www.cisa.gov`, `raw.githubusercontent.com` and `dependency-check.github.io`. When that run fails during the update and the data directory already holds a populated database, collection reruns with `-DautoUpdate=false`. With no populated database, gate 3 is recorded as "not executed: database update failed: `<error>`". The `-DnvdDatafeedUrl` flag of gate 3 collection is what makes the update read the NVD JSON feeds on `nvd.nist.gov`; without it Dependency-Check uses its NVD API host `services.nvd.nist.gov`, which is not an allowed host, so a collection run without the flag MUST NOT run, and the host list is NEVER widened to admit that API host.
- Gate 3 threshold: collection always passes `-Dowasp.failBuildOnCVSS=11`. No CVSS score exceeds 10, so collection never fails on a finding, even after a human enables the SEC-14 gate, and the gate and the register measure the same scope.
- Gate 3 scan failure: with a threshold of 11, a non-zero collection exit after the update is a scan failure (an unreadable database, an analysis error or an unresolvable artifact). Gate 3 is then recorded as "not executed: scan failure: `<error>`". That is NEVER a pass and NEVER a policy failure; it reverts nothing, and the Project Guide lists it.
- Gate 3 enforcement: a checker script outside the working tree reads the collected JSON and owns the gate result. It fails every vulnerability whose CVSS v3.x base score is 7.0 or higher unless an exemption applies. A fix counts as available whenever no exemption applies, including when the fixed version is "not identified in scanner data". The only exemptions, both read from `SECURITY_FINDINGS.md`, are an occurrence the register marks Not-applicable with its evidence, and a "no fix available" row with its upstream tracking link that carries one of exactly two forms of affirmative evidence: the advisory's own affected range is open-ended, for example an NVD configuration whose matching CPE range has no `versionEnd*` bound, and no source names a fixed release for that package in any release line; or a vendor disposition `will_not_fix`, `fix_deferred` or `end_of_life` whose stated scope covers every release line of the package. A disposition scoped to one release line or distribution rules out only that line, so a fixed release in another line counts as available, and a disposition or an open-ended range NEVER exempts an advisory for which any source names a fixed release. Missing metadata NEVER qualifies: a Trivy `Status` `affected` without a `FixedVersion`, an absent field, or a range that only ends `versionEndIncluding` the resolved version earns no exemption.
- Gate 3 npm scope: collection disables the Dependency-Check Node, Yarn and pnpm audit analyzers, so its report carries no `STUDIO/studio-ui` advisory. For an npm upgrade unit the scoped check therefore reads `npm audit --package-lock-only --json --registry=https://registry.npmjs.org/` run in `STUDIO/studio-ui`, which reads only `package-lock.json`, installs nothing and runs no script, and applies the same CVSS rule and exemptions to the GHSA IDs that unit owns.
- Gate 3 npm scan failure: an `npm audit` check counts as executed only when `npm audit` exits 0 or 1 and writes a parseable JSON report whose `auditReportVersion` is 2 and that carries a `vulnerabilities` object. `npm audit` exits 1 whenever it reports an advisory, so exit 1 with such a report is an executed check, not a failure. Every other outcome (another exit status, an error-only JSON such as an `error` object without a report, a missing or unparseable report, or another report version) is a scan failure, and that check is then recorded as "not executed: npm scan failure: `<error>`". That is NEVER a pass and NEVER a policy failure; it reverts nothing, and the Project Guide lists it.
- Gate 3 per directive: a Dependency upgrade directive runs the enforcement check only on the occurrences its upgrade unit owns; other directives do not run gate 3.
- Gate 3 final aggregate: after every directive has run, the enforcement check runs once over the whole Dependency-Check report and the `STUDIO/studio-ui` `npm audit` report. That `npm audit` run follows the npm scan failure rule: when it is not executed, the aggregate records "not executed: npm scan failure: `<error>`" for its `STUDIO/studio-ui` part and NEVER reports that part as passed. Its failures go to the Project Guide hand-off, and it reverts nothing.

PROJECT GUIDE HAND-OFF:
the final Project Guide lists, as human tasks:
- every switch property with its recommended secure value, what flipping it breaks, and the target release;
- every Operator-only finding;
- every failed directive with its error;
- actual generated LOC against the estimate for each finding.
- The target release for every switch flip is 6.6.0, the next minor release after `6.5.0`, unless a directive states otherwise.
- The Operator-only findings are [SEC-18](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-18), [SEC-19](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-19), [SEC-21](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-21), [SEC-24](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-24) and [SEC-44](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-44); section 6 of `SECURITY_FINDINGS.md` carries their actions, and no directive changes code for them.
- Beside the [SEC-13](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-13) switch, the guide MUST list as a human task the proxy rate limit that is mandatory in `ad` mode at the secure value: the proxy MUST rate-limit the OpenL Studio login form and HTTP Basic requests per client, because a client that locks 10000 names fills the lockout table and every account without an entry is then refused, its correct password included, until those locks end. Section 6 of `SECURITY_FINDINGS.md` carries the action, and the guide NEVER lists the `ad`-mode flip without it.
- The guide lists the tests not run (the ITEST suites, the two archetype builds, the `Util/openl-maven-plugin` Invoker builds and `MailConfigValidatorTest`) and the behavior no in-JVM test verifies: container-level refusal of an oversized multipart upload (SEC-26), and a database's evaluation of the purge delete restriction against an update committed between the select and the delete, which the mocked Criteria tests prove only as the restriction the purge builds (SEC-12).
- The guide lists a baseline failure with what unblocks it, every gate 3 that was not executed with its reason, and the result of the final aggregate gate.
- Human decision [SEC-03](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-03): no directive was written for SEC-03, and a human MUST decide and apply this recommendation. Estimated LOC: Production: 290–520; Test: 1059–1703; Configuration and build: 14–26; Documentation: 10–15.
  - Switch property `rules.block-system-classes`, default `false`, secure value `true`: a constant and accessor in `OpenLSystemProperties` beside `DISPATCHING_VALIDATION` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLSystemProperties.java:9-17`), declared in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/resources/openl-default.properties`, following the `dispatching.validation` precedent at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:21` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/resources/openl-default.properties:31`. The engine reads external parameters first and the JVM system property second (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLSystemProperties.java:19-27`) and never reads Spring default files.
  - `WARN` owners: `RuleServiceOpenLServiceInstantiationFactoryImpl` for OpenL Rule Services and a proposed new OpenL Studio startup configuration bean, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SystemClassPolicyConfiguration.java`, modelled on `PasswordEncoderConfig`; each logs one `WARN` naming SEC-03, the property and `true` while the value is `false`. `WebStudio` MUST NOT own it, because each user session creates one.
  - Propagation: add the key to the `externalParameters` props of the `ruleServiceInstantiationFactory` bean (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/resources/openl-ruleservice-core-beans.xml:18-22`), which `RuleServiceOpenLServiceInstantiationFactoryImpl` passes into compilation (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/core/RuleServiceOpenLServiceInstantiationFactoryImpl.java:50`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/core/RuleServiceOpenLServiceInstantiationFactoryImpl.java:74`), and copy it in `WebStudio` beside `copyExternalProperty(OpenLSystemProperties.DISPATCHING_VALIDATION)` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/ui/WebStudio.java:185-186`), so every compilation that uses its external properties (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/ui/WebStudio.java:869`) receives it.
  - Policy before resolver construction: `XlsBinder.bind` builds the module's `OpenL` and `TypeResolver` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:189-196`) before the external parameters reach the binding context (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:222-223`). The change MUST read the effective policy at the start of `bind` from `parsedCode.getExternalParams()` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/syntax/code/IParsedCode.java:51`), external parameter first and JVM property second; in a module compilation that map is the compilation's policy snapshot (next item), so the read returns the snapshot's explicit value. It MUST pass that value through `makeOpenL` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:443`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:470`) to the `TypeResolver` constructor. The default resolver (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/OpenL.java:44`) reads the JVM property.
  - One policy snapshot per compilation: the parameters one compilation reads come from mutable sources at different times. `OpenLCompileManager.processSource` hands the caller's own `source.getParams()` map to the parsed code by reference (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLCompileManager.java:107-110`) and reads that map again for external dependencies (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLCompileManager.java:248-256`), `BindingContext.setExternalParams` copies it later (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/BindingContext.java:243-246`, called at `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:222-223`, after the `OpenL` and its `TypeResolver` were built at `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:189-196`), and the accessor falls back to the global JVM property whenever the key is absent (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLSystemProperties.java:19-27`). Separate policy reads by the factory, the binder and the member checks therefore see different values when the map or the property changes during a compilation, so a module bound under one policy can be cached under the other. Each compilation MUST resolve the policy exactly once into an immutable snapshot: an unmodifiable copy of the source module's parameters, taken once, in which `rules.block-system-classes` holds the effective value resolved from that copy first and the JVM property second, so the precedence stays unchanged. `RulesEngineFactory` MUST take the snapshot under its lock and hand it to the compilation through a new `OpenLManager.compileModuleWithErrors` overload that receives it (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLManager.java:170-176`, called at `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:147`); the existing overload and every other public signature stay unchanged, and a compilation that enters through them takes its own snapshot at the start of `processSource`, so every module compilation path has exactly one. `processSource` MUST set that snapshot, NEVER the caller's map, as the parsed code's external parameters and MUST read the external dependencies from it. `XlsBinder.bind`, `makeOpenL`, the library-import refusal, the binding context, whose copy keeps the explicit key, every `BindHelper` member check and the factory's validation context, which today is built without parameters (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:159-160`) and MUST receive the snapshot as its external parameters, all read that one value and NEVER fall back to the JVM property during the compilation. A change to the caller's map or to the JVM property during a compilation takes effect only at the next `getCompiledOpenClass` or `newEngineInstance` call.
  - Policy-aware cache key: the `openls` cache (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:135`) is keyed by module URI (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:189`), and one `RulesEngineFactory` keeps one `XlsBinder` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:186-196`). The key MUST become `XLSX::<uri>::<policy>`, so an `OpenL` built under one policy is NEVER reused under the other and the duplicate check in `registerOpenL` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:141-146`) applies per key.
  - Atomic `OpenL` construction: `openls` is a `HashMap` that `bind` reads, fills through `makeOpenL` and writes without a lock (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:135-146`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:190-196`), so two first compilations of one key can both build an `OpenL`, and the second registration fails with `already exists`. It MUST become a concurrent map whose miss path looks up, builds and registers one key's `OpenL` as one atomic step: the first caller runs `makeOpenL`, concurrent callers of that key wait for and reuse that instance, and callers of another key NEVER wait for it. Only `makeOpenL` runs inside that step, and it NEVER re-enters `bind` or touches `openls`. Dependency compilation (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLCompileManager.java:95-105`, a separate `RulesEngineFactory` per dependency at `DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:143`) and module binding stay outside it, so a nested compilation NEVER waits on it. Each `TypeResolver` receives its policy as a final constructor argument and NEVER changes it. The `registerOpenL` duplicate check stays, per key.
  - Policy-aware compiled-module cache: `RulesEngineFactory.getCompiledOpenClass` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:179-183`) returns its one cached `CompiledOpenClass` without checking the policy and initializes it without a lock, so once a module is cached a changed policy never reaches `XlsBinder` and the key above is never consulted. `getInterfaceClass` calls `getCompiledOpenClass` twice (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:99`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:101`), and `newEngineInstance` calls it and then `getInterfaceClass` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:209`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:212`), so one engine can combine parts of different compilations. Every `getCompiledOpenClass` call MUST take one policy snapshot, as above, under the factory's lock and return the cached result only when the cached policy equals the snapshot's; otherwise it compiles with that snapshot and publishes the result, the snapshot's policy and the interface class generated from that same result (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:97-113`) as one unit. `getInterfaceClass` and `newEngineInstance` MUST take that one unit and NEVER combine a result of one compilation with an interface or a policy of another. An interface class supplied through a constructor or `setInterfaceClass` is NEVER replaced, `newEngineInstance` NEVER writes the cache itself, and concurrent first calls compile once per policy. Public constructors and methods stay unchanged, and with an unchanged policy every call returns the same cached result, as today.
  - Policy-aware dependency cache: OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services compile every project module as a dependency of a virtual module (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleMultiModuleInstantiationStrategy.java:251-272`, created at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/ui/ProjectModel.java:1348` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/core/RuleServiceOpenLServiceInstantiationFactoryImpl.java:72`). Each dependency compiles in its own `RulesEngineFactory` (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:142-153`) from parameters built from the dependency manager's parameters and the module's properties (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:94-117`), so it takes its own snapshot, and `SimpleDependencyLoader.getCompiledDependency` returns its cached `CompiledDependency` without checking a policy (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:69-85`); after a policy change, a compilation under the secure policy therefore links a dependency compiled under the permissive one. `OpenLCompileManager.processSource` MUST hold its snapshot's policy as the current thread's compilation policy, a thread-scoped value beside the accessor in `OpenLSystemProperties`, from its start until it returns, and then restore the previous value, as the dependency manager already scopes its compilation stack to the thread (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/AbstractDependencyManager.java:51-52`); every dependency load of that compilation, before binding (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLCompileManager.java:95-105`) and during it (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/dependency/DependencyBindingContext.java:55`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/dependency/DependencyBindingContext.java:127`), runs on that thread. `getCompiledDependency` MUST resolve the requested policy once: the thread's compilation policy, or, when no compilation is active on the thread, the value from the dependency manager's parameters first and the JVM property second, which it then holds as the thread's compilation policy until it returns. It MUST return a cached `CompiledDependency` only when the policy recorded with it equals the requested one, in both cache reads (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:71-82`); otherwise it compiles the dependency again under its existing lock, and `compileDependency` MUST put the requested policy into the dependency's parameters after the module properties (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:113-114`), so a module property NEVER changes it, record that policy with the result and, where it caches the result today (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:126-130`), replace the cached entry only after calling `onResetComplete` for the replaced one, as `reset` does (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:164-171`). A compilation NEVER links a dependency compiled under another policy, and every existing public signature stays unchanged.
  - Enforcement in `TypeResolver`: a denied type MUST NEVER be returned through the pre-registered core classes (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:177`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:184-194`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:368`), the aliases built from module imports (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:346-355`, imports loaded at `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:457`), the `found` cache (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:371-377`), package lookups (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:404-419`) or dynamic loading (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:421-425`).
  - Library imports: `makeOpenL` registers every import that ends in `.*` and names a class as a Java library (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:451-459`), whose static methods and fields enter the module namespace and its automatic casts (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:472`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:476`) without passing through `TypeResolver`. Under the secure policy, which is the value `bind` read from the compilation's snapshot, `makeOpenL` MUST NEVER register a class from the denied set as a library; it records a binding error naming the import instead, as it already records import errors (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:465-466`).
  - Member binding: one policy-aware member check in `BindHelper`, beside `checkOnDeprecation` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/BindHelper.java:120-171`), MUST run before every binder returns a bound method or field, and refuse it with a binding error when its declaring type or its return type is denied. It reads the policy from the compilation's snapshot that `bindingContext.getExternalParams()` holds (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/binding/XlsModuleOpenClass.java:133`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/BindingContextDelegator.java:189-190`) through the same accessor, so every member check of one compilation sees the value its resolver was built with. It judges each type by the Java class it stands for: an array by its root component type, and a static, module-specific or other delegating type by the type it delegates to, because `StaticOpenClass.getInstanceClass()` returns `java.lang.Class` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/types/StaticOpenClass.java:127-128`) and judging that would refuse every static call. The check runs on every successful return: namespace method calls (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/MethodNodeBinder.java:73-81`), target method calls (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/MethodNodeBinder.java:255-271`, through `ModuleSpecificOpenMethod.findMethodCaller` at `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/module/ModuleSpecificOpenMethod.java:81-83`), static calls, which bind through the target path, the call-as-field sugar `Name(argument)` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/MethodNodeBinder.java:200-221`), unqualified field and variable names (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/IdentifierBinder.java:28-46`), and field access on a target (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/IdentifierBinder.java:85-147`). The constructor type lookup (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/binding/impl/MethodNodeBinder.java:95`) resolves through `TypeResolver` and is refused there. `MethodSearch.findMethod` stays unchanged.
  - Denied set: `ClassLoader`, `Process`, `ProcessBuilder`, `Runtime`, `System`, `Thread`, `ProcessHandle`, `ThreadGroup`, `java.lang.Class`, and the packages `java.lang.reflect`, `java.lang.invoke`, `javax.script`, `sun.*` and `jdk.*`.
  - Test plan: `DEV/org.openl.rules/test` covers both states on each enforcement path (new `DEV/org.openl.rules/test/org/openl/conf/TypeResolverSecurityTest.java`) and on each member-binding path (new `DEV/org.openl.rules/test/org/openl/binding/impl/SystemClassBindingTest.java`): a namespace call, a target call, a static call, the call-as-field sugar, an unqualified field, a field on a target, a constructor, a library import of a denied class, a member of a permitted library class whose return type is denied, and a member returning an array of a denied type. At the default each binds; at the secure value each fails binding with a clear error; a static call on a permitted class such as `Math.max` binds in both states. Precedence: an external parameter at the secure value with the JVM property at the default, and the reverse, are each decided by the external parameter (new `DEV/org.openl.rules/test/org/openl/engine/SystemClassPolicyPrecedenceTest.java`). Reuse goes only through the public `RulesEngineFactory` API (new `DEV/org.openl.rules/test/org/openl/rules/runtime/SystemClassPolicyReuseTest.java`): one factory compiles the same module at the default, the test sets the external parameter on that factory's source module to `true`, and the next `getCompiledOpenClass` and `newEngineInstance` refuse `java.lang.Runtime`; the reverse order binds it again; with an unchanged policy two calls return the same cached result. Dependency reuse goes only through the public `SimpleDependencyManager` and `RulesEngineFactory` API, without reflection (new `DEV/org.openl.rules.project/test/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SystemClassPolicyDependencyTest.java`): one dependency manager serves a requesting module that depends on a module binding `java.lang.Runtime`; after a compilation at the default binds it, a compilation with `rules.block-system-classes` set to `true` on the requesting module's source compiles the dependency again and refuses it instead of reusing the cached one; the reverse order binds it again; an unchanged policy returns the same cached `CompiledDependency`; a dependency module property set to `false` leaves the requested `true` in force; and a load with no compilation active on the thread follows the dependency manager's parameters first and the JVM property second. Policy change during compilation, also in `SystemClassPolicyReuseTest` and through the same public API without reflection: the factory's source module returns a parameter map that answers the default for `rules.block-system-classes` on its first read and `true` on every later read, standing in for a concurrent change; the whole compilation, resolver and member checks included, follows the first value and binds `java.lang.Runtime`, and the result is cached under the default; the next `getCompiledOpenClass` compiles again under `true` and refuses it, and `newEngineInstance` refuses it from that same result; a change to that map or to the JVM property after a compilation leaves the published result, its interface and its policy unchanged until the next `getCompiledOpenClass` or `newEngineInstance` call. The test NEVER clears a cache by reflection or compiles around the factory. Concurrency (also `DEV/org.openl.rules/test/org/openl/rules/runtime/SystemClassPolicyReuseTest.java`): threads released together by one latch call `getCompiledOpenClass` on one factory and all receive one result without an error; threads released together compile the same module URI through one shared `XlsBinder` (an `OpenL` built as `RulesEngineFactory.getOpenL` builds it, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:186-196`, compiled through `OpenLManager.compileModuleWithErrors`), under one policy and under both policies, and every result binds without an `already exists` error and follows its own policy. Propagation tests in both states go into new `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/core/SystemClassPolicyPropagationTest.java` and new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/ui/SystemClassPolicyPropagationTest.java`.
  - Migration note: the human MUST add one SEC-03 entry to `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`, the file the Default-changing directives create, and create it with the same header when no Default-changing directive did. The entry states what setting `rules.block-system-classes=true` breaks: every rule, datatype or test that resolves a class or package in the denied set, imports one as a library, or calls a method or reads a field whose declaring or return type is denied, such as `System.currentTimeMillis()`, `Thread.currentThread()` or `getClass()`, stops binding with an error, in OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services alike. It lists the denied classes and packages and states how users upgrade: compile and test every project with the property set to `true` outside production, read the startup `WARN` and the binding errors, replace each refused call with a rule-level equivalent or a method of the project's own Java code outside the denied set, remove denied imports, and then set `rules.block-system-classes=true` in both applications for the 6.6.0 target release; until then the default `false` keeps today's behavior.
  - Risk: the change touches core compiler paths (the policy snapshot in `OpenLCompileManager`, `XlsBinder`, `TypeResolver`, `MethodNodeBinder`, `IdentifierBinder`, the member check in `BindHelper`, the compiled-module cache in `RulesEngineFactory` and the compiled-dependency cache in `SimpleDependencyLoader`), so the full `DEV/` test suite MUST pass with the property at its default before the change is merged.
- Human decision [SEC-14](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-14): no directive was written for SEC-14, and a human MUST decide and apply this configuration. Estimated LOC: Production: 0–0; Test: 0–0; Configuration and build: 50–90; Documentation: 0–0.
  - The `owasp` profile (`pom.xml:1989-2006`) declares the property `owasp.failBuildOnCVSS` with the value `7` and configures the plugin's `failBuildOnCVSS` as `${owasp.failBuildOnCVSS}`, NEVER as a literal: a literal `<configuration>` value cannot be overridden from the command line, so gate-3 collection would lose its report-only threshold.
  - The profile sets `suppressionFiles` to a new root-level `dependency-check-suppressions.xml`, which holds only verified false positives and no-fix entries whose evidence meets the "no fix available" definition of the Gate 3 enforcement bullet, each with `notes` and `until`.
  - The Trivy scan in `.github/workflows/trivy.yml:56-59` adds `--exit-code 1 --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --ignorefile .trivyignore` and NEVER uses `--ignore-unfixed`, because that flag also drops findings whose fixed-version metadata is merely absent.
  - The job checks out no repository files (`.github/workflows/trivy.yml:52-59`), so the same `run` block first writes `.trivyignore` from a workflow-level list. Each entry is the finding ID with an `exp:` date and a comment naming the evidence, and the list holds exactly the IDs exempted in `dependency-check-suppressions.xml`.
  - Failure-safe reporting: the `run` block (`.github/workflows/trivy.yml:56-120`) runs in the `ghcr.io/aquasecurity/trivy:0.74.0` container (`.github/workflows/trivy.yml:50`), whose default shell stops at the first non-zero exit, and the `Archive report results` step (`.github/workflows/trivy.yml:122-127`) has no failure condition, so `--exit-code 1` alone loses the step summary and the JSON artifact on a policy failure. The same `run` block MUST capture the `trivy image` exit status instead of stopping on it (`status=0` before the scan, `|| status=$?` after it), MUST still convert the JSON report and append the summary to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` whenever that report exists, and MUST then exit with the captured status. The `Archive report results` step MUST carry `if: always()`, so the JSON report is uploaded when the scan fails. A non-zero scan exit NEVER becomes a passing job.
  - Verification for the human: the suppression file is well-formed XML in the Dependency-Check suppression namespace, the two exemption lists hold the same IDs, a collection run with `-Dowasp.failBuildOnCVSS=11` exits 0 while Open CVSS ≥ 7.0 findings remain, and a Trivy scan that reports an unexempted HIGH or CRITICAL finding fails the job while its step summary and JSON artifact are still published.
  - Enabling these gates fails CI for every CVSS ≥ 7.0 finding still open after remediation, so the human MUST enable them only once the final aggregate gate reports zero such findings.

CRITICAL Directive: Upgrade Spring Boot until the ITEST Tomcat leaves every owned advisory range

- Finding: [DEP-08](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#dep-08)
- CVE/CWE: CVE-2026-65637, CVE-2026-65905, CVE-2026-53434, CVE-2026-55276, CVE-2026-59083, CVE-2026-59084, CVE-2026-65182, CVE-2026-68525, CVE-2026-65183, CVE-2026-66422, CVE-2026-68569, CVE-2026-65927, CVE-2026-68763, CVE-2026-53404, CVE-2026-73180, CVE-2026-55955, CVE-2026-55956, CVE-2026-50229, CVE-2026-66299
- Severity: Critical 9.8
- Bucket: Dependency upgrade
- Files and classes: `pom.xml:91` (`spring.boot.version` 3.5.16), consumed by the Spring Boot starter declarations at `pom.xml:429-450`. The only consumer of `org.apache.tomcat.embed:tomcat-embed-core` 10.1.55 is `ITEST/itest.spring-boot/spring-boot-web-app`, through `spring-boot-starter-web` and `spring-boot-starter-tomcat` 3.5.16.
- Required fix:
  - MUST set `spring.boot.version` to the lowest Spring Boot release on Maven Central whose `spring-boot-starter-tomcat` resolves `tomcat-embed-core` outside all 19 owned ranges. The register names 10.1.56 and 10.1.58 as fixed Tomcat versions; under the target publication preflight these are scanner data, not registry evidence, and CVE-2026-59083 and CVE-2026-59084 have no fixed version in scanner data.
  - MUST find that release by reading `maven-metadata.xml` of `org/springframework/boot/spring-boot-starter-tomcat` on Maven Central and the Tomcat version each candidate POM declares, in ascending version order: the 3.5 releases above 3.5.16 first, then the later 3.x minor lines, then later major lines. MUST confirm the choice with the scoped gate-3 check.
  - Later line: when no 3.5 release qualifies, the lowest qualifying later-line release is the target, NEVER "unavailable". The directive MUST state the line change in the Project Guide with a compatibility assessment from local evidence: the Spring Framework line and Java release the candidate POM requires, against `spring.framework.version` (`pom.xml:90`) and compiler release 21 (`pom.xml:1304`); the Spring LDAP constraints at `pom.xml:93-96` and `pom.xml:101-102`; and the APIs that release notes bundled in local-repository artifacts deprecate or remove, grepped at the Spring Boot call sites in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl` and `ITEST/itest.spring-boot`. Without that evidence the assessment is Low confidence, matching the DEP-08 LOC row. A candidate whose assessment establishes an incompatibility that no change of this unit or its coupled group resolves, for example a Java release above 21, does not qualify.
  - Coupling: a candidate whose POM requires a Spring Framework line that `spring.framework.version` does not resolve cannot pass this gate without the [DEP-09](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#dep-09) upgrade. That POM requirement is the concrete coupling evidence: the directive MUST cite it and MUST run with the DEP-09 directive as one coupled group, in which `spring.framework.version` takes the lowest release of the required line that clears the DEP-09 Open ranges. Each unit's gate still covers only its own occurrences, and a revert undoes the group's edits together.
  - When no release of any line qualifies, the directive fails its gate under FAILURE HANDLING and NEVER records "no fix available", because no owned row carries no-fix evidence.
  - NEVER pin `tomcat-embed-core` in a module POM. Outside such a coupled group, NEVER change `spring.framework.version`, which the BOM import at `pom.xml:422-428` manages independently.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: no new test class; the regression evidence is the scoped gate-3 check. A code change the upgrade forces MUST carry a regression test in the existing test module of the changed class. `ITEST/itest.spring-boot/spring-boot-web-app` stays outside the validation command and is reported as not run.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 0–0; Test: 0–0; Configuration and build: 1–3; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, the scoped gate-3 check over the `tomcat-embed-core` occurrences of the 19 listed CVEs reports zero failures, and the collected Dependency-Check report lists none of the 19 IDs against the upgraded `tomcat-embed-core`.

CRITICAL Directive: Require JWT authentication on every OpenL Rule Services deployment endpoint

- Finding: [SEC-01](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-01)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-306
- Severity: Critical 9.8
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: `JWTValidator.authorize` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:79-111`, with its `/admin/` exemption at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:83-85`, its OpenAPI suffix exemption at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:87-89` and its bearer-token check at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:91-110`; unchanged read references: the deploy routes of `RulesDeployerRestController` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java` (class path `/deploy` under the `/admin` server at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:33`, upload at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:49-52`, redeploy with a multi-segment deploy path at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:65-69`, download at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:86-89`, delete at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:110-112`), `RuleServicesFilter.skipAuthorization` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:204-209`) and `OpenApiResource`, the genuine documentation contract (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/jaxrs/OpenApiResource.java:25`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/jaxrs/OpenApiResource.java:36`); new test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidatorTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - `JWTValidator.authorize` MUST classify a request as a deployment request when its path info, the same `getPathInfo()` value both exemptions read, is exactly `/admin/deploy` or starts with `/admin/deploy/`. It MUST make that test first, before the `/admin/` exemption (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:83-85`) and before the OpenAPI suffix exemption (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:87-89`).
  - A deployment request MUST go straight to the bearer-token check (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:91-110`) and NEVER pass through either exemption, so upload, redeploy, download and delete require a valid token whenever `ruleservice.authentication.enabled=true`, whatever deployment name or deploy path the request carries.
  - Deployment requests now reach the refusal catch of the bearer-token check (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:105-106`), which logs the original jose4j exception, whose message carries raw claims and the compact token. This directive MUST replace that statement with the categorized, secret-free `WARN` line that the SEC-43 directive defines (one line holding the fixed category and the request path info), NEVER logging the token, a claim value, the original exception, its message or its cause. This catch edit is shared with SEC-43 and counted in this directive's LOC row.
  - The fix MUST NOT be built by only removing `/admin/deploy` from the `/admin/` exemption: the redeploy deploy path (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:65-69`) and the delete deployment name (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:110-112`) accept values ending in `openapi.json` or `openapi.yaml`, so such a fix leaves those requests exempt through the suffix test.
  - The OpenAPI exemption MUST stay unchanged for every non-deployment path, so the genuine `openapi.json` and `openapi.yaml` documents that `OpenApiResource` serves under each service address stay public.
  - MUST keep `/admin/healthcheck/readiness` public: `RuleServicesFilter.skipAuthorization` already exempts `/admin/healthcheck/`, never matches a deployment path, and stays unchanged.
  - MUST keep the conditional bean (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:34`) and the `ruleservice.deployer.enabled = false` default (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:22-24`) unchanged, and this Code fix NEVER widens to other `/admin/` paths, because the SEC-11 switch owns every other non-health `/admin/` path (the information, service-list, error, manifest, Swagger UI service-list and configuration endpoints) and keeps `/admin/healthcheck/` public.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: new package-private `JWTValidatorTest` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/`, modelled on `RuleServicesFilterTest`. It builds `JWTValidator` from a `MockEnvironment` whose JWKS is a key set written to a JUnit `@TempDir` and referenced as a `file:` location, signs its tokens in-JVM with the matching test key, captures the lines `JWTValidator` logs through the slf4j-simple test provider (`pom.xml:1193-1196`), and drives `authorize` with mocked `HttpServletRequest` objects that carry the method, the path info and the `Authorization` header; no server starts. `ITEST/itest.security/test/org/openl/test/JWTValidatorTest.java` is outside the validation command and never counts for this gate. `JWTValidatorTest` MUST prove each of these cases:
  - an upload, a redeploy, a download and a delete under `/admin/deploy` with ordinary deployment names are refused without a token and authorized with a valid token;
  - suffix collisions are refused without a token and with an invalid token, and authorized with a valid token: a redeploy whose deploy path ends in `openapi.json` and one whose deploy path ends in `openapi.yaml`, each with a single-segment and with a multi-segment deploy path, and a delete whose deployment name ends in each suffix;
  - the genuine `openapi.json` and `openapi.yaml` documents under a service address outside `/admin/` are authorized without a token;
  - `/admin/healthcheck/readiness` is authorized without a token;
  - deployment requests with a malformed, an expired, a wrong-audience and an unsigned or disallowed-algorithm token are refused, and the captured log holds the category and none of the token, claim values or jose4j message text.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 32–65; Test: 196–367; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `JWTValidatorTest` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test` proves that an unauthenticated call to each deploy endpoint, every suffix-collision case included, is refused while the genuine OpenAPI documents and `/admin/healthcheck/readiness` are served without a token, and that every refused deployment token is logged as one line holding its category and none of the token, claim values or jose4j message text.

CRITICAL Directive: Add remote-access switches for anonymous OpenL Studio single mode and unauthenticated OpenL Rule Services

- Finding: [SEC-02](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-02)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-1188
- Severity: Critical 9.8
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `SingleSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SingleSecurityConfig.java:27-31`); `RuleServicesFilter` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:68-99`); `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:23-31`; `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59`; tests new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SingleSecurityConfigTest.java` and existing `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilterTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`, whose header this directive's LOC row carries.
- Required fix:
  - At `security.single.allow-remote-access=false`, OpenL Studio in `user.mode=single` MUST refuse every request whose remote peer is not a loopback address with 403 and MUST serve loopback requests unchanged.
  - At `ruleservice.authentication.allow-anonymous-remote=false`, OpenL Rule Services with `ruleservice.authentication.enabled=false` MUST refuse every request whose remote peer is not a loopback address with 403, except the `/admin/healthcheck/` paths that `RuleServicesFilter.skipAuthorization` keeps public, and MUST serve loopback requests unchanged.
  - Both checks MUST read the connection's original peer address before forwarded-header processing, so a client-supplied `X-Forwarded-For` value NEVER passes them.
  - At the defaults (`true`) both applications MUST behave exactly as today.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.single.allow-remote-access` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside the `security.single.*` keys (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:35-43`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `ruleservice.authentication.allow-anonymous-remote` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `ruleservice.authentication.enabled` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `WARN` owners: `SingleSecurityConfig` logs one line when `user.mode=single` and remote access is allowed; `RuleServicesFilter` initialization logs one line when `ruleservice.authentication.enabled=false` and anonymous remote access is allowed. Each line names SEC-02, its property and `false`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`: create the file with its header and add the SEC-02 entry. Flipping either value refuses anonymous remote callers; OpenL Studio users move to `multi`, `ad`, `saml` or `oauth2` mode, and OpenL Rule Services clients enable JWT authentication or reach the application through a loopback proxy.
- Tests: `SingleSecurityConfigTest` and `RuleServicesFilterTest` with mocked requests whose remote address is set explicitly. Default: a request from a non-loopback address is served in each application. Secure: a request from a non-loopback address is refused and a loopback request is served in each application, and a non-loopback `/admin/healthcheck/readiness` request stays served in OpenL Rule Services. Partially hardened, per application, because `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws` do not depend on each other: each application's test MUST assert that its own owner logs the SEC-02 startup `WARN` at the default and logs none at the secure value, and MUST NEVER set the other application's property, `SingleSecurityConfigTest` in `user.mode=single` for `SingleSecurityConfig` and `RuleServicesFilterTest` with `ruleservice.authentication.enabled=false` for `RuleServicesFilter`. OpenL Studio hardened with OpenL Rule Services at its default is proven by `SingleSecurityConfigTest` at the secure value logging no `WARN` together with `RuleServicesFilterTest` at the default asserting the remaining `WARN`, and the reverse by `RuleServicesFilterTest` at the secure value logging no `WARN` together with `SingleSecurityConfigTest` at the default asserting the remaining `WARN`.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 80–140; Test: 200–360; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 10–17
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `SingleSecurityConfigTest` and `RuleServicesFilterTest` prove the default, secure and partially hardened states above, the partially hardened ones through that per-application pair, each insecure condition logs its own `WARN`, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-02 entry.

The `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry states that the reference stack stays a development stack, how to adopt the commented profile, and that adopting it removes remote debugging, the wildcard CORS origin, the enabled deployer and the published database port.
- Tests: none; the gate is the commented-lines diff check.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 0–0; Test: 0–0; Configuration and build: 28–48; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: in this directive's own diff of `compose.yaml`, every added line starts with `#` after optional spaces and no existing line changes; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-20 entry; the validation command passes.

CRITICAL Directive: Upgrade Spring Security until core, LDAP and OAuth2 resource-server leave every owned range

- Finding: [DEP-11](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#dep-11)
- CVE/CWE: CVE-2026-59270, CVE-2026-41707, CVE-2026-47841, CVE-2026-47842, CVE-2026-59276
- Severity: Critical 9.1
- Bucket: Dependency upgrade
- Files and classes: `pom.xml:100` (`spring.security.version` 6.5.11), imported through `spring-security-bom` at `pom.xml:462-468`; each CVE row owns three occurrences, `spring-security-core`, `spring-security-ldap` and `spring-security-oauth2-resource-server` 6.5.11, all Runtime.
- Required fix:
  - MUST set `spring.security.version` to the lowest Spring Security release on Maven Central, at or above the identified 6.5.12 (a register floor that is scanner data, not registry evidence, under the target publication preflight), that moves all three artifacts outside all five owned ranges, read from `maven-metadata.xml` of `org/springframework/security/spring-security-bom` in ascending version order: the 6.5 releases at or above 6.5.12 first, then the later 6.x minor lines, then later major lines. MUST confirm the choice with the scoped gate-3 check.
  - MUST keep `spring.ldap.version` 3.3.8 (`pom.xml:103`) while the target stays on the Spring Framework 6 line. For a target whose `spring-security-ldap` POM requires `spring-ldap-core` 4.x, the directive MUST move `spring.ldap.version` to the release that POM declares and MUST state that change, because 3.3.8 stays on Spring Framework 6 and 4.x requires Spring 7 (`pom.xml:101-102`). The Micrometer override (`pom.xml:93-97`) stays unchanged on every line, because it mitigates [DEP-01](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#dep-01).
  - Later line: when no 6.5 release qualifies, the lowest qualifying later-line release is the target, NEVER "unavailable". The directive MUST state the line change in the Project Guide with a compatibility assessment from local evidence: the Spring Framework line and Java release the candidate POM requires, against `spring.framework.version` (`pom.xml:90`) and compiler release 21 (`pom.xml:1304`); the Spring LDAP constraints at `pom.xml:93-96` and `pom.xml:101-102`; and the APIs that release notes bundled in local-repository artifacts deprecate or remove, grepped at the Spring Security call sites in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security` and `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl`. Without that evidence the assessment is Low confidence, matching the DEP-11 LOC row. A candidate whose assessment establishes an incompatibility that no change of this unit or its coupled group resolves, for example a Java release above 21, does not qualify.
  - Coupling: a candidate whose POM requires a Spring Framework line that `spring.framework.version` does not resolve cannot pass this gate without the [DEP-09](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#dep-09) upgrade. That POM requirement is the concrete coupling evidence: the directive MUST cite it and MUST run with the DEP-09 directive as one coupled group, in which `spring.framework.version` takes the lowest release of the required line that clears the DEP-09 Open ranges. Each unit's gate still covers only its own occurrences, and a revert undoes the group's edits together.
  - When no release of any line qualifies, the directive fails its gate under FAILURE HANDLING and NEVER records "no fix available".
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: no new test class; the regression evidence is the scoped gate-3 check over all three artifacts. A code change the upgrade forces MUST carry a regression test in the existing test module of the changed class.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 0–0; Test: 0–0; Configuration and build: 1–3; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, the scoped gate-3 check over the 15 owned occurrences reports zero failures, and the collected Dependency-Check report lists none of the five IDs against any of the three upgraded artifacts.

CRITICAL Directive: Upgrade Testcontainers until its shaded HTTP components leave every owned range

- Severity: Critical 9.1
- Bucket: Dependency upgrade
- Files and classes: `pom.xml:168` (`testcontainers.version` 2.0.5), consumed by the `${testcontainers.version}` declarations at `pom.xml:275`, `pom.xml:293`, `pom.xml:305`, `pom.xml:316`, `pom.xml:328` and `pom.xml:340`; `org.apache.httpcomponents.client5:httpclient5` 5.5.1 and `org.apache.httpcomponents.core5:httpcore5` 5.3.6 are shaded in `com.github.docker-java:docker-java-transport-zerodep` 3.7.1, which six ITEST modules reach in test scope.

The register names `httpclient5` 5.6.4 (CVE-2026-71290) and 5.6.3 (CVE-2026-64607), which under the target publication preflight are scanner data, not registry evidence; the containing release and the `httpcore5` fixed versions for CVE-2026-54399 and CVE-2026-54428 are not identified in scanner data.

  - Later line: the register names the `httpclient5` fixes only in the 5.6 line, above the shaded 5.5.1, and identifies no containing release, so the target can lie in a later Testcontainers minor or major line. When no 2.0 release qualifies, the lowest qualifying later-line release is the target, NEVER "unavailable". The directive MUST state the line change in the Project Guide with a compatibility assessment from local evidence: the Java release the candidate requires, read from its POM or from the class-file version of its JAR, against compiler release 21 (`pom.xml:1304`); the `testcontainers` line that the `testcontainers-keycloak` 4.3.1 POM (`pom.xml:169`) declares and that the `s3mock-testcontainers` 5.2.3 POM (`pom.xml:170`) receives through the Spring Boot 4.1.1 BOM its `s3mock-parent` imports, 2.0.5 in both at the audited commit, against the candidate's line; and the Testcontainers APIs that release notes bundled in local-repository artifacts deprecate or remove, grepped at the Testcontainers call sites in `ITEST/itest.studio/acl`, `ITEST/itest.studio/sso`, `ITEST/itest.kafka.smoke`, `ITEST/itest.tracing`, `ITEST/itest.storelogdata` and `ITEST/itest.s3`. This assessment is the only compatibility evidence for those six modules, because the validation command's `-P '!itest'` never builds them. Without that evidence the assessment is Low confidence, matching the DEP-12 LOC row. A candidate whose assessment establishes an incompatibility that no change of this unit resolves, for example a Java release above 21, does not qualify.
  - When no release of any line qualifies, the directive fails its gate under FAILURE HANDLING and NEVER records "no fix available", because no owned row carries no-fix evidence.
  - NEVER treat the root `httpcomponents.client5.version` and `httpcomponents.core5.version` pins (`pom.xml:107-108`) as a fix: they govern unshaded copies and NEVER replace shaded bytes.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: no new test class; the regression evidence is the scoped gate-3 check. The six ITEST modules stay outside the validation command and are reported as not run.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 0–0; Test: 0–0; Configuration and build: 1–3; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, the scoped gate-3 check over the four owned shaded occurrences reports zero failures, and the collected Dependency-Check report lists none of the four IDs against the components shaded in the resolved `docker-java-transport-zerodep`.
