`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializerTest.java`, existing `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfigTest.java`, existing `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioOpenApiTest.java` (setup extracted, fixture test unchanged), new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioMockMvcTest.java` and new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityChainTestConfiguration.java` (shared security test context, carried here), existing `STUDIO/studio-ui/src/services/apiCall.test.ts`, new `STUDIO/studio-ui/src/routes/RedirectRoute.test.tsx`, new `STUDIO/studio-ui/src/pages/LoginPage.test.tsx`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - Issuance: at `security.csrf.enabled=true`, a `CsrfFilter` with a cookie token repository MUST issue a script-readable `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie with the same `SameSite` and `Secure` attributes as the session cookie, and MUST load the token on every response, so the cookie exists before the first write. One `CookieCsrfTokenRepository` bean, created by `withHttpOnlyFalse()`, and one `SpaCsrfTokenRequestHandler` bean, both declared in `SecurityConfig`, which every mode loads, MUST serve every `CsrfFilter`, the SSO `CsrfAuthenticationStrategy` and every `CsrfLogoutHandler`.
  - Token request handler: in Spring Security 6.5.11 `new CsrfFilter(repository)` installs `XorCsrfTokenRequestAttributeHandler`, which resolves only an XOR-masked token and refuses the raw cookie value the UI sends, and `CsrfConfigurer` offers no single-page-application mode. Every `CsrfFilter` MUST therefore use one new `SpaCsrfTokenRequestHandler` that implements `CsrfTokenRequestHandler`, set through `csrf(c -> c.csrfTokenRepository(...).csrfTokenRequestHandler(...))` on the `HttpSecurity` chains and through `setRequestHandler(...)` on the hand-assembled chains. Its `handle` MUST delegate to `XorCsrfTokenRequestAttributeHandler`, so the `_csrf` request attribute stays masked, and MUST then call `get()` on the supplied token, so the deferred token materializes on every request, GET included, and the repository writes the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie on the response to every request that carries none. Its `resolveCsrfTokenValue` MUST read the `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header, else the `_csrf` parameter, return that value when a constant-time comparison finds it equal to the raw token, and otherwise return the result of `XorCsrfTokenRequestAttributeHandler.resolveCsrfTokenValue`, so a masked token stays accepted and every other value stays refused.
  - Placement: the chains are assembled by hand, so the filter MUST be added explicitly to every chain a browser reaches with an ambient credential, each listed below; every listed chain except the static one loads the session context, so securing another first-match chain NEVER covers it. On every chain the token check MUST run before the login, logout and protected handlers it guards:
    - `single`: `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SingleSecurityConfig.java:27`, through `HttpSecurity.csrf(...)`.
    - `multi` and `ad`: the catch-all `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:42` through `HttpSecurity.csrf(...)`, whose standard position precedes the login and logout filters; the REST chain `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:28` keeps that configurer disabled and adds the `CsrfFilter` with `addFilterAfter(csrfFilter, BasicAuthenticationFilter.class)`, because the configurer's standard position runs before `PatAuthenticationFilter` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:31`) validates a PAT.
    - The static chain `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityConfig.java:49`, in every mode.
    - `oauth2`: the REST chain `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:90-103`, after `bearerTokenAuthenticationFilter` and before `bearerExceptionTranslationFilter`; the catch-all `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:106-117`, after `securityContextPersistenceFilter`; the OIDC logout chain, before its `LogoutFilter` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:54-60`).
    - `saml`: the REST chain `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:107-118`, after `patAuthenticationFilter` and before `webExceptionTranslationFilter`; the catch-all `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:123-134`, after `Saml2LogoutRequestFilter` and before `exceptionTranslationFilter`; the SAML logout chain, after `Saml2LogoutRequestFilter` and before `LogoutFilter("/", samlLogoutHandler)` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:55-66`).
    - The filter MUST NOT be added to any other chain: the SAML assertion consumer is covered under SSO messages, and the OIDC callback and authorization-request chains (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:66-85`) and the SAML metadata and authentication-request chains (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:72-79`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:96-103`) hold only the protocol filter that publishes SP metadata or starts or completes an IdP exchange.
  - Login token replacement: the SAML and OIDC login filters run only the `sessionAuthenticationStrategy` bean (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:65-68`, used at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:224` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:188`), so no `CsrfAuthenticationStrategy` runs on an SSO login. In the secure state that bean MUST run a `CsrfAuthenticationStrategy` on the shared repository, with `setRequestHandler` set to the `SpaCsrfTokenRequestHandler` bean, ahead of the bean's existing strategies, including those the SEC-06 directive composes, because the strategy's default `XorCsrfTokenRequestAttributeHandler` leaves the replacement token deferred and writes no replacement cookie on the login response. In the default state the bean stays unchanged. The SAML assertion consumer still carries no `CsrfFilter`, because the strategy runs after authentication, replaces the token the login request carries and checks none.
  - Logout: in the secure state every local logout MUST match POST only, because the token check passes GET as a safe method; in the default state the current logout matchers stay. In the secure state every logout MUST also expire the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie through a `CsrfLogoutHandler` on the shared repository that runs before any handler that commits the response: `HttpSecurity.csrf(...)` adds it to the logout of every `HttpSecurity` chain, the `multi` and `ad` logout included; the OIDC `logoutFilter` bean (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:140-146`) MUST put it before the collected handlers, constructed in that method and NEVER declared as a `LogoutHandler` bean, because the method collects every `LogoutHandler` bean and `Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler` redirects to the IdP (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler.java:39-46`); the SAML local `LogoutFilter` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:65`) MUST take it before `samlLogoutHandler`, which flushes the response (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/SamlLogoutSuccessHandler.java:38-45`); the validated IdP logout runs it through the `samlLogoutFilter` bean, as SSO messages states. In the default state the logout handlers stay unchanged.
  - Exemptions: only a request whose credential a browser never attaches by itself, that a filter earlier in the same chain validated in this request, and whose validated authentication governs the request at the token check, is exempt: a PAT in the `Authorization: Token` scheme (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:37`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:68-69`) on the REST chains, and an OAuth2 bearer token in the `Authorization: Bearer` scheme on the OAuth2 REST chain (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:90-95`). The validating filter runs first and stops an invalid credential before the token check: `PatAuthenticationFilter` answers 401 for an unparseable or invalid PAT (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:74-89`), and the bearer filter's failure handler answers 401, or 500 for an `AuthenticationServiceException` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:45-56`). Validation alone NEVER earns the exemption: when the security context already holds an authenticated, non-anonymous authentication whose name equals the PAT's user, `PatAuthenticationFilter` installs nothing (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:93-97`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:117-129`), so the session authentication keeps governing the request, with the `ADMIN` access that `@AdminPrivilege` refuses to a PAT and the handlers that `@NotPatAuth` closes to every PAT (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdminPrivilege.java:31-34`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/NotPatAuth.java:31`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AuthorizationExpressions.java:24-26`). The exemption MUST therefore rest on request-scoped evidence that the validated credential governs, NEVER on the header alone:
    - `PatAuthenticationFilter` MUST store the `PatAuthenticationToken` it installs as a request attribute under a constant name declared on the class, only on the branch that installs it (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:93-97`); its authentication decisions stay unchanged in both states.
    - The `bearerTokenAuthenticationFilter` bean (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:39-58`) MUST save the context it creates through `setSecurityContextRepository` with a `DelegatingSecurityContextRepository` of the default `RequestAttributeSecurityContextRepository` and a `RequestAttributeSecurityContextRepository` bound to a dedicated attribute name that no other filter writes. The filter replaces the context with the authenticated bearer token on every success.
    - The REST chains' `CsrfFilter` MUST get a `setRequireCsrfProtectionMatcher` that combines `CsrfFilter.DEFAULT_CSRF_MATCHER` with the negation of a test that holds only when the attribute the chain's validating filter wrote in this request holds the same authentication instance, compared by identity, that the `SecurityContextHolder` holds at the token check; for the bearer attribute that instance is the authentication of the saved `SecurityContext`.
    - A write that carries a valid PAT beside a session of the PAT's own user therefore needs the token, as a session write does. The exemption NEVER rests on the type of the authentication in the security context, because a context loaded from the session is ambient whatever its type, and it NEVER applies on a chain without the validating filter. HTTP Basic (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:27-32`) MUST NOT be exempt, because browsers cache Basic credentials and resend them on cross-site requests.
  - SSO messages: the SAML assertion consumer (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:84-90`) MUST carry no `CsrfFilter`, because SAML signature and `InResponseTo` checks protect it. Only an IdP LogoutRequest validated by `Saml2LogoutRequestFilter` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:57`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:64`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:125`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:131`) is handled without a token, because that filter precedes the `CsrfFilter` in the SAML logout chain and in the catch-all. The `samlLogoutFilter` bean (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:164-176`) sets the filter's matcher to `/logout` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:174`), replacing its `/logout/saml2/slo` default, while the SP advertises `{baseUrl}/logout/saml2/slo` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:55`). The logout chain takes only `/logout` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:62`), so an IdP message at the advertised location reaches the catch-all, whose filter never matches it, and arrives at the token check unvalidated. In the default state the bean keeps its `/logout` matcher and its handler. OIDC has no exception: its logout chain is local logout and its callback is a GET (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:66-72`). In the secure state:
    - The bean MUST match `/logout/saml2/slo` as well as `/logout`. The filter acts only on a request that carries `SAMLRequest`; it refuses a message that fails validation with 401, and an undecodable one with an exception, before any logout handler runs, and it passes every other request on to the token check.
    - The bean's logout handler MUST be a `CsrfLogoutHandler` on the shared repository followed by a `SecurityContextLogoutHandler`, NEVER `SamlLogoutSuccessHandler`, because that handler sends a new SP LogoutRequest and flushes the response (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/SamlLogoutSuccessHandler.java:38-45`) before the filter writes the LogoutResponse the IdP awaits.
    - No filter in the tree validates an IdP LogoutResponse, so a LogoutResponse is NEVER exempt: a POST-binding LogoutResponse at the advertised location, which arrives after the local and IdP sessions ended, is refused with 403.
  - SameSite: `security.session-cookie.same-site` MUST be applied through `SessionCookieConfig` in `SpringInitializer` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:40`); `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml` stays unchanged. The empty default sets no attribute. `saml` mode uses `None` with `Secure`, because the IdP's POST binding is a cross-site POST that `Lax` strips of the session cookie holding the saved authentication request.
  - UI: `STUDIO/studio-ui/src/services/apiCall.ts:200-216` MUST send the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie value as an `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header on every non-GET request, and `STUDIO/studio-ui/src/pages/LoginPage.tsx:24-30` MUST send it on the login POST. Local logout (`STUDIO/studio-ui/src/routes/RedirectRoute.tsx:16-17`, `STUDIO/studio-ui/src/pages/403.tsx:14`) MUST become a form POST to `/logout` carrying the token as the `_csrf` field; both states accept that POST, so the UI change NEVER depends on the switch. Each request MUST read the cookie when it is sent, never a cached value, because every login replaces the token and every logout expires it: a form login through the `CsrfAuthenticationStrategy` that `HttpSecurity.csrf(...)` registers, an SSO login as Login token replacement states, and every logout as Logout states.
  - Validation: `security.session-cookie.same-site` accepts only the empty default (no attribute), `Strict`, `Lax` and `None`, matched case-insensitively and written as `Strict`, `Lax` or `None`. `SpringInitializer` MUST refuse every other value before it configures `SessionCookieConfig`.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.csrf.enabled` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `false`; recommended secure value `true`.
  - `security.session-cookie.same-site` in the same file; default empty (attribute not set); recommended secure value `Lax` in `single`, `multi`, `ad` and `oauth2` modes and `None` with the `Secure` attribute in `saml` mode.
  - `WARN` owners: `SecurityConfig` logs one line while CSRF is disabled; `SpringInitializer` logs one line while SameSite is empty and one line while SameSite is `None` outside `saml` mode. Each line names SEC-04, its property and the secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping `security.csrf.enabled` refuses session and Basic writes without the token and stops GET logout. Script clients that use Basic read the token from the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie any GET returns and send it as `X-XSRF-TOKEN`, or move to a PAT. A client that sends a PAT together with a session cookie of the PAT's own user needs the token, or drops the cookie. In `saml` mode the advertised `/logout/saml2/slo` processes a validated IdP LogoutRequest, and a POST-binding IdP LogoutResponse there is refused with 403 after both sessions ended. `SameSite=None` requires HTTPS.
- Tests:
  - Shared base: no security test extends `AbstractStudioOpenApiTest`, because a subclass inherits `generatesOpenApiDocument`, which loads `functionality/<last package segment>.json` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioOpenApiTest.java:37`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioOpenApiTest.java:43-53`), its `MockMvc` is private (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioOpenApiTest.java:40-41`), and `MockConfiguration` builds that `MockMvc` without any security filter (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/MockConfiguration.java:44-47`). Its server-free setup MUST move into the new parent `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest` beside it: `@ExtendWith(SpringExtension.class)`, `@WebAppConfiguration` and the `MockMvc` the context provides, as a protected field. `AbstractStudioOpenApiTest` extends that parent and keeps `generatesOpenApiDocument`, its fixture loading and its comparison unchanged, so `TestRunner000` keeps passing against `functionality/app000.json`.
  - Security context, shared with SEC-05, SEC-11 and SEC-29: `SecurityChainTestConfiguration` holds one nested configuration per `user.mode`, each registering `SecurityConfig` and that mode's production chains; the security tests extend `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest` with it and set `user.mode` and the switch values through `@TestPropertySource`. Its `MockMvc` MUST be built by `MockMvcBuilders.webAppContextSetup(context).addFilters(filterChainProxy)` from the `filterChainProxy` bean that `SecurityFilter` invokes in production (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityFilter.java:39-41`), so every request passes the production chains. REST requests set `servletPath("/rest")`, the dispatcher mapping (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:155`). A test controller records the authenticated name of every write it serves, at one non-REST path and at three REST handlers: one a PAT is permitted to call, one annotated `@AdminPrivilege` and one annotated `@NotPatAuth`. Each mode context registers `MethodSecurityConfig` and `AuthorizationExpressions`, so method security decides as in production.
  - Mode contexts, server-free: `single` imports `SingleSecurityConfig`; `multi` imports `CommonAuthenticationConfig`, `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig`, `MultiSecurityConfig` and `PasswordEncoderConfig` over Mockito `openlUserDao`, `adminUsersInitializer` and `privilegeMapper` beans; `ad` imports the same chain classes with a Mockito `AuthenticationProvider` in place of the LDAP provider (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdSecurityConfig.java:33-34`); `saml` imports `CommonAuthenticationConfig` and `SamlSecurityConfig`, with `security.saml.saml-server-metadata-url` set to a `file:` IdP metadata fixture that declares a POST-binding `SingleLogoutService` location, and the SP and IdP key pairs and certificates generated in-JVM (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:43-53`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:71`). `oauth2` MUST NOT import `OAuth2SecurityConfig`, because its import of `OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration` reaches `OAuth2ImportSelector`, which fetches the issuer metadata over HTTP while the context is parsed (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:20-22`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:31-35`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:48-53`); its test `@Bean` methods call the public `OAuth2SecurityConfig` chain methods with the production filters instead, the bearer filter comes from `OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.bearerTokenAuthenticationFilter` over a `JwtAuthenticationProvider` whose Mockito `JwtDecoder` decodes one test bearer value and throws `BadJwtException` for every other value, and the logout handler reads a fixed `InMemoryClientRegistrationRepository`. Every mode except `single` gets a `SessionRegistryImpl` and runs the real `PatAuthenticationFilter` over a Mockito `PatAuthService`. No collaborator reads a database, a directory, an IdP or the network.
  - Credentials and tokens: an existing session is a `MockHttpSession` holding an authenticated `SecurityContext` under `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository.SPRING_SECURITY_CONTEXT_KEY`, the attribute the production context filters read. Every secure-state request that carries a token first performs a GET through the same chain, reads the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie from that response, and resends that exact value as the cookie and as the `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header or the `_csrf` field. `SecurityMockMvcRequestPostProcessors.csrf()`, `user(...)` and `TestSecurityContextHolder` MUST NOT be used, because they bypass the production token repository, request handler or context filters.
  - `CsrfSecurityFilterChainTest`, on that context. Default: a state-changing request without a token is accepted, a write with a valid PAT beside a session of the PAT's own user and no token is accepted, and a GET to `/logout` logs out. Secure: the first GET response carries a script-readable `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie with the configured `SameSite` value, and `Secure` in `saml` mode; writes without the token are refused with 403, and succeed with the resent cookie value, for the existing session on the REST and catch-all chains of `multi`, `ad`, `oauth2` and `saml`, the OAuth2 REST chain and the SAML catch-all included, for any request on the `single` chain, and for Basic on the `multi` and `ad` REST chain; the masked value of the `_csrf` request attribute is accepted and a changed value refused; a PAT write with no session and no token succeeds, and so does such a bearer write on the OAuth2 REST chain; on the REST chains of `multi`, `ad`, `oauth2` and `saml`, a write with a valid PAT beside a session of an ADMIN user with the PAT's own name and no token is refused with 403 and records no write on all three REST handlers, and with the resent value each handler serves it under the session authentication; beside a session of another user the same PAT write without the token is served under the PAT authentication: the PAT-permitted handler records the PAT user, and the `@AdminPrivilege` and `@NotPatAuth` handlers refuse it with 403; a write with no PAT and no token whose stored session context holds a `PatAuthenticationToken`, or on the OAuth2 REST chain a bearer authentication, is refused with 403; a write with an existing session, no token and an invalid PAT, or on the OAuth2 REST chain an invalid bearer token, is refused with 401 and the test controller records no write; in `multi`, `oauth2` and `saml` modes a local logout POST without the token is refused, with the resent value in `_csrf` it logs out and its response expires the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie, a following write that resends the old value as `X-XSRF-TOKEN` without that cookie is refused with 403, the next GET issues a different value, and a GET does not log out (cookie double-submit is stateless, so the test models the browser dropping the expired cookie); in `saml` and `oauth2` the `sessionAuthenticationStrategy` bean applied to a request carrying the cookie writes a different value, after which a write that sends the old value as `X-XSRF-TOKEN` beside the new cookie is refused with 403 and the new value succeeds; at `/logout/saml2/slo` and at `/logout`, a validated IdP LogoutRequest built from a signed test fixture is processed without a token: the session is invalidated, and the response expires the `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie and carries the LogoutResponse addressed to the fixture IdP's logout location and no new LogoutRequest; the same message unsigned or signed by another key is answered with 401, and a malformed one is refused with 401 or, when undecodable, an exception, in each case before any logout handler runs, with no LogoutResponse sent and the session still authenticated; at `/logout/saml2/slo` a POST without `SAMLRequest` and without the token, and a POST-binding LogoutResponse without the token, are refused with 403; the assertion endpoint is not CSRF-checked. Partially hardened: CSRF enabled with SameSite empty asserts the remaining `WARN`.
  - `SpringInitializerTest`: the session cookie carries no SameSite at the default and the mode's value at the secure setting; the empty value and `None` outside `saml` mode each log their `WARN`. The value `lax` is applied as `Lax`, and `Lux` and `Lax,Strict` are refused at startup with the property-naming error.
  - `CommonAuthenticationConfigTest`: at `security.csrf.enabled=true` the `sessionAuthenticationStrategy` bean applied to a mocked request that carries an `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie expires that cookie and writes a different value; at the default it writes no `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie.
  - Vitest beside `apiCall.test.ts`: the header carries the exact `XSRF-TOKEN` value set in `document.cookie` on non-GET requests and is absent on GET, and a value changed between two requests is sent as changed; `RedirectRoute.test.tsx` and `LoginPage.test.tsx` prove that logout submits a POST with `_csrf` and that the login POST carries the header.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 248–416; Test: 1206–2036; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 12–20

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switch that stops external group names from granting ADMIN

- Finding: [SEC-10](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-10)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-269
- Severity: High 7.5
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `GetUserPrivileges.mapAuthorities` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivileges.java:49-56`), `Privileges` (`STUDIO/org.openl.security/src/org/openl/rules/security/Privileges.java:11-18`), `AdminPrivilege` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdminPrivilege.java:32`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; new test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivilegesTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - At `security.external-groups.allow-admin-mapping=false`, `GetUserPrivileges` MUST NOT grant `ADMIN` from an OpenL group matched by an external authority name (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivileges.java:52`) or from a raw external `ADMIN` authority kept as is (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivileges.java:54`).
  - Explicit local grants stored for the user and the non-administrative privileges of matched groups MUST stay unchanged.
  - At the default (`true`) the mapping MUST behave exactly as today.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.external-groups.allow-admin-mapping` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `WARN` owner: `GetUserPrivileges` logs one line while the value is `true` in `ad`, `saml` or `oauth2` mode, naming SEC-10, the property and `false`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value removes `ADMIN` from users who receive it only through an IdP group named like an OpenL group; administrators are then granted `ADMIN` explicitly in OpenL Studio.
- Tests: `GetUserPrivilegesTest`. Default: an external `Administrators` authority grants `ADMIN`. Secure: the same authority does not grant `ADMIN`, a raw external `ADMIN` authority does not grant it, and explicit local grants and other group permissions remain. The `WARN` is asserted at the default in `ad` mode and absent at the secure value.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 30–55; Test: 90–150; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `GetUserPrivilegesTest` proves both states and the `WARN`, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-10 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add switched multipart parser limits to the OpenL Studio dispatcher servlet

- Finding: [SEC-26](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-26)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-770
- Severity: High 7.5
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `SpringInitializer.contextInitialized` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:93-140`), `SpringInitializer.registerDispatcherServlet` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:153-158`), `PropertySourcesLoader.initialize` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:49-55`, read only), `ApiExceptionControllerAdvice.handleMultipartException` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/common/ApiExceptionControllerAdvice.java:130-137`, read only), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializerTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/common/ApiExceptionControllerAdviceTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - Startup order: `contextInitialized` MUST load the effective environment with `PropertySourcesLoader.initialize` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:119`) before it reads either limit and before it registers the dispatcher, which today happens first, at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:106`. It MUST still register the dispatcher before `applicationContext.refresh()` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:132`), so the refresh listener that registration adds (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:150-151`) receives the first `ContextRefreshedEvent`.
  - Test seam: the steps from `new XmlWebApplicationContext()` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:105`) through that load, followed by the limit read, its `WARN` lines and the registration, MUST sit in one package-private `SpringInitializer` method that `contextInitialized` calls, so a test runs the real startup order without refreshing the context.
  - That method MUST read both limits once from `applicationContext.getEnvironment()` and pass them to `registerDispatcherServlet`, which MUST build its `MultipartConfigElement` from those arguments instead of the literal `-1L` values (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:157`), keeping the file-size threshold unchanged. `registerDispatcherServlet` NEVER reads a property itself.
  - `SpringInitializer` NEVER reads a property before that load: the SEC-04 SameSite value it applies through `SessionCookieConfig` also comes from the loaded environment.
  - The container fixes the servlet registration at startup, so a changed limit takes effect at the next start; `refreshContext` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:215-228`) NEVER re-registers the dispatcher.
  - The servlet container enforces these limits, so the fix NEVER adds an application-level size check.
  - Validation: both values are `long` byte counts. A negative value keeps the limit unlimited, as the `-1L` of `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:157` does today, and zero or a positive value is a limit the container enforces. A blank, non-integer or out-of-`long`-range value MUST be refused before `registerDispatcherServlet` builds the element.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `upload.max-file-size` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `-1` (bytes; negative means unlimited); recommended secure value `104857600`.
  - `upload.max-request-size` in the same file; default `-1`; recommended secure value `209715200`.
  - `WARN` owner: `SpringInitializer` logs, from the values read after the environment load, one line per negative limit, each naming SEC-26, its property and its secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the limits refuses uploads above 100 MiB per file or 200 MiB per request; operators who import larger projects raise the values and align their ingress limits. A changed limit takes effect at the next restart of OpenL Studio.
- Tests: server-free, in `SpringInitializerTest` and `ApiExceptionControllerAdviceTest`.
  - Capture: `SpringInitializerTest` captures the registered `MultipartConfigElement` with a mocked `ServletContext`, as `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializerTest.java:22-40` does, by calling the package-private startup method, NEVER only `registerDispatcherServlet` by reflection.
  - Property sources: the mock returns the context path `/webstudio` and answers `getInitParameterNames` and `getInitParameter` with init parameters, which `PropertySourcesLoader` reads through `ServletContextPropertySource` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:64-65`). The init parameter `openl.home` names a JUnit temporary directory, so `DynamicPropertySource` reads its settings file from that directory and never from a developer's or another test's directory.
  - Default (no limit configured): the element carries `-1` and `-1`, and both `WARN` lines are logged.
  - Secure (both limits as init parameters): it carries `104857600` and `209715200`, and no SEC-26 `WARN` is logged.
  - Partially hardened (only `upload.max-file-size=104857600`, in the `webstudio.properties` settings file that `DynamicPropertySource` reads from that directory, `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:86-89`): it carries `104857600` and `-1`, and only the request-limit `WARN` is logged.
  - Refused values: a blank value, `10MB` and `9223372036854775808` for each property fail registration with the property-naming error.
  - A configured value reaches the element only when the environment loads before registration, so these state tests fail under today's order.
  - The existing direct test passes explicit limits to the new `registerDispatcherServlet` signature and keeps its threshold assertion.
  - `ApiExceptionControllerAdviceTest` proves that a container part-limit failure maps to the upload-size error.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 35–60; Test: 100–180; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and the tests prove the registered values in each state, the refused values, the `WARN` lines and the error mapping, with every state test running the package-private startup method against limits configured in property sources that `PropertySourcesLoader` reads. Container-level refusal of an oversized upload is listed in the Project Guide as not verified in-JVM.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switched row cap to the XLSX run-result export

- Finding: [SEC-27](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-27)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-770
- Severity: High 7.5
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `ProjectsRunController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsRunController.java:63`, export at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsRunController.java:183-188`), `ResultExport` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:24-129`), `RulesResultExport.writeInfo` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/RulesResultExport.java:11-13`, 0 rows), `TestResultExport.writeInfo` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExport.java:17-34`, 2 rows and 1 skipped), `BaseExport` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseExport.java:16-18`, read only), `BaseParameterExport` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:30-57` `write`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:79-97` `getAllNonEmptyFields`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:116-150` `performWrite`), `ParameterExport` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:24-35` `doWrite`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:37-78` header row, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:124-165` value rows, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:219-326` `addValueTasks`, `addArrayValueTasks`, `getRowHeight` and `getMaxHeight`), `FieldDescriptor.getMaxArraySize` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FieldDescriptor.java:141-187`, read only), `FlattenParameterExport` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FlattenParameterExport.java:27-168`), new `ExportRowLimitExceededException` and `ExportRowCount` in package `org.openl.rules.testmethod.export`, `ProjectsController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsController.java:966`, read only), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/ValidationMessages.properties` (new key beside `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/ValidationMessages.properties:148`); tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExportTest.java`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FlattenParameterExportTest.java` (the exporter's existing tests, in the OpenL Studio module and the exporter's package), and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsRunControllerTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - Row accounting: an emitted row is a row the exporter creates through `Sheet.createRow` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:92`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:139`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExport.java:21`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExport.java:25`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:42`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:119`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:128`), counted once per sheet (`Result n`, `Parameters n`), because physical rows are what the workbook holds and a merged region is one entry on a row already counted; the cap applies to the workbook total, the sum over every sheet. A merged region creates no row (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:138-143`), so a merged-only tail, the part of a merged region below the last row its write created, adds nothing and NEVER moves the count. No row index is created twice: rows within one write ascend (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:126-130`), and each write starts after the last row the previous write created (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:54`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:82-84`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:126`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:32`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:160`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:53-55`). The `FIRST_ROW` offset and `SPACE_BETWEEN_RESULTS` gaps (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseExport.java:16-18`) and the rows skipped after info rows (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExport.java:32`) and after each `Parameters of` row (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:46`) are never created and never count. Each group's count is therefore independent of the row it starts at, so the total does not depend on `testsPerPage`.
  - Result groups: each MUST count its info rows through a new abstract per-subclass count beside `writeInfo` (0 for `RulesResultExport`, 2 for `TestResultExport`), 1 header row and 1 row per test unit `writeResults` iterates (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:123-129`).
  - Parameter groups: a group with at least one test (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:39-41` skips the others) MUST count its `Parameters of` row plus the rows its writer lays out.
  - For a `FlattenParameterExport` group, the rows its writer lays out MUST be counted as 1 ID row and every row `writeRowValues` writes, one per parameter, field path and array index, minus the rows the skip-empty rule (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FlattenParameterExport.java:153-155`) drops when `skipEmptyParameters` is true.
  - `ParameterExport` lays out 1 header row and, per test description, every row index its value tasks create. For nested arrays whose elements each write a cell, that is `getMaxHeight` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:275-326`, through `FieldDescriptor.getMaxArraySize`); a `Collection` field of an object takes more, because `addValueTasks` and `addArrayValueTasks` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:219-273`) write it one element per row while `FieldDescriptor.calcArraySizeForChild` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FieldDescriptor.java:159-187`) does not expand it. An array whose elements write no cell, as when every field of the element type is skipped as empty (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FieldDescriptor.java:32-33`), takes fewer: `getRowHeight` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:284-290`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:293-300`) still gives each element one row, but `addValueTasks` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:230-246`) adds no task for an empty field list, so only the first row of the ID's `getMaxHeight` merged region (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:137-139`) is created and the rest is a merged-only tail. `getMaxHeight` therefore NEVER serves as the count, in either direction.
  - Shared preflight: a package-private `ResultExport.countRows(testsPerPage, skipEmptyParameters, flattenParameters, maxRows, results)` returning a new `ExportRowCount` (the total and each sheet's count) MUST run before `new SXSSFWorkbook()` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:31`), page groups as `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:39-56` does, and count each `Parameters n` sheet through a package-private `BaseParameterExport.countRows(tests, skipEmptyParameters, maxRows)` that receives the cap less the rows already counted.
  - One layout: `ParameterExport` and `FlattenParameterExport` MUST each lay out rows through one traversal that takes a row sink. The export sink builds the `WriteTask`s and calls `performWrite` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:116-150`); the counting sink only counts each row index at which the export sink's `performWrite` would call `createRow` (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:119`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:128`), needs no `Styles` and touches no workbook. Preflight and export therefore NEVER diverge.
  - Field reuse: the preflight MUST compute each group's `getAllNonEmptyFields` result (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:79-97`) once, and the capped export's `Parameters n` write MUST reuse it, because the comment at `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/BaseParameterExport.java:48-49` calls that computation very expensive.
  - Bounded and saturating: counts accumulate in `long`. The preflight MUST stop at the first group, test unit, test description or flatten row that takes the total above `maxRows` and report `maxRows + 1`, so its work is bounded by the cap. `export` and `countRows` MUST refuse a `maxRows` above `Integer.MAX_VALUE` with `IllegalArgumentException`, so `maxRows + 1` never overflows.
  - Refusal: a new overload `ResultExport.export(OutputStream, int testsPerPage, Boolean skipEmptyParameters, boolean flattenParameters, long maxRows, TestUnitsResults...)` MUST throw `ExportRowLimitExceededException`, carrying the cap and the saturated count, when the total exceeds a non-negative `maxRows`, before any workbook exists and with no byte written to the stream; a total equal to the cap MUST export. The existing overloads (`DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:20-28`) MUST delegate with `-1`, so `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsController.java:966` (the tests export, `TestResultExport`) and every other caller keep today's output.
  - Controller: at a non-negative `test.run.export.max-rows`, read as an `int`, `ProjectsRunController` MUST pass the cap as `maxRows`, keep `Integer.MAX_VALUE` as `testsPerPage` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsRunController.java:184`) and NEVER substitute the cap into `testsPerPage`, which only paginates sheets. It MUST convert `ExportRowLimitExceededException` into a `BadRequestException` with the new code `run.export.max-rows.exceeded.message` and the cap as its argument; the `openl.error.400.run.export.max-rows.exceeded.message` text in `ValidationMessages.properties` names `test.run.export.max-rows` and the cap.
  - Default: at a negative value (default `-1`) the export MUST run no preflight and MUST behave exactly as today's `Integer.MAX_VALUE` call, with the same sheets, cells and merged regions.
  - Validation: `test.run.export.max-rows` is an `int`. A negative value keeps the export unlimited, and zero or a positive value is the cap. A blank, non-integer or out-of-`int`-range value MUST be refused when the `ProjectsRunController` bean is constructed (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsRunController.java:63`), before any export.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `test.run.export.max-rows` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `test.run.thread.count` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:66`); default `-1` (negative means unlimited); recommended secure value `100000`.
  - `WARN` owner: `ProjectsRunController` bean construction logs one line while the value is negative, naming SEC-27, the property and `100000`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value refuses exports above 100000 rows, counted across every result and parameter sheet with headers and expanded nested values included; users export filtered runs or raise the cap.
- Tests:
  - `ProjectsRunControllerTest`. Default: a result above 100000 rows is exported in full. Secure: the same export is refused with the property-naming error, and a result at the cap exports. The `WARN` is asserted at the default and absent at the secure value. In both states the controller passes `Integer.MAX_VALUE` as `testsPerPage`, so a capped export still holds one `Result 1` and one `Parameters 1` sheet. A blank, non-integer or `2147483648` value fails bean construction with the property-naming error.
  - Exporter tests in the OpenL Studio module's `org.openl.rules.testmethod.export` test package: for every fixture below, the preflight per-sheet counts (`TestResultExportTest`) and the `BaseParameterExport.countRows` sheet count (`ParameterExportTest`, `FlattenParameterExportTest`) equal the physical rows read back from each sheet of the written workbook (`getPhysicalNumberOfRows()`), and the preflight total equals their sum.
  - Multiple groups: the three-group `example3` fixture (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExportTest.java:52`) at `testsPerPage` 1 and `Integer.MAX_VALUE`, with the same total.
  - Nested arrays and collections: `ParameterExportTest` fixtures `listOfObjects` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java:86`), `complexObjectWithListAndMap` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java:102`), `arrayOfObjects` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java:119`), `twoParametersWithArray` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java:224`) and `paramsWithPK` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java:298`), plus a new fixture whose object parameter holds two `A` values in a `List` field, which the writer emits one row per element; `FlattenParameterExportTest` fixtures `arrayOfObjects` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FlattenParameterExportTest.java:80`), `nestedArrayWithComplex_Case1` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FlattenParameterExportTest.java:214`) and `twoParametersWithArray` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/FlattenParameterExportTest.java:264`).
  - Merged-only tails: in `ParameterExportTest`, two groups, each with one test description whose `A[]` parameter holds ten `new A(null)` values, with `skipEmptyParameters` true, so both `A` fields are skipped as empty and no element writes a cell; written together on one sheet, as `twoTestsInSheet` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExportTest.java:259`) does, and each on its own sheet, as `testsPerPage` 1 gives each group its own `Parameters n` sheet. Each group counts 3 rows (its `Parameters of` row, header row and ID row); the ten-row merged ID regions, the first of which reaches into the second group's rows on the shared sheet, add nothing; both layouts total 6 (one sheet of 6, two sheets of 3); and `BaseParameterExport.countRows` over both groups on one sheet reports 6 at a cap of 6 and the saturated 6 (`cap + 1`) at a cap of 5.
  - Flatten states: `flattenParameters` false and true, each with `skipEmptyParameters` true and false, the `halfFilled` and `emptyParameters` fixtures of both parameter test classes included.
  - Exact boundary: a cap equal to the total exports the same sheets, cells and merged regions as the uncapped export; a cap of the total minus 1 throws `ExportRowLimitExceededException` with zero bytes written; an empty run at cap 0 exports, as `savesAnEmptyWorkbookWhenNoTestRan` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/TestResultExportTest.java:149-159`) does.
  - Saturation and default: a fixture above a cap of 10 reports exactly 11; the `-1` export of `example3` equals the existing overload's output; every existing assertion of the three exporter test classes passes unchanged.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 190–330; Test: 540–820; Configuration and build: 3–6; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes; `ProjectsRunControllerTest` proves both states, the refused values, the `WARN` and the unchanged `testsPerPage`; `TestResultExportTest`, `ParameterExportTest` and `FlattenParameterExportTest` prove preflight-equals-emitted for every listed fixture, the merged-only-tail fixture in both layouts included, the exact boundary and the saturated count; and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-27 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switch that requires HTTPS for the OIDC issuer and every discovered endpoint

- Finding: [SEC-33](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-33)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-319
- Severity: High 7.5
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `OAuth2ImportSelector.selectImports` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:20-25`), `OAuth2Configuration` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:31-35`, metadata request at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:48-54`), `LazyClientRegistrationRepository.init` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/LazyClientRegistrationRepository.java:30-45`), `OAuth2JwtAccessTokenConfiguration.jwtDecoder` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2JwtAccessTokenConfiguration.java:38-46`), the `registrationRepository` bean of `OAuth2SecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:169-172`), new `OAuth2EndpointPolicy` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2EndpointPolicy.java`), `OAuth2AuthenticationSettings.store` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/OAuth2AuthenticationSettings.java:82-91`, first `setProperty` through `super.store` at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/OAuth2AuthenticationSettings.java:84`, issuer at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/OAuth2AuthenticationSettings.java:87`); read only, the settings saves that commit only after `store` returns (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/rest/controller/CRUDSettingsController.java:51-54`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/rest/controller/CRUDSettingsController.java:58-64`), and the callers that send the client secret, an authorization code or a token to the registration's endpoints: the token client and user service of `OAuth2SecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:163-166`), `UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector` (introspection at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:52-55`, userinfo at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:72-73`), `OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration.opaqueTokenIntrospector` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration.java:28-35`) and `Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler` (end-session redirect carrying the ID token, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler.java:26-29`); `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; new tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2EndpointPolicyTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2DiscoveryPolicyTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - Parsing: `OAuth2ImportSelector.selectImports` MUST read `security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints` once, before it constructs `OAuth2Configuration` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:20-21`), into a new `OAuth2EndpointPolicy`, and MUST register that policy as a singleton beside `oauth2Config` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:22`). `OAuth2Configuration`, `LazyClientRegistrationRepository` and `jwtDecoder` MUST use that instance and NEVER read the property again.
  - Validation: the value is trimmed and read case-insensitively as `true` or `false`, and an unset property is `true`. Any other value, blank included, MUST fail OpenL Studio startup in `oauth2` mode with an error naming the property, the rejected value and the accepted values.
  - Issuer: at `false`, `security.oauth2.issuer-uri` MUST be an absolute URI with a host whose scheme, compared case-insensitively, is `https`, checked before every discovery request: in `selectImports` before `new OAuth2Configuration`, in the `OAuth2Configuration` constructor before `getConfiguration` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:33-34`), in `LazyClientRegistrationRepository.init` before `ClientRegistrations.fromOidcIssuerLocation` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/LazyClientRegistrationRepository.java:32-33`) and in `jwtDecoder` before the decoder is built (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2JwtAccessTokenConfiguration.java:40`). Any other value, blank, relative and unparsable included, MUST fail with an explicit error naming `security.oauth2.issuer-uri` and `security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints`.
  - One rule: `OAuth2EndpointPolicy` MUST hold the switch parsing of the Validation rule and the issuer check of the Issuer rule in `public static` methods, because `OAuth2AuthenticationSettings` sits in `org.openl.rules.webstudio.web.admin.security`, outside the policy's package. `OAuth2ImportSelector` MUST use the same methods, so startup and the settings save apply one rule.
  - Settings save: `OAuth2AuthenticationSettings.store` MUST read the switch through `properties.getProperty("security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints")` and parse it with the policy's switch-parsing method before `super.store`, its first `setProperty`, so a refused switch value fails the save with the property-naming error. At `false`, it MUST apply the issuer check to the submitted `issuerUri` before `super.store` and fail the save with the same error naming `security.oauth2.issuer-uri` and `security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints`, so the stored settings stay unchanged and a save NEVER commits an issuer that the next `oauth2`-mode start refuses. The save MUST NEVER fetch issuer metadata: today's save makes no discovery request, and the discovered endpoints stay checked at startup only. The error NEVER contains the client secret or the user-info part of the submitted URI.
  - Registration endpoints: at `false`, `LazyClientRegistrationRepository.init` MUST check the built registration before it stores it (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/LazyClientRegistrationRepository.java:41`): its authorization URI (`authorization_endpoint`), token URI (`token_endpoint`), JWK Set URI (`jwks_uri`), userinfo URI (`userinfo_endpoint`) and, when the provider metadata holds it, `end_session_endpoint`. Each present value that is not an absolute `https:` URI with a host, malformed values included, MUST fail the registration with an explicit error naming `security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints` and the metadata field, so no login redirect and no token, userinfo, introspection, JWK Set or end-session request ever uses that registration.
  - Introspection endpoint: at `false`, `selectImports` MUST apply the same check to `introspection_endpoint` before it selects `OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:24-25`), so `UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector` NEVER sends the client ID, the client secret or a token to a plain-HTTP endpoint.
  - Refusal propagation: `LazyClientRegistrationRepository.init` MUST propagate a policy refusal instead of logging it and leaving the registration unset (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/LazyClientRegistrationRepository.java:42-44`), so the `jwtDecoder` and `opaqueTokenIntrospector` beans, which look up the registration at startup, fail with the explicit error. Its existing catch for every other discovery failure, which keeps the lazy retry while the identity provider is unreachable, stays unchanged. A refusal message and a `WARN` line NEVER contain the client secret, an authorization code, a token or the user-info part of a URI.
  - JWT decoder: at `false`, `jwtDecoder` MUST look up the checked registration before it builds the decoder, and MUST build the decoder through `NimbusJwtDecoder.withIssuerLocation(issuerUri)`, which is what `JwtDecoders.fromIssuerLocation` builds in Spring Security 6.5.11, with `restOperations` set to the policy's `RestTemplate`. That template's request interceptor MUST refuse, before sending, every request whose URI is not an absolute `https:` URI, so the decoder's own metadata and JWK Set requests NEVER reach a plain-HTTP URI; the validator set at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2JwtAccessTokenConfiguration.java:42-45` stays unchanged.
  - Default: at `true`, discovery, registration, the JWT decoder, the OAuth2 settings save and every request MUST behave exactly as today, and only the `WARN` lines are added.
  - Test seams: `OAuth2Configuration` MUST gain a package-private constructor that takes the policy and the `RestOperations` that fetches the metadata, `OAuth2ImportSelector` a package-private constructor that takes the `RestOperations` it hands to `OAuth2Configuration`, and `LazyClientRegistrationRepository` a package-private constructor that takes the policy and the discovery function, which defaults to `ClientRegistrations::fromOidcIssuerLocation`, so tests substitute recording mocks for all three. The registration check, the endpoint check and the policy's `RestTemplate` MUST be reachable from the test package.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `security.oauth2.issuer-uri` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:246`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `WARN` owners: `OAuth2ImportSelector` logs, at configuration import, one line while the value is `true` and the issuer is not an absolute `https:` URI, and one line while the value is `true` and `introspection_endpoint` is not; it owns these lines because it reads the issuer and the switch first and triggers the first discovery request before any bean exists. `LazyClientRegistrationRepository` logs, when it builds the registration, one line for each present `authorization_endpoint`, `token_endpoint`, `jwks_uri`, `userinfo_endpoint` and `end_session_endpoint` that is not an absolute `https:` URI while the value is `true`; it owns these lines because the endpoints exist only once discovery has built the registration. Each line names SEC-33, the property, the issuer property or the metadata field, and `false`, and no other class repeats it.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value refuses OpenL Studio startup in `oauth2` mode when `security.oauth2.issuer-uri`, or any authorization, token, JWK Set, userinfo, introspection or end-session endpoint the identity provider advertises, is not an absolute `https:` URI, and refuses the OpenL Studio OAuth2 settings save of an issuer that is not an absolute `https:` URI; operators serve the identity provider and every advertised endpoint over HTTPS before they set `false`.
- Tests: `OAuth2EndpointPolicyTest` and `OAuth2DiscoveryPolicyTest`, both new package-private JUnit 5 classes in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/`, built on `ClientRegistration` builders, provider metadata maps, a recording `RestOperations`, a recording discovery function, `MockRestServiceServer` and an in-memory `PropertiesHolder`; no test contacts a host. Default: an `http:` issuer passes the policy and logs its `WARN`, the recording fetcher receives today's metadata request, and an `http:` registration is returned unchanged with one `WARN` per insecure endpoint. Secure: an `http:` issuer is refused before any request, proven by the recording `RestOperations` of `OAuth2Configuration` and the recording discovery function of `LazyClientRegistrationRepository`, which both record no call, and the error propagates; a registration whose `token_endpoint`, `jwks_uri`, `userinfo_endpoint`, `authorization_endpoint` or `end_session_endpoint` is `http:` is refused and no registration is exposed; an `http:` `introspection_endpoint` is refused before `OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration` is selected; mocked HTTPS metadata that advertises an `http:` `jwks_uri` is refused by the policy `RestTemplate` before the JWK Set request is sent; an all-`https:` registration passes with no `WARN`. Malformed: a relative, host-less, blank or unparsable issuer or endpoint value is refused at `false`. Switch values: a blank value and `yes` fail with the property-naming error, `TRUE` and `False` are accepted, and an unset property behaves as `true`. Settings save, in `OAuth2EndpointPolicyTest` with an `InMemoryProperties` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.workspace/src/org/openl/config/InMemoryProperties.java`) over a `MockEnvironment` as the in-memory `PropertiesHolder`: at `false`, `OAuth2AuthenticationSettings.store` refuses an `http:` issuer with the error naming both properties and leaves the `PropertiesHolder` unchanged, with an empty `getConfig()`, a relative issuer is refused the same way, and an `https:` issuer is stored; at `true`, and with the switch unset, it stores the `http:` issuer as today; a blank or `yes` switch value fails the save with the property-naming error and leaves the `PropertiesHolder` unchanged. No refusal message or `WARN` line contains the client secret.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 105–180; Test: 315–460; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `OAuth2EndpointPolicyTest` and `OAuth2DiscoveryPolicyTest` prove the default and secure states, the refusal of an `http:` issuer before any discovery request, the refusal of every listed `http:` or malformed endpoint, the refused and unset switch values and the `WARN` lines without contacting a host; `OAuth2EndpointPolicyTest` proves that the OAuth2 settings save refuses an `http:` issuer at `false` with the error and leaves the `PropertiesHolder` unchanged, stores an `https:` issuer at `false` and the `http:` issuer at `true`, and fails on a refused switch value; and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-33 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switch that refuses unauthenticated SAML IdP metadata locations

- Finding: [SEC-35](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-35)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-319
- Severity: High 7.5
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` (constructor `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:35-38`, metadata load `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:52-58`, catch-all `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:64-66`, certificate pin `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:70-83`), created only through the unchanged `OpenLSamlBuilder` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/OpenLSamlBuilder.java:60-63`) by the `saml`-mode `SamlSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:47`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:180-188`); `SAMLAuthenticationSettings` (metadata URL `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:36-41`, `store` `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:81-96`); `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; new test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/saml/SamlMetadataTransportTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - The `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` constructor MUST read and validate `security.saml.allow-insecure-metadata` once, before `init()`: the value is `true` or `false`, compared case-insensitively after trimming, an unset property is `true`, and any other value, blank included, MUST fail construction with an explicit error naming the property, the rejected value and the accepted values.
  - The location rule MUST allow a metadata location only when it parses as an absolute URI whose scheme, compared case-insensitively, is `https` with a host, or `file` with an empty or `localhost` host, and MUST refuse every other value at `false`: blank, relative and scheme-less values, which the resource loader reads from the classpath, `classpath:`, `http:`, `jar:`, every other scheme, and `file:` with any other host, which is not a local resource. Local metadata is therefore referenced only by a `file:` URL.
  - At `false`, a refused location MUST fail the constructor with an explicit error naming `security.saml.saml-server-metadata-url` and `security.saml.allow-insecure-metadata` before any metadata fetch, and the error MUST NEVER contain the location value, because a URL can carry user-info credentials. The refusal MUST propagate and fail `saml`-mode startup, and MUST NEVER be caught by the `init()` catch-all (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:64-66`).
  - The same location check MUST run before every metadata fetch in `init()` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:52-58`), so a lazy retry NEVER fetches a refused location.
  - One public static check in `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` MUST hold the switch parsing and the location rule. At `false`, `SAMLAuthenticationSettings.store` MUST apply it to the submitted metadata URL before its first `setProperty` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:81-96`) and fail the save with the same error, so the stored settings stay unchanged and a save NEVER commits a location that the next `saml`-mode start refuses.
  - The repository MUST gain a package-private constructor that takes the metadata loader, and the public constructor MUST pass `RelyingPartyRegistrations::fromMetadataLocation`, so the tests prove that the check runs before any fetch without opening a location.
  - `https:` locations MUST keep today's loading through the JDK default trust store and hostname verification, and MUST NEVER use a trust-all trust manager or hostname verifier; `file:` locations keep today's loading.
  - In both states a non-blank `security.saml.server-certificate` MUST keep replacing the metadata verification credentials exactly as today (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:70-83`), and an allowed location that cannot be read MUST keep today's logged error and lazy retry.
  - At `true`, loading, the lazy retry and the settings save MUST behave exactly as today; the `WARN` is the only addition.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.saml.allow-insecure-metadata` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `security.saml.saml-server-metadata-url` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:214`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `WARN` owner: the `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` constructor logs one line while the value is `true` and the metadata location is one the secure value refuses, naming SEC-35, the property and `false`. The constructor owns the line because it alone reads the metadata location at startup, it runs once per `saml`-mode start while `init()` retries lazily, and it already holds the one parse of the switch.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value fails startup and the SAML settings save for a plain-HTTP, classpath or other non-HTTPS, non-local metadata location; operators publish the IdP metadata over HTTPS or download it to a local file referenced by a `file:` URL, and keep pinning the IdP certificate in `security.saml.server-certificate`.
- Tests: `SamlMetadataTransportTest` (new) in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/saml/`, with `MockEnvironment` settings, a recording metadata loader passed to the package-private constructor, log capture, an IdP metadata XML fixture written to a `@TempDir` with a locally generated certificate, locally generated SP key material and an in-memory `PropertiesHolder`. It NEVER opens a remote location.
  - Default: an `http:` location passes the scheme policy, reaches the recording loader and logs the `WARN`, the same holds with the property unset, and upper-case and space-padded values are accepted.
  - Secure: an `http:` location fails construction with the error naming both properties before the loader is invoked, and blank, scheme-less, `classpath:`, `jar:` and non-local `file:` locations fail the same way; an `https:` location passes the scheme check and reaches only the recording loader, so it is never opened; the local `file:` fixture builds the registration through `RelyingPartyRegistrations::fromMetadataLocation` with the fixture's verification certificate and single sign-on location; no `WARN` is logged.
  - Pin and retry: a pinned `security.saml.server-certificate` replaces the fixture's verification certificate at the default and at the secure value; an unreadable local `file:` location logs the error and leaves no registration, and the next lookup builds the registration once the file exists.
  - Settings save: at `false`, `SAMLAuthenticationSettings.store` refuses an `http:` URL with the same error and leaves the `PropertiesHolder` unchanged, and it stores `https:` and `file:` URLs; at `true` it stores the `http:` URL as today.
  - Refused values: blank and `yes` fail construction with the error naming the property, the rejected value and the accepted values, before the loader is invoked.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 40–65; Test: 162–245; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `SamlMetadataTransportTest` proves the default, unset, secure and refused-value states, the refusal before any fetch, the pin in both states and the settings-save refusal without opening a remote location, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-35 entry.
