CRITICAL Directive: Parse deployer descriptors with a hardened XML parser and refuse document type declarations

- Finding: [SEC-36](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-36)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-611
- Severity: High 7.5
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: `RulesDeployerService.evaluateXPath` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:104-113`) and its two callers in the same class, the `rules.xml` project-name read in `deployInternal` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:269-273`) and the `rules-deploy.xml` version read in `createFileData` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:411-419`); unchanged read references: `validateSignature` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:437-448`), the upload and redeploy routes of `RulesDeployerRestController`, whose `IllegalArgumentException` handlers return 400 with the exception message (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:52-58`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:69-76`), and `DeployClasspathJarsBean.deployFirst`, which logs that message (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/loader/DeployClasspathJarsBean.java:120-121`); new test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerServiceDescriptorTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - `evaluateXPath` MUST parse the descriptor into a DOM `Document` with a `DocumentBuilderFactory` that is namespace-aware and non-validating, as the parser behind today's XPath call is, and that sets `XMLConstants.FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING` to `true`, `http://apache.org/xml/features/disallow-doctype-decl` to `true`, `http://xml.org/sax/features/external-general-entities`, `http://xml.org/sax/features/external-parameter-entities` and `http://apache.org/xml/features/nonvalidating/load-external-dtd` to `false`, XInclude awareness and entity-reference expansion to `false`, and `XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_DTD` and `XMLConstants.ACCESS_EXTERNAL_SCHEMA` to the empty string.
  - Every one of these settings MUST be applied before any descriptor is parsed. When one cannot be applied, the deployment MUST be refused, and the descriptor MUST NEVER be parsed by a factory that lacks it.
  - The XPath expression MUST be evaluated on that parsed `Document`, and NEVER on an `InputSource`, a stream or any other source that makes the XPath implementation parse the descriptor itself.
  - When the hardened parse refuses or cannot read `rules.xml` or `rules-deploy.xml`, a document type declaration and a document that is not well-formed included, `deploy` MUST throw `IllegalArgumentException` before anything is saved to the repository, so the existing handlers answer 400. It MUST NEVER fall back to the archive name or to an unversioned deployment name, as the silent catch at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:110-112` does today.
  - The refusal message MUST be a fixed text that names only the refused descriptor file, `rules.xml` or `rules-deploy.xml`, because the handlers return it to the caller and `DeployClasspathJarsBean` logs it. It MUST NEVER carry descriptor content, a parser exception message or cause, an entity name or system identifier, or resolved content, and the fix MUST NEVER log any of them.
  - A descriptor without a document type declaration that parses today MUST give exactly today's result: the `/project/name` and `/rules-deploy/version` expressions, their trimming to null, the archive-name fallback for a missing or blank project name and the unversioned deployment name for a missing or blank version stay unchanged.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: new package-private JUnit 5 class `RulesDeployerServiceDescriptorTest` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/`, NEVER annotated `@Disabled`, because the existing `RulesDeployerServiceTest` is disabled at class level (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerServiceTest.java:47`) and none of its methods runs. It builds `RulesDeployerService` on a mocked `Repository` as `RulesDeployerServiceTest` does (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerServiceTest.java:68-73`), writes every archive in-JVM into a JUnit `@TempDir`, assembles each descriptor text at run time, and captures the deployer's log output in-JVM from `System.err`, where the test classpath's slf4j-simple writes (`pom.xml:1192-1196`). No server starts, and no fixture names a network location. It MUST prove each of these cases:
  - a `rules.xml`, and separately a `rules-deploy.xml`, whose internal document type declaration declares an external entity naming a secret file in the `@TempDir` is refused with `IllegalArgumentException`, nothing is saved, and the secret file's content appears in none of the refusal message, the captured `FileData` names and the captured log output;
  - a descriptor with a document type declaration and no entity is refused the same way, and a descriptor that is not well-formed is refused;
  - an ordinary `rules.xml` still names the deployment by its `/project/name`, and an ordinary `rules-deploy.xml` still adds its `/rules-deploy/version` suffix.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 20–40; Test: 132–192; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `RulesDeployerServiceDescriptorTest` proves that every descriptor carrying a document type declaration, the external-entity cases included, and every descriptor that is not well-formed is refused before any save without the secret file's content reaching a deployment name, the refusal message or the log, while ordinary `rules.xml` names and `rules-deploy.xml` versions are read as today.

CRITICAL Directive: Add switched minimum and maximum lengths for internal passwords

- Finding: [SEC-08](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-08)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-521
- Severity: High 7.4
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `InternalPasswordConstraintValidator` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/validation/InternalPasswordConstraintValidator.java:25-36`), `MultiSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/MultiSecurityConfig.java`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/rest/validation/UsersValidatorTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - `InternalPasswordConstraintValidator` MUST read `security.password.min-length` and `security.password.max-length` instead of the literal 25, refusing a non-blank password shorter than the minimum or longer than the maximum with the existing size messages.
  - The blank-password handling MUST stay as today: multi mode still refuses a blank password when internal users can be created.
  - This directive NEVER adds login lockout; SEC-13 owns it.
  - Validation: both values are `int`. `security.password.min-length` and `security.password.max-length` MUST each be 0 or above, and the minimum MUST NOT exceed the maximum. `MultiSecurityConfig` MUST refuse a value outside these bounds, and a minimum above the maximum with an error naming both properties, when OpenL Studio starts in `multi` mode, the only mode that stores internal passwords (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/MultiSecurityConfig.java:24-25`). The blank-password handling above stays independent of both bounds.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.password.min-length` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `security.password.encoder` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:5`); default `0`; recommended secure value `12`.
  - `security.password.max-length` in the same place; default `25`; recommended secure value `64`.
  - `WARN` owner: `MultiSecurityConfig` logs one line while the minimum is below 12 and one line while the maximum is below 64, each naming SEC-08, its property and its secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the minimum refuses new passwords shorter than 12 characters; existing passwords keep working until they are changed.
- Tests: `UsersValidatorTest`. Default: a 0-character and a 26-character password follow today's mode-specific rules. Secure: a password shorter than 12 is refused, 26 to 64 characters are accepted, and above 64 is refused. Partially hardened: the minimum at 12 with the maximum at 25 asserts the remaining `WARN`. At startup in `multi` mode the `MultiSecurityConfig` check refuses with the property-naming error a minimum of `-1`, a maximum of `-1`, a blank or non-integer value, and a minimum of `30` with a maximum of `20`; a minimum equal to the maximum, `0` with `0` included, is accepted, and an unset property behaves as its default.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 35–60; Test: 100–180; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `UsersValidatorTest` proves the default, secure, partially hardened and refused states, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-08 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add switched failed-login counting and timed lockout to OpenL Studio

- Finding: [SEC-13](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-13)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-307
- Severity: High 7.4
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-49`), `MultiSecurityConfig.daoAuthenticationProvider` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/MultiSecurityConfig.java:39-47`), `AdSecurityConfig.adAuthenticationProvider` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdSecurityConfig.java:33-46`), new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FailedLoginTracker.java`, new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/LockoutAuthenticationProvider.java`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; read only: `CommonAuthenticationConfig.authenticationManager` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:37-49`), `UserInfoUserDetailsServiceImpl.loadUserByUsername` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/UserInfoUserDetailsServiceImpl.java:31-52`), `HibernateUserDao.getUserByName` (`STUDIO/org.openl.security.standalone/src/org/openl/rules/security/standalone/dao/HibernateUserDao.java:32-39`), `LdapToOpenLUserDetailsMapper` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/ad/LdapToOpenLUserDetailsMapper.java:100-117`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/ad/LdapToOpenLUserDetailsMapper.java:250-255`); new tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/FailedLoginTrackerTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/LoginLockoutTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - In `multi` and `ad` modes with `security.login.max-failed-attempts` above 0, `FailedLoginTracker` MUST count consecutive failed logins per canonical account key thread-safely and MUST refuse that account's login for `security.login.lockout-seconds` once the count reaches the limit; a successful login resets the count, and the lock ends when the period expires.
  - At the default (`0`) `LockoutAuthenticationProvider` MUST lock no account, exactly as today: it MUST pass every attempt to the provider it wraps and return that provider's result or exception unchanged, creating no entry and making no lookup. The Git credential back-off (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.git/resources/openl-default.properties:14-19`) MUST stay unchanged, and SEC-08 stays a separate directive.
  - One enforcement point for both channels: `MultiSecurityConfig.daoAuthenticationProvider` and `AdSecurityConfig.adAuthenticationProvider` MUST each return the provider they configure today wrapped in `LockoutAuthenticationProvider`, which stays the only `AuthenticationProvider` bean of its mode; no injection point names either concrete provider type. The login form (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:43`) and HTTP Basic (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:30`) therefore count against one shared state per account, because Spring Security 6.5.11 (`pom.xml:100`) builds the parent manager of both `HttpSecurity` chains from that single bean through `InitializeAuthenticationProviderBeanManagerConfigurer`, and the `CommonAuthenticationConfig` `ProviderManager` wraps the same bean. `supports` MUST delegate to the wrapped provider, and only a `UsernamePasswordAuthenticationToken` is ever counted. `LockoutAuthenticationProvider` MUST forward `setMessageSource` and `afterPropertiesSet` to the wrapped provider whenever it implements `MessageSourceAware` or `InitializingBean`, so the wrapped provider keeps every container callback it receives today. The wrapping MUST happen only in the `return` of those two bean methods, around the provider they build and configure, and `LockoutAuthenticationProvider` NEVER constructs that provider or changes its settings beyond forwarding those two callbacks, so the provider stays reachable unwrapped where it is built: once SEC-17 moves the AD construction into the non-bean method `AdSecurityConfig.activeDirectoryProvider`, `adAuthenticationProvider` wraps that method's result, and SEC-17's test reads the provider from that method whether or not this directive is applied.
  - Wiring: `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` MUST declare `FailedLoginTracker` as a bean built from the two validated values and `Clock.systemUTC()`, and both provider bean methods receive it, `daoAuthenticationProvider` together with the `openlUserDao` `UserDao`. `LockoutAuthenticationProvider` MUST offer one factory method per mode that takes the wrapped provider, so the tests wrap a Mockito provider with the production key rule of either mode and give the tracker a controllable `Clock`. Both values MUST be injected with inline defaults equal to their declared defaults, `${security.login.max-failed-attempts:0}` and `${security.login.lockout-seconds:300}`, so a context that does not load `openl-default.properties` starts with lockout off. The SEC-04 `multi` and `ad` mode contexts of `SecurityChainTestConfiguration` therefore load without a change: both import `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig`, which declares the tracker; `multi` already holds the Mockito `openlUserDao`, which the default wrapper never queries; and `ad` puts a Mockito provider in place of `AdSecurityConfig`, so nothing wraps it. Every test on those contexts, `CsrfSecurityFilterChainTest` included, MUST keep passing unchanged.
  - Canonical account key: the key MUST be derived before any count is read or written, so every name the mode resolves to one account shares one key. `multi`: the stored login name that `UserDao.getUserByName` returns for the submitted name, the lookup `loadUserByUsername` makes, so the database's own name comparison decides. A name resolving to no account gets no key, creates no entry and reaches the wrapped provider uncounted; a lookup that throws counts nothing and fails the attempt, without calling the wrapped provider, with an `InternalAuthenticationServiceException` carrying that cause, as `DaoAuthenticationProvider` reports the same failure of `loadUserByUsername` today. `ad`: the bind principal `ActiveDirectoryLdapAuthenticationProvider` derives from the submitted name with surrounding whitespace removed, lower-cased with `Locale.ROOT`: the name alone when `security.ad.domain` is blank or the lower-cased name ends with the lower-cased domain, otherwise the name, `@` and the domain (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/ad/LdapToOpenLUserDetailsMapper.java:250-255`), because the directory matches logon names without regard to case (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/ad/LdapToOpenLUserDetailsMapper.java:100-117`).
  - Atomic counting: per key, admitting an attempt, keeping a failure, releasing a reservation and clearing after a success MUST each be one atomic step, for example one `ConcurrentHashMap.compute` call. An admitted attempt reserves its place in the count BEFORE the wrapped provider runs, and the count is the kept failures plus the open reservations, so concurrent attempts NEVER let more than `security.login.max-failed-attempts` outcomes through: the attempt whose reservation brings the count to the limit starts the lock and keeps its own outcome, and every later attempt during the lock is refused. A `BadCredentialsException` keeps the reservation as a failure. Any other `AuthenticationException`, such as the `InternalAuthenticationServiceException` of an unreachable user database or directory, releases the reservation and passes through unchanged, and a lock whose count a release brings below the limit ends at once, so an outage NEVER locks an account. A success clears the entry, its lock included, and a reservation opened before that clearing NEVER changes the entry that replaces it.
  - Bounded state: `FailedLoginTracker` MUST key its entries by the SHA-256 digest of the account key's UTF-8 bytes, so an entry's size NEVER depends on the submitted name, and MUST hold at most 10000 entries, a fixed capacity and not a property, because SEC-13 adds exactly the two properties below. An unlocked entry expires `security.login.lockout-seconds` after its latest failure and a locked entry when its lock ends; an entry holding an open reservation neither expires nor is evicted. An expired entry counts as absent and is removed when its key is next used, and every expired entry is removed before any eviction. Admitting a key into a full table MUST evict the unlocked entry with the fewest failures, the oldest latest failure first, and NEVER a locked entry. When no entry can be evicted, an attempt whose key has no entry is refused as a locked attempt is, and `FailedLoginTracker` logs at most one `WARN` line per lockout period naming SEC-13 and the capacity.
  - Saturation trade-off: that fail-closed refusal is a deliberate rule. In `ad` mode the key comes from the submitted name without a directory lookup, so names that reach no account can fill the table with locks, and a table holding 10000 locked entries refuses every account that has no entry, its correct password included, until the earliest lock ends and frees its entry. The remediation run MUST NEVER avoid that refusal by evicting a locked entry, which would end a lock early, or by passing an attempt whose key has no entry to the wrapped provider uncounted, which would leave guesses against every untracked account unlimited while the table stays full. The proxy rate limit that the migration entry below requires is the mandatory complement.
  - Indistinguishable refusals: a refused attempt MUST still call the wrapped provider exactly once and discard its outcome, so a lock NEVER shortens the refusal path, and a correct password during the lock is refused and NEVER resets the count. The refusal MUST rethrow the wrapped provider's `BadCredentialsException`, and otherwise throw a new `BadCredentialsException` with the message that provider gives a wrong password, read through the forwarded `MessageSource` under that provider's key (`AbstractUserDetailsAuthenticationProvider.badCredentials` in `multi`, `LdapAuthenticationProvider.badCredentials` in `ad`, default `Bad credentials`). The login form therefore keeps its redirect to `/login?error` and HTTP Basic its 401 from `HttpStatusEntryPoint`, with the same headers and body as an unknown name or a wrong password, and a lock-specific message, status, header, redirect target or exception type NEVER reaches a client. The lock reason stays on the server: `FailedLoginTracker` logs one INFO line naming SEC-13 when a lock starts, NEVER with the submitted name, the account key or a password.
  - Validation: both values are `int` and MUST be 0 or above. `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` MUST refuse a negative, blank, non-integer or out-of-`int`-range value when OpenL Studio starts in `multi` or `ad` mode. Attempts `0` keeps lockout off, and lockout `0` with attempts above `0` stays valid and logs its `WARN`.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.login.max-failed-attempts` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `0` (0 disables lockout); recommended secure value `5`.
  - `security.login.lockout-seconds` in the same file; default `300`; recommended secure value `300`.
  - `WARN` owner: `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` in `multi` or `ad` mode logs one line while the attempts value is `0` and one line while the lockout is `0` with attempts above `0`, each naming SEC-13, its property and its secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the attempts value locks an account for 300 seconds after 5 failures without a success, each within 300 seconds of the one before, counted across the login form and HTTP Basic and across the name forms that reach one account, and a locked account is refused exactly as a wrong password is. A script client that uses HTTP Basic counts against the same limit as the account's interactive logins, so it MUST stop resending a refused credential. In `ad` mode at the secure value the proxy MUST rate-limit per client the OpenL Studio login form (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:43`, `/studio/login` behind the proxy) and HTTP Basic requests (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:27-30`, `/studio/rest/` behind the proxy), and operators MUST configure that limit before they flip the value, because the reference proxy (`compose.yaml:149-199`) has no rate limit: a client that locks 10000 names fills the lockout table, and every account it does not track is then refused, its correct password included, until those locks end. That limit and wider API rate limiting stay proxy actions (section 6 of `SECURITY_FINDINGS.md`).
- Tests:
  - `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/FailedLoginTrackerTest.java` (new) drives `FailedLoginTracker` and `LockoutAuthenticationProvider` with a controllable `Clock`, a Mockito `UserDao` and a Mockito wrapped provider. `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/LoginLockoutTest.java` (new) runs a MockMvc context holding both `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` chains over the same mocks, in `multi` and `ad` modes, with no database, directory or server.
  - Default: repeated failed logins never lock the account, no entry is created, and the wrapped provider's result or exception passes through unchanged.
  - Secure: the account is refused after 5 failures until the lockout ends, then a correct login succeeds. 4 concurrent failures below the limit leave a count of exactly 4. 20 concurrent attempts on one account, with the wrapped provider held until all 20 have been admitted or refused, let exactly 5 reach their own outcome and refuse the other 15, whether the provider refuses or accepts them. An `InternalAuthenticationServiceException` is not counted and passes through unchanged, a release ends a lock its reservation started, and a `UserDao` lookup that throws fails the attempt with an `InternalAuthenticationServiceException` and counts nothing.
  - Partially hardened: `security.login.lockout-seconds` at its secure value `300`, which is also its default, with `security.login.max-failed-attempts` at its default `0` asserts the remaining attempts `WARN`. Separately, attempts `5` with lockout `0` asserts the lockout `WARN`.
  - Refused at startup with the property-naming error: `-1` for either property and a blank or non-integer value. An unset property behaves as its default.
  - Account keys: in `multi`, a name the Mockito `UserDao` resolves to the stored `alice`, such as `Alice`, shares the counter of `alice`, and a name resolving to no account creates no entry. In `ad` with `security.ad.domain` `example.com`, `alice`, `ALICE`, `alice` with surrounding spaces and `Alice@Example.com` share one counter; with a blank domain, `alice@example.com` and `ALICE@EXAMPLE.COM` share one.
  - Both channels: failures alternating between the login form and HTTP Basic, under those alias names, lock the account after 5 in total, and the lock then refuses both channels.
  - Bounds: in `multi`, 20000 distinct unknown names create no entry. In `ad`, 20000 distinct names leave at most 10000 entries, an entry with 4 failures survives a flood of single-failure names, and an account locked before the flood stays refused until its lock ends. A count expires 300 seconds after its latest failure, and a lock when it ends. With every entry locked, a new name is refused as a locked attempt is, and the saturation `WARN` appears once per lockout period.
  - Saturation trade-off: in `FailedLoginTrackerTest`, in `ad` mode with every one of the 10000 entries locked, an untracked account with its correct password is refused as a locked attempt is, with the same exception type and message and one wrapped-provider call, until the controllable `Clock` passes the earliest lock's end. The same correct password then logs in, and every other locked account stays refused until its own lock ends.
  - Indistinguishable refusals: at the secure value, an unknown name, a known name with a wrong password and a locked account with its correct password each get the same redirect to `/login?error` from the login form and the same 401 status, headers and body from HTTP Basic, apart from the session cookie value, and each calls the wrapped provider exactly once. The tests compare responses and invocation counts and NEVER measure elapsed time. At the default, the unknown-name and wrong-password cases keep today's responses, and the correct password after 5 failures logs in.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 160–280; Test: 421–715; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `FailedLoginTrackerTest` and `LoginLockoutTest` prove the default, secure, partially hardened and refused states, one counter per account across its name forms, the login form and HTTP Basic, the admission, capacity and expiry bounds, the saturation trade-off case (an untracked account's correct password refused as a locked attempt is until the earliest lock ends, then logged in, with every other lock kept), and identical refusals for unknown, wrong-password and locked attempts, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-13 entry with the `ad`-mode proxy rate-limit requirement.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switched JWT signature algorithm allow-list to OpenL Rule Services

- Finding: [SEC-42](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-42)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-327
- Severity: High 7.4
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: the `JWTValidator` constructor (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:44`, key resolvers `HttpsJwksVerificationKeyResolver` and `JwksVerificationKeyResolver` at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:55-66`, algorithm constraint at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:68-75`), `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`; test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidatorTest.java` (created by SEC-01; created here when absent); `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - A non-empty `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms` MUST replace `AlgorithmConstraints.DISALLOW_NONE` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:74`) with a permit-list constraint holding exactly the listed algorithms.
  - The empty default MUST keep `DISALLOW_NONE`, and the expiry, issuer, audience and signature checks (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:68-75`) MUST stay unchanged in both states. The family rule, the key filter and the construction-time key check below apply only to a non-empty list, so the empty default keeps today's key selection exactly, and the recommended secure value MUST stay a valid asymmetric list.
  - Parsing: the constructor MUST read `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms` before it resolves the JWKS location (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:55-66`), split it on commas and trim each element. A value that is empty after trimming is the legacy default and keeps `DISALLOW_NONE`. In any other value each element MUST be a case-sensitive JWS algorithm name that jose4j 0.9.7 (`pom.xml:109`) registers in `AlgorithmFactoryFactory.getInstance().getJwsAlgorithmFactory().getSupportedAlgorithms()`, other than `none`, and a repeated name counts once.
  - Validation: an empty element, as in `RS256,,ES256` or a trailing comma, an unknown name such as `RS255`, a case variant such as `rs256` and `none` MUST each fail construction with an error naming `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms` and the rejected element, so a malformed list NEVER becomes a permit-list constraint.
  - Family: a non-empty list MUST hold one family only. The family is the key type jose4j assigns to each registered name through `getJwsAlgorithmFactory().getAlgorithm(name).getKeyType()`: `oct` is HMAC (`HS256`, `HS384`, `HS512`), and `RSA` (`RS256`, `RS384`, `RS512`, `PS256`, `PS384`, `PS512`), `EC` (`ES256`, `ES384`, `ES512`, and `ES256K` only where the JDK registers it) and `OKP` (`EdDSA`) are asymmetric. A list that mixes an HMAC name with any asymmetric name, as in `RS256,HS256` or `HS256,ES256`, MUST fail construction before the JWKS location is resolved, with an error naming `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms`, the conflicting HMAC and asymmetric elements and the two accepted families.
  - Key binding: while the list is non-empty, a verification key MUST be used only with the listed algorithms of its own key type: an `RSA` key with `RS*` and `PS*`, an `EC` key with the `ES*` name of its curve (`P-256` `ES256`, `P-384` `ES384`, `P-521` `ES512`, `secp256k1` `ES256K`), an `OKP` key with `EdDSA`, and an `oct` key with `HS*`. A JWKS key that carries `alg` MUST be used only when that `alg` is in the list and equals the token header algorithm. One key filter MUST enforce both rules before key selection, for the key set read at construction and for every key set the `https:` resolver fetches; `HttpsJwksVerificationKeyResolver.select(JsonWebSignature, List)` is protected in jose4j 0.9.7, so a nested subclass inside `JWTValidator` filters the fetched keys.
  - Construction-time key check: a key set the constructor reads at construction (every location that is not `https:`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:60-66`) that holds no key compatible with any listed algorithm MUST fail construction with an error naming `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms`, the listed algorithms and the key types the set holds. Compatible means the key's `kty` is the algorithm's key type, an `EC` key's `crv` is that algorithm's curve, and any `alg` the key carries is in the list.
  - Header and key type, kept: jose4j 0.9.7 (`pom.xml:109`) already refuses a token whose header algorithm does not match the resolved key's type, and the fix MUST keep both checks. Key selection in `VerificationJwkSelector` keeps only keys whose `kty` is the header algorithm's key type, and `JsonWebSignature.verifySignature` runs the algorithm's `validateVerificationKey`: `RS*`, `PS*`, `ES*` and `EdDSA` refuse a key that is not a `PublicKey`, `ES*` refuses a key on another curve, `RS*` and `PS*` refuse an RSA key below 2048 bits, and `HmacUsingShaAlgorithm` refuses a key that is not a `SecretKey`. The fix MUST NEVER call `JwtConsumerBuilder.setRelaxVerificationKeyValidation()` and MUST NEVER resolve keys outside that selector. jose4j compares a key's `alg` only when several keys remain after its `kid`, `kty`, `use` and `key_ops` filters, and never with the list, so the key filter above is required.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `ruleservice.authentication.enabled` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59`); default empty (every algorithm except `none`); recommended secure value `RS256,RS384,RS512,PS256,PS384,PS512,ES256,ES384,ES512`.
  - `WARN` owner: the `JWTValidator` constructor logs one line while authentication is enabled and the list is empty, naming SEC-42, the property and the secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value refuses tokens signed with an algorithm outside the list, including HMAC algorithms; issuers sign with a listed algorithm. An HMAC issuer instead lists only `HS256`, `HS384` or `HS512` with a local `oct` key set, because a list that mixes HMAC and asymmetric names, or a local key set with no key for a listed algorithm, fails startup.
- Tests: `JWTValidatorTest`. Default: a token signed with any supported algorithm other than `none` and compatible with the configured key is accepted. Secure: a token signed with an algorithm outside the list is refused, while listed algorithms and the issuer, audience and expiry checks keep working. The `WARN` is asserted at the default with authentication enabled. Malformed configuration: `RS256,,ES256`, `RS256,`, `RS255`, `rs256` and `RS256,none` each fail construction with the property-naming error before any JWKS location is opened; a whitespace-only value and an unset property each keep `DISALLOW_NONE`, and `RS256 , ES256` permits exactly those two algorithms. Family and key binding: `RS256,HS256` and `HS256,ES256` each fail construction with the property-naming error before any JWKS location is opened; an `HS256` list with a local `oct` key set accepts an `HS256` token and refuses an `RS256` token; the secure value with a local RSA key set refuses a token whose header names `HS256`; a local key set with no key compatible with the list, such as an `EC`-only set under `RS256`, fails construction with the property-naming error; a local key whose `alg` is outside the list, or differs from the token header, is not used, so a token that only that key verifies is refused.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 59–111; Test: 222–376; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `JWTValidatorTest` proves both states, the malformed-configuration refusals, the mixed-family and key-binding refusals and the `WARN`, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-42 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add authenticated ENC2 secret encryption beside unchanged ENC decryption

- Finding: [SEC-07](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-07)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-327
- Severity: High 7.1
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `PassCoder` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:23-24` zero IV, `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:38-39` and `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:59-60` blank-key pass-through, `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:71-76` truncated SHA-1 key), `DynamicPropertySource` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:138-220`: `save` encrypts only `*password` keys at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:146` and skips a failed encryption at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:147-152`, `encodePassword` returns plain text on a blank key at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:190-197`, `decode` returns an empty string on failure at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:214-216`, `getProperty` decodes without the key at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:92-103`), `ApplicationPropertySource.getProperty` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/ApplicationPropertySource.java:199-209`, the second `decode` caller, value-only `decode` call at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/ApplicationPropertySource.java:206`), `PropertySourcesLoader` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:57-100`), new `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/SecretReencryptor.java`, `DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:12-38` (`secret.key` resolves to the blank legacy `repository.encode.decode.key` at `DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:38`); tests existing `DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoderTest.java`, existing `DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env/RefPropertySourceTest.java`, new `DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env/SecretReencryptorTest.java`, new `DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoaderTest.java` and new `DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env/EncryptedPropertyResolutionTest.java` with its new resource `DEV/org.openl.spring/test-resources/encrypted-properties.properties`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - This directive stands alone and NEVER groups with another finding.
  - MUST add `ENC2(...)` encryption with authenticated encryption (AES-GCM), a random IV per value and a salted, deliberately slow key derivation from `secret.key`, carrying the salt and IV inside the encoded value, under these version `1` parameters:
    - Key derivation: PBKDF2 with HMAC-SHA256 (the JDK `SecretKeyFactory` algorithm `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256`) MUST derive a 256-bit AES key from the effective `secret.key` with exactly 600000 iterations, the OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet minimum for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256. A single or unsalted hash and the legacy `getKey` truncation (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:71-76`) NEVER serve `ENC2`, and the `PBEKeySpec` password MUST be cleared after each derivation.
    - Randomness: every encryption MUST draw a fresh 16-byte salt and, separately, a fresh 12-byte (96-bit) GCM IV from one `java.security.SecureRandom` with the platform default algorithm, NEVER `SecureRandom.getInstanceStrong()`, which blocks on some hosts. Salt and IV are NEVER derived from the key, the plain text, the property name, a counter or each other and NEVER reused, so one plain text encrypted twice gives two different values.
    - Tag: the cipher MUST be `AES/GCM/NoPadding` with the full 128-bit tag (`GCMParameterSpec(128, iv)`); a shorter tag is NEVER written or accepted.
    - Encoding: the text between `ENC2(` and `)` MUST be padded standard Base64 (`java.util.Base64`) of the version byte `1`, the salt, the IV and the ciphertext with its tag, in that order. Version `1` fixes every parameter above, and the iteration count is NEVER read from a value, so a stored value cannot lower or raise the work factor. A later work-factor or algorithm change MUST add a new version byte and keep decoding version `1`.
    - Validation before derivation: decoding MUST refuse invalid Base64, any version byte other than `1` and a decoded length below 45 bytes (1 version byte, 16 salt bytes, 12 IV bytes and 16 tag bytes) with the explicit `ENC2` error defined below, which names the property and NEVER the value. The refusal comes before any key derivation, so a malformed value costs no derivation.
    - Derivation cost: `DynamicPropertySource.getProperty` decodes on every read (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:102`), so `PassCoder` MUST cache derived keys in a thread-safe in-memory map keyed by the `secret.key` value and the salt, bounded at 256 entries with least-recently-used eviction. A miss derives again, the tag is still checked on every decode, and a changed `secret.key` NEVER hits an entry derived from the previous value. `PassCoder` MUST count each derivation in a package-private counter that `PassCoderTest` reads.
  - At `secret.encryption.format=ENC2`, `DynamicPropertySource.save` MUST write `ENC2(...)` values; at the default it writes `ENC(...)` exactly as today.
  - `secret.encryption.name-suffixes` MUST select which property names `save` encrypts; the default `password` reproduces today's `endsWith("password")` check.
  - Key precondition: whenever the format is `ENC2`, an `ENC2(...)` value is decoded or `SecretReencryptor` runs, a non-blank effective `secret.key` MUST be present. It is resolved through the environment, so the legacy `repository.encode.decode.key` reference counts; absent, empty and whitespace-only values are blank.
    - `checkSecretSettings()` (Switch) MUST refuse format `ENC2` with a blank key by throwing an exception naming `secret.key` and `secret.encryption.format`, which fails the environment load.
    - At format `ENC2`, `save` MUST check the key before it changes the in-memory settings or the file whenever the given settings hold a selected property. A blank key or a failed `ENC2` encryption MUST throw an exception naming that property and `secret.key` and leave the settings and the file unchanged; `save` NEVER stores a selected property in plain text or as `ENC(...)`.
    - Decoding an `ENC2(...)` value with a blank key MUST raise the explicit `ENC2` error below.
    - The new `PassCoder` `ENC2` methods MUST reject a blank key and NEVER derive a key from a blank value; the legacy pass-through at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:38-39` and `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:59-60` stays.
    - `SecretReencryptor` MUST abort on a blank key before it reads or writes the file.
    - Every such error names properties only, NEVER the `secret.key` value, the stored text or the plain text. At format `ENC` a blank key keeps today's behavior: the load succeeds, and `save` stores plain text without throwing.
  - A failed `ENC2(...)` decryption, including a tampered value or a wrong key, MUST raise an explicit error naming the property key and NEVER return an empty string.
  - Contextual decoding: the property key MUST reach decryption from both property-source paths. MUST add the keyed overload `DynamicPropertySource.decode(String name, String value)`, and `DynamicPropertySource.getProperty` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:92-103`, call at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:102`) and `ApplicationPropertySource.getProperty` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/ApplicationPropertySource.java:199-209`, call at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/ApplicationPropertySource.java:206`) MUST pass it the requested property name. The existing value-only `decode(String)` MUST stay, keeping its signature and its current results for `ENC(...)` and plain values, and no production code calls it.
    - The `ENC` failure `WARN` and the `ENC2` error MUST both name that property. Through a `$ref` reference, which `RefPropertySource` resolves by reading the referenced key (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/RefPropertySource.java:61-63`), they MUST name the referenced key whose stored value failed.
    - An `ENC2(...)` value read before the source is registered (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:89`) raises the same explicit error, NEVER an empty string.
  - `ENC(...)` decryption, including its current empty-string result on failure, MUST stay unchanged; one `WARN` naming the property key on an `ENC` decryption failure is the only addition.
  - `SecretReencryptor` in `DEV/org.openl.spring` MUST rewrite the `ENC(...)` values of a settings file as `ENC2(...)` values and leave every other line unchanged, under this contract:
    - Entry points: `public static int convert(Path settingsFile, String secretKey, String legacyCipher)`, returning the number of converted values, and `main(String[] args)`, taking exactly one argument, the settings file. `main` passes `System.getenv()` to a package-private `run(String[] args, Map<String, String> environment)`, which reads the key from `SECRET_KEY`, the environment name Spring resolves for `secret.key`, and the legacy cipher from `SECRET_CIPHER`, default `AES/CBC/PKCS5Padding` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:35`). The key is NEVER a command-line argument and NEVER printed, and no method calls `System.exit`. `run` prints the number of converted values and the converted property keys, NEVER a value, and throws on every refusal and failure, so `main` ends with a non-zero exit status through the uncaught exception and a successful run ends with status 0.
    - Input: the file is parsed with the `PropertiesUtils.load` grammar (`DEV/org.openl.commons/src/org/openl/util/PropertiesUtils.java:41-150`: UTF-8, separators, backslash escapes, continuation lines). Each `ENC(...)` value is decrypted with the legacy cipher through the legacy `PassCoder.decode` directly, NEVER through `DynamicPropertySource.decode`, whose failure result is an empty string.
    - Output: only those values MUST become `ENC2(...)`, keeping the key text and separator; every other line, `ENC2(...)` value, plain value, comment, continued and escaped property MUST stay byte-identical. A file with no `ENC(...)` value is NEVER rewritten.
    - Failure: a decryption failure, a malformed value (invalid Base64, unbalanced `ENC(`, empty `ENC()`), decrypted bytes that are not valid UTF-8, or a read or write failure MUST abort without replacing the file. Errors name the file and, for a value, its property key and line, NEVER a value.
    - Offline precondition: conversion MUST run only while no process reads or writes the file, so every process that loads it through `PropertySourcesLoader`, namely OpenL Studio, OpenL Rule Services and any application embedding `OpenLService`, on every node that shares the `openl.home.shared` folder holding it, MUST be stopped first. `DynamicPropertySource.save` synchronizes only on its own instance (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:138`), builds its write from its in-memory snapshot (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:139`) and rewrites the whole file (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:199-206`), so converting a live file either discards a concurrent save or is overwritten by a later save from a stale snapshot. Quiescence, not a lock, is the guarantee: `run` MUST print this prerequisite before converting, and the SEC-07 `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry states it as an operator step.
    - File checks: the path MUST be examined without following links (`LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS`). A symbolic link, a directory, a special file and a regular file with more than one hard link (`unix:nlink` above 1 where the platform reports it) are refused with an error naming the file before anything is written, because a move would replace the link itself or leave another name holding the `ENC(...)` values; the operator converts the link target's own path.
    - Change detection: `convert` MUST record the file key (`BasicFileAttributes.fileKey()` where the platform supplies one), the size and the last-modified time with the content it reads, and re-read them without following links immediately before the move. Any difference, a file replaced under the same name included, aborts with the error naming the file and leaves the file at that path untouched. This check detects a change and is not an atomic compare-and-replace, so the offline precondition stays the guarantee. A package-private `convert` overload takes a hook that runs between the read and the move, used only by `SecretReencryptorTest`.
    - Replacement: the new content goes to a temporary file created in the same folder with the original file's permissions, and `Files.move` with `StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE` replaces the original. When the file system refuses an atomic move, the conversion aborts and NEVER falls back to a copy or a non-atomic move. On any failure the temporary file is deleted and the file at that path stays byte-identical.
  - Validation: `secret.encryption.format` accepts exactly `ENC` and `ENC2`, case-sensitive after trimming. `DynamicPropertySource` MUST refuse every other value, a blank one included, with an error naming the property and both accepted values before `save` writes any value.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `secret.encryption.format` in `DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside the `secret.*` keys (`DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:12-38`); default `ENC`; recommended secure value `ENC2`.
  - `secret.encryption.name-suffixes` in the same place; default `password`; recommended secure value `password,secret,secret-key,access-key,account-key,client-secret,local-key`.
  - `WARN` owner: `DynamicPropertySource`, through a package-private `checkSecretSettings()` that `PropertySourcesLoader.loadEnvironment` MUST call exactly once per environment load, immediately after `props.initFirewall()` (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:98`) and before `loadEnvironment` returns. It reads `secret.encryption.format`, `secret.encryption.name-suffixes` and `secret.key` through the completed resolver (settings file, application files, environment, system properties, firewall). It logs one startup line while the format is `ENC` and one while the suffix list omits any suffix of the secure value, which leaves those secrets in plain text; each line names SEC-07, its property and its secure value. It then performs the `ENC2` key check of the key precondition.
  - The constructor, which loads the settings before the source is inserted (`DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:88-90`), logs nothing for SEC-07; `reloadIfModified` and `save` NEVER repeat the startup lines; the `ENC` decryption-failure `WARN` stays a separate decode-time line. OpenL Studio (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:119`), OpenL Rule Services (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:42`) and `OpenLService` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/OpenLService.java:283`) all reach `loadEnvironment`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the format writes `ENC2(...)` values that earlier releases cannot read, so every node is upgraded before the flip and `SecretReencryptor` converts existing settings files only while every process that uses them is stopped; `ENC(...)` values keep decrypting, and each `ENC2(...)` value costs one PBKDF2 derivation per process.
- Tests: in `DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env`, in the `PassCoderTest` style, in-JVM on temporary files and one test resource, with no server.
  - `PassCoderTest`, `RefPropertySourceTest` and `SecretReencryptorTest`. Default: `ENC(...)` values round-trip unchanged and a failed `ENC` decryption keeps its current result and logs the `WARN`. Secure: `ENC2(...)` values round-trip; a tampered value or a wrong key raises the explicit error naming the property; the utility converts `ENC` values to `ENC2`; `ENC(...)` values still decrypt.
  - `ENC2` format, in `PassCoderTest`: a known-answer value that the test builds itself with the JDK's `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256` at 600000 iterations and `AES/GCM/NoPadding` with a 128-bit tag decodes through `PassCoder`, while the same value built at 599999 iterations or with a 96-bit tag raises the explicit error. An encoded value carries version `1`, a 16-byte salt, a 12-byte IV and a 16-byte tag, and encrypting one plain text twice gives different salts, IVs and values. Invalid Base64, version bytes `0` and `2` and a 44-byte payload each raise the explicit error naming the property and never the value, with zero derivations on the package-private derivation counter. One value decoded twice derives once, a different `secret.key` derives again, and the cache never exceeds 256 entries.
  - In `PropertySourcesLoaderTest`, a format of `ENC3`, `enc2` or a blank value is refused with the property-naming error before any value is written.
  - `PropertySourcesLoaderTest` loads a real environment through `PropertySourcesLoader` with a settings file in a temporary `openl.home.shared` folder. Values set only in that file, beside a non-blank `secret.key`, decide the startup lines: default, both; secure, none; partially hardened (format `ENC2`, suffixes `password`), the suffix line only. Because those values exist only in the settings file, the test proves that `checkSecretSettings()` runs after the source is inserted at `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PropertySourcesLoader.java:90`. Each line appears once per load and never after `reloadIfModified` or `save`.
  - Key precondition, each with an absent and with a blank `secret.key`. `PropertySourcesLoaderTest`: format `ENC2` fails the load naming `secret.key`; `save` on a `DynamicPropertySource` built directly over format `ENC2` throws naming `secret.key`, leaves the settings and the file unchanged and stores no plain text; an `ENC2(...)` value read at format `ENC` raises the explicit error. `PassCoderTest`: the `ENC2` methods refuse the key while the legacy methods keep their pass-through. `SecretReencryptorTest`: the conversion aborts with the file unchanged. At format `ENC` with an absent key, `PropertySourcesLoaderTest` proves the load succeeds and `save` stores plain text as today.
  - Contextual errors: a wrong-key and a tampered `ENC2(...)` value each raise the error naming the right key when read from the settings file and from an application profile file (`{appName}-{profile}.properties` in a temporary folder named by `openl.config.location`) in `PropertySourcesLoaderTest`, and through a `$ref` reference in `RefPropertySourceTest`, where it names the referenced key.
  - Property resolution, in `EncryptedPropertyResolutionTest`: it resolves the new resource `encrypted-properties.properties` through `PropertySourcesLoader` as `PropertyResolverTest` does (`DEV/org.openl.spring/test/org/openl/spring/env/PropertyResolverTest.java:12-13`), so `ApplicationPropertySource` reads it. A wrong-key and a tampered `ENC2(...)` value each raise the explicit error naming its key, and a valid `ENC2(...)` value, an `ENC(...)` value and a plain value resolve to their plain text through the keyed path.
  - Converter failures, in `SecretReencryptorTest`: conversion and round-trip through the `ENC2` decoder; comments, continued and escaped properties, `ENC2(...)` and plain values stay byte-identical; a file without an `ENC(...)` value is not rewritten; a wrong key and each malformed value abort with the file unchanged, losing no data; a failed write leaves the original intact; `run` refuses a missing or extra argument and an absent `SECRET_KEY`, and the test NEVER calls `System.exit`.
  - Converter file and change checks, in `SecretReencryptorTest`: a symbolic link (where the file system supports links), a directory and a file with a second hard link are refused with the file unchanged. A hook between the read and the move that appends a property, that changes only the last-modified time, or that replaces the file under the same name makes the conversion abort, leaving the file exactly as the hook left it and no temporary file behind. `run` prints the offline prerequisite.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 235–405; Test: 867–1645; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 12–20
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes; `PassCoderTest`, `RefPropertySourceTest`, `SecretReencryptorTest`, `PropertySourcesLoaderTest` and `EncryptedPropertyResolutionTest` prove the default, secure, partially hardened and refused states, the key-precondition, contextual-error and converter-failure cases with the key-naming error on both property-source paths, the `ENC2` format, work-factor, malformed-value, salt and IV non-reuse and derived-key cache cases, and the converter's file-type, link and change-detection refusals; and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-07 entry with its offline-conversion step.

CRITICAL Directive: Add switched separate SAML encryption keys and a shorter certificate validity

- Finding: [SEC-23](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-23)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-323
- Severity: High 7.0
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `Migrator` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/Migrator.java:103-114`, startup path), `KeyPairCertUtils` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtils.java:39-57`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtils.java:78-85`), `SAMLAuthenticationSettings` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:26-27`, settings-save path `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:89-95`, revert `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:99-110`), `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:40-67`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:94-98`), `SamlSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:180-188`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; new tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/MigratorSamlKeysTest.java`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtilsTest.java`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettingsTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/saml/SamlKeyRegistrationTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - At `security.saml.separate-encryption-key=true`, each purpose MUST hold its own key and certificate pair: the signing pair in `security.saml.local-key` and `security.saml.local-certificate` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:26-27`) and a decryption pair in the new settings `security.saml.encryption.local-key` and `security.saml.encryption.local-certificate`. Their `local-key` suffix keeps the new private key inside the SEC-07 recommended `secret.encryption.name-suffixes` value.
  - At `true`, `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` MUST build the decryption credential from the decryption pair and NEVER from the signing pair (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:50-51`).
  - `KeyPairCertUtils` MUST add an overload that takes the validity in days from `security.saml.certificate-validity-days`, sets `notAfter` that many days after the generation instant, keeps `notBefore` two days before it, and raises an explicit error instead of returning `null` when generation fails. The no-argument `generateCertificate()` MUST stay unchanged: `notBefore` two days before generation and `notAfter` `10 * 365` days after it (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtils.java:50-57`), and `null` with a logged error on failure (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtils.java:78-85`).
  - In `saml` mode, a `security.saml.certificate-validity-days` value that is not a positive integer MUST fail startup and the settings save with an explicit error naming the property.
  - At `true`, the startup path (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/Migrator.java:107-114`) and the settings-save path (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:89-95`) MUST classify each purpose's pair through one shared check in `KeyPairCertUtils` before writing any setting, and act on its state:
    - both settings absent: generate a pair for that purpose only;
    - both settings present and decodable, with the private key matching the certificate's public key: once the cross-purpose check below passes, keep both values unchanged and NEVER regenerate them;
    - incomplete (one setting absent), mismatched (the private key does not match the certificate's public key) or undecodable (a value that is not a Base64 PKCS#8 RSA key or X.509 certificate): fail with an explicit error naming both settings of that purpose, and write and regenerate nothing;
    - generation failure: fail with an explicit error naming both settings of that purpose, and write nothing.
  - At `true`, after both pairs classify as complete and matching, or after a pair is generated for an absent one, and before the first `setProperty` and before the registration is built, the same shared check in `KeyPairCertUtils` MUST compare the RSA modulus (`RSAPublicKey.getModulus()`) of the decryption certificate's public key with the modulus of the signing certificate's public key. Equal moduli mean one key serves both purposes, whatever the certificates, public exponents or encodings, so a decryption pair copied from the signing pair and the signing key re-issued under another certificate are both caught. That duplicate cross-purpose state MUST fail startup, the settings save and the registration with an explicit error naming all four settings (`security.saml.local-key`, `security.saml.local-certificate`, `security.saml.encryption.local-key` and `security.saml.encryption.local-certificate`), MUST write and regenerate nothing, and MUST NEVER silently rotate or replace either pair, because rotation stays the operator's decision. A newly generated decryption pair MUST pass the same comparison before it is written. At `false` the comparison NEVER runs, so today's behavior stays.
  - Every error MUST name the settings and NEVER contain their values.
  - At `true`, a failed settings save MUST leave the stored settings unchanged, because the check runs before the first `setProperty`. `SAMLAuthenticationSettings.revert` MUST revert both settings of both pairs together (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:99-110`), so a revert never leaves an incomplete pair.
  - An installation that holds only today's signing pair MUST keep it byte-identical when the switch flips to `true`, and MUST gain only the decryption pair. Flipping back to `false` serves both purposes from the signing pair again and leaves the stored decryption pair unchanged.
  - At `true`, `LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository` MUST raise the explicit error naming the setting when a local key or certificate is absent or fails to decode, or its pair is mismatched, instead of logging the failure and returning no registration (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:40-67`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:94-98`). At `true` it MUST also raise the explicit error naming the four settings, instead of building a registration, when the decryption and signing certificates' public keys share an RSA modulus. An IdP metadata location that cannot be read keeps today's lazy retry.
  - At `false`, the pair rules MUST stay exactly as today: one pair serves both purposes, a missing key or certificate regenerates both members of the pair, a generation failure logs an error and writes nothing, and a decoding failure is logged and leaves no registration. At the defaults the certificate dates also stay as today: `notBefore` two days before generation and `notAfter` 3650 days after it, a 3652-day interval.
  - Validation: `security.saml.certificate-validity-days` is an `int` and MUST be 1 or above. `KeyPairCertUtils` MUST compute the expiry with `java.time` arithmetic and keep the two-day backdating of `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtils.java:50-52`, and a value whose expiry falls after `9999-12-31T23:59:59Z`, the latest date an X.509 certificate encodes, MUST be refused. In `saml` mode a refused value fails startup or the settings save with an error naming the property before any key is generated, so it NEVER produces a certificate or touches existing keys.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.saml.separate-encryption-key` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside the `security.saml.*` keys (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:231`); default `false`; recommended secure value `true`.
  - `security.saml.certificate-validity-days` in the same place; default `3650`; recommended secure value `730`. It sets the days from generation to `notAfter` for each newly generated certificate; `notBefore` stays two days before generation, and existing certificates keep their dates.
  - `WARN` owner: `SamlSecurityConfig` in `saml` mode logs one line while one key serves both purposes and one line while the validity exceeds 730 days, each naming SEC-23, its property and its secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the key switch publishes distinct signing and encryption certificates in the SP metadata, so each IdP imports the new metadata; at `true` an incomplete, mismatched or undecodable pair fails startup with an error naming its settings, so the operator restores the matching value or removes both settings of that pair before the flip; an installation that copied the signing pair into the encryption settings fails at `true` with the error naming the four settings, so before the flip the operator removes both encryption settings, which generates a fresh decryption pair, or supplies a distinct pair; a 730-day certificate needs rotation every two years.
- Tests: `KeyPairCertUtilsTest`, `MigratorSamlKeysTest`, `SAMLAuthenticationSettingsTest` and `SamlKeyRegistrationTest`, with locally generated key material, an in-memory `PropertiesHolder` and a local IdP metadata file, NEVER a remote metadata location. Each certificate date MUST be asserted against the generation instant, `notBefore` two days before it and `notAfter` the configured days after it, and NEVER as a 3650-day or 730-day interval between `notBefore` and `notAfter`.
  - Default: one generated pair serves both purposes with `notAfter` 3650 days after generation; the no-argument `generateCertificate()` keeps its dates and its `null` result on failure; a missing certificate regenerates both members and a generation failure writes nothing, as today; at `false` a stored decryption pair equal to the signing pair is not checked and startup proceeds as today.
  - Secure: both pairs are generated, each with `notAfter` 730 days after generation; a complete, matching pair is kept unchanged on startup and on settings save; an installation holding only the signing pair keeps it byte-identical and gains a decryption pair; each incomplete, mismatched or undecodable pair and a forced generation failure raise the explicit error naming its settings and leave the stored settings unchanged; `revert` reverts both settings of both pairs; the registration's decryption credential comes from the decryption pair; a decryption pair copied from the signing pair, and the signing key re-issued under a different locally generated certificate, each raise the explicit error naming the four settings on startup (`MigratorSamlKeysTest`), on settings save (`SAMLAuthenticationSettingsTest`, with the stored settings unchanged) and at registration (`SamlKeyRegistrationTest`), and neither is regenerated; `KeyPairCertUtilsTest` classifies both as the duplicate cross-purpose state; a distinct existing decryption pair is kept unchanged; a generated decryption pair's modulus differs from the signing pair's; a validity that is not a positive integer is refused naming the property.
  - Partially hardened: the separate key on with validity 3650 asserts the remaining `WARN`, and the SP metadata lists the signing and the encryption certificate.
  - Refused with the property-naming error before any key is generated: `0`, `-1`, a blank or non-integer value and a validity whose expiry falls after `9999-12-31`; `1` is accepted.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 130–220; Test: 320–540; Configuration and build: 14–24; Documentation: 10–18
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, the four tests prove the default, secure, partially hardened and refused states, the duplicate cross-purpose state and every pair state above, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-23 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switched maximum lifetime for personal access tokens

- Finding: [SEC-09](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-09)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-613
- Severity: Medium 6.8
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `PatGeneratorServiceImpl.generateToken` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatGeneratorServiceImpl.java:61-86`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatGeneratorServiceImplTest.java` and new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/PatLifetimeOpenApiTest.java`, which extends new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioMockMvcTest.java` (shared, counted in SEC-04; created here, with the content the SEC-04 `Tests:` give it, when absent because SEC-04 did not apply); `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - At a positive `security.pat.max-lifetime-days`, token creation MUST refuse a missing `expiresAt` and an `expiresAt` beyond that many days after creation, and the REST API MUST answer such a request with a stable 400 response.
  - At the default (`0`) an absent expiry MUST stay accepted, exactly as today, and the existing past-expiry check (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatGeneratorServiceImpl.java:66-68`) MUST stay unchanged.
  - Existing tokens MUST keep their stored expiry.
  - Validation: `security.pat.max-lifetime-days` is an `int` and MUST be 0 or above. `PatGeneratorServiceImpl` MUST refuse a negative, blank, non-integer or out-of-`int`-range value at startup. `0` keeps expiry optional and logs its `WARN`, a value above 365 stays valid and logs its `WARN`, and the latest permitted expiry is the creation instant plus that many days, computed with `java.time` arithmetic.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.pat.max-lifetime-days` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `0` (0 means expiry optional); recommended secure value `90`.
  - `WARN` owner: `PatGeneratorServiceImpl` logs one line while the value is `0` and one line while it is above 365, each naming SEC-09, the property and `90`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value refuses new tokens without an expiry or with an expiry beyond 90 days; automation renews its tokens before they expire.
- Tests: `PatGeneratorServiceImplTest` and `PatLifetimeOpenApiTest`. `PatLifetimeOpenApiTest` (MockMvc) extends `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest`, which SEC-04 carries and this directive creates when absent, and NEVER `AbstractStudioOpenApiTest`, whose inherited `generatesOpenApiDocument` would load `functionality/openapi.json`, which does not exist (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioOpenApiTest.java:37`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioOpenApiTest.java:43-53`); its context registers `PersonalAccessTokenController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/users/rest/controller/PersonalAccessTokenController.java`) and `ApiExceptionControllerAdvice` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/common/ApiExceptionControllerAdvice.java`) over the switched generator service and mocked collaborators. Default: a PAT without `expiresAt` is created. Secure: a PAT without `expiresAt`, or with an expiry beyond 90 days, is refused with 400, and one within 90 days is created. The `WARN` is asserted at `0` and above 365. `-1`, a blank and a non-integer value fail bean construction with the property-naming error; an `expiresAt` exactly 90 days after creation is created and one a second later is refused. An unset property behaves as `0`.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 40–70; Test: 168–317; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, the two tests prove both states, the refused values, the 90-day boundary and the `WARN` lines, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-09 entry.
