`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/model/constraint/ADConnectionConstraintValidator.java:16-50`, which returns `true` without a check when the credentials are absent at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/model/constraint/ADConnectionConstraintValidator.java:17-21`, and otherwise builds a provider from the submitted URL and binds with the administrator-entered credentials at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/model/constraint/ADConnectionConstraintValidator.java:23-31`), created through the bean factory by `SpringConstraintValidatorFactory` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/config/ValidationConfiguration.java:39`), `ADAuthenticationSettings` (`store` `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/ADAuthenticationSettings.java:66-73`, whose `super.store` at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/ADAuthenticationSettings.java:68` makes the first `setProperty` at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/AuthenticationSettings.java:62`, before the submitted URL is written at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/ADAuthenticationSettings.java:70`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:274`; new tests `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/AdSecurityConfigTest.java`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/settings/model/constraint/ADConnectionConstraintValidatorTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/ADAuthenticationSettingsTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - `security.ad.server-url` reaches JNDI unchanged as `java.naming.provider.url` in both binds, and JNDI reads it as a list of LDAP URLs separated by spaces and fails over to each entry in turn. The check MUST therefore split the value on runs of spaces, as JNDI does, and judge every entry, NEVER only the start of the value.
  - At `security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap=false`, AD-mode startup MUST fail with an explicit error naming `security.ad.server-url` and the property unless the value holds at least one entry and every entry is an LDAP URL with the `ldaps` scheme, compared case-insensitively, and a host. This refuses an `ldap://` entry in any position and any letter case, an entry with another scheme, and an entry without a scheme or without a host.
  - Shared check: one `public static boolean checkServerUrl(String serverUrl, String allowPlaintextLdap)` in `AdSecurityConfig` MUST hold the switch parsing, the URL-list rule above and its explicit error, and AD-mode startup, the AD settings connection check and the AD settings save MUST decide only through it, so all three apply one rule. The switch value is `true` or `false`, compared case-insensitively after trimming, an unset property is `true`, and any other value, blank included, MUST fail with an explicit error naming the property, the rejected value and the accepted values. At `true` the check judges no URL and returns `false`; at `false` it throws the URL-list error on a refusal and otherwise returns `true`, and the caller then MUST require TLS. Its errors MUST NEVER contain the URL value, because the connection check passes the error to `buildConstraintViolationWithTemplate` as a message template and the submitted URL is request input. The check MUST be public and static, because `AdSecurityConfig` is a bean only in `ad` mode (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdSecurityConfig.java:21`) while OpenL Studio checks and saves the AD settings in whichever mode is active, from the packages `org.openl.studio.settings.model.constraint` and `org.openl.rules.webstudio.web.admin.security`.
  - Construction point: `AdSecurityConfig` MUST check the URL list through `checkServerUrl`, log the `WARN`, and build and configure the provider in one package-private static method `activeDirectoryProvider`, which takes the domain, server URL, search filter, `security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap` value and mapper, and returns the `ActiveDirectoryLdapAuthenticationProvider` itself, NEVER a wrapper of it. `adAuthenticationProvider` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdSecurityConfig.java:33-46`) MUST obtain its provider only from that method and keep its own return: that provider, or that provider wrapped in `LockoutAuthenticationProvider` where SEC-13 is applied. The method MUST NEVER be a bean, because Spring Security 6.5.11 (`pom.xml:100`) builds the global `ProviderManager` behind the form-login and HTTP Basic chains from the mode's `AuthenticationProvider` bean only while it is the single one (`InitializeAuthenticationProviderBeanManagerConfigurer`), and `CommonAuthenticationConfig.authenticationManager` collects every such bean (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:37-45`), so a second, unwrapped one would let a login bypass the lockout wrapper.
  - At `false`, both JNDI environments MUST set `java.naming.security.protocol` to `ssl`: the provider's through `setContextEnvironmentProperties` in `activeDirectoryProvider`, and the mapper's in `bindAsUser`. JNDI then opens every connection over TLS, a referral the mapper follows included, so a referral to an `ldap://` server fails instead of carrying the bind password in plain text.
  - AD settings connection check: `ADConnectionConstraintValidator` MUST receive the `Environment` through its only public constructor, as `SidExistsValidator` receives its services (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/validation/SidExistsValidator.java:13-15`), because `SpringConstraintValidatorFactory` creates every constraint validator of OpenL Studio through the bean factory. When credentials are present, it MUST pass the submitted `serverUrl` and the `security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap` value of that `Environment` to `checkServerUrl` before it constructs `ActiveDirectoryLdapAuthenticationProvider`, and a refusal MUST become a constraint violation whose message is the check's error, with no provider constructed and no bind. When the check returns `true`, the validator MUST set `java.naming.security.protocol` to `ssl` on its provider through `setContextEnvironmentProperties` before `authenticate`, so the administrator-entered credentials travel only over TLS. When it returns `false`, the validator MUST behave exactly as today and NEVER call `setContextEnvironmentProperties`, and without credentials it keeps returning `true`.
  - AD settings save: `ADAuthenticationSettings.store` MUST pass the submitted `serverUrl` and `properties.getProperty("security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap")` to `checkServerUrl` before `super.store` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/ADAuthenticationSettings.java:68`), whose chain makes the first `setProperty`, and MUST let its error fail the save, so the stored settings stay unchanged and a save NEVER commits a URL that the next `ad`-mode start refuses. This also covers a save without credentials, which the connection check skips. At `true` the save stores the settings exactly as today.
  - `ldaps://` URLs MUST keep working in any letter case, and the default (`true`) MUST behave exactly as today: the value reaches both binds unchanged, no entry is refused, no `java.naming.security.protocol` entry is added, `activeDirectoryProvider` makes no `setContextEnvironmentProperties` call, because that setter refuses an empty map, and the mapper keeps `java.naming.referral=follow`.
  - The default `security.ad.server-url` value MUST stay unchanged.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `security.ad.server-url` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:274`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `WARN` owner: `AdSecurityConfig` logs one line while any entry of `security.ad.server-url` uses the `ldap` scheme, compared case-insensitively, and the value is `true`, naming SEC-17, the property and `false`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value refuses AD-mode startup, the AD settings connection check and the AD settings save unless every entry of `security.ad.server-url` is an `ldaps://` URL, and every directory connection OpenL Studio opens, the connection check and followed referrals included, uses TLS, so a referral to a server without LDAPS fails and group lookup falls back to the groups the provider returns; operators serve LDAP over `ldaps://` with a trusted certificate, set `security.ad.server-url` to the directory's `ldaps://` endpoints and add that certificate's issuing CA to the truststore of the JVM that runs OpenL Studio.
- Tests: `AdSecurityConfigTest`, with no remote connection, drives every startup state below through `AdSecurityConfig.activeDirectoryProvider` and NEVER through the `adAuthenticationProvider` bean method, whose parameters and return type SEC-13 changes, so it passes whether or not SEC-13 is applied. It reads the provider's JNDI environment from the returned provider with `ReflectionTestUtils.getField(provider, "contextEnvironmentProperties")`, the map that `setContextEnvironmentProperties` replaces, because the final provider class has no getter; the module already reads private fields this way (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/HealthCheckServletTest.java:70`). Default: an `ldap://` URL, and a list of an `ldaps://` entry followed by an `ldap://` entry, each start AD mode, log the `WARN` and reach both JNDI environments unchanged with no `java.naming.security.protocol` entry. Secure: an `ldap://` URL fails startup with the explicit error, and so do that mixed list, an `LDAP://` entry, an entry with another scheme, an entry without a scheme and an entry without a host; a mocked single `ldaps://` URL, a list of two `ldaps://` URLs and an `LDAPS://` URL succeed, and the provider's and the mapper's JNDI environments carry `java.naming.security.protocol=ssl`. Refused values: a blank and a `yes` value of `security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap` fail `activeDirectoryProvider` with the error naming the property, the rejected value and the accepted values. `ADConnectionConstraintValidatorTest` (new) in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/settings/model/constraint/` builds the validator with a `MockEnvironment`, a mocked `ConstraintValidatorContext` and Mockito `mockConstruction` of `ActiveDirectoryLdapAuthenticationProvider`, as `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/repository/upload/ProjectUploaderTest.java:40` mocks a constructed collaborator, so no test opens a connection: at `false` an `ldap://` URL with credentials is refused with a constraint violation carrying the error naming `security.ad.server-url` and `security.ad.allow-plaintext-ldap`, and no provider is constructed and no `authenticate` call is made; at `false` an `ldaps://` URL is accepted, and its one constructed provider receives `setContextEnvironmentProperties` with `java.naming.security.protocol=ssl` before `authenticate`; at `true`, and with the property unset, an `ldap://` URL is accepted as today, with one `authenticate` call and no `setContextEnvironmentProperties` call. `ADAuthenticationSettingsTest` (new) in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/` stores complete AD settings into an `InMemoryProperties` over a `MockEnvironment`: at `false` an `ldap://` URL fails `store` with the same error and leaves `getConfig()` empty, and an `ldaps://` URL is stored; at `true`, and with the property unset, an `ldap://` URL is stored.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 50–80; Test: 244–382; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `AdSecurityConfigTest` proves both states, the mixed-list, letter-case, other-scheme and malformed-entry refusals, the refused switch values, the `ssl` protocol in both JNDI environments and the `WARN` without a remote connection, `ADConnectionConstraintValidatorTest` proves that at `false` an `ldap://` connection check is refused with the error, no provider constructed and no bind, and an `ldaps://` check reaches `authenticate` only after `java.naming.security.protocol=ssl` is set on its provider, and that at `true` and unset an `ldap://` check behaves as today, `ADAuthenticationSettingsTest` proves that at `false` an `ldap://` save fails with the `InMemoryProperties` unchanged and an `ldaps://` save is stored, and that at `true` and unset an `ldap://` save is stored, no test opens a connection, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-17 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Keep PAT authentication request-scoped on the SAML and OAuth2 REST chains

- Finding: [SEC-29](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-29)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-613
- Severity: Medium 6.8
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: the `securityContextPersistenceFilter` bean of `CommonAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:32-35`, default `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository`, read only), the `restEndpointsFilterChain` beans of `SamlSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:105-118`, read only) and `OAuth2SecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:88-103`, read only), `PatAuthenticationFilter` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:63-100`, pass-through without a PAT at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:68-72`, context set at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:93-97`), `PatAuthenticationToken` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/model/PatAuthenticationToken.java:15-19`), `PatValidationServiceImpl` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatValidationServiceImpl.java:88-105`, revocation and expiry, read only), `PatAuthServiceImpl` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatAuthServiceImpl.java:63-70`, account checks, read only), `AdminPrivilege` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdminPrivilege.java:31-34`, read only), `NotPatAuth` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/NotPatAuth.java:31`, read only), `AuthorizationExpressions` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AuthorizationExpressions.java:24-26`, read only), `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-34`, `multi` and `ad` REST chain, read only); tests new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatSessionPersistenceTest.java`; the SEC-04 security test context, new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioMockMvcTest.java` and new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityChainTestConfiguration.java` with its write-recording test controller (shared, counted in SEC-04; created here, with the content the SEC-04 `Tests:` give them, every mode context included, when absent because SEC-04 did not apply).
- Required fix:
  - A request that `PatAuthenticationFilter` authenticates on the `saml` and `oauth2` REST chains MUST NEVER create an HTTP session and MUST NEVER create or update the security context stored in an existing session.
  - `PatAuthenticationToken` MUST carry Spring Security's `org.springframework.security.core.Transient` annotation, the marker `JwtAuthenticationToken` and `BearerTokenAuthentication` carry in Spring Security 6.5.11, so the `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository` behind the shared `securityContextPersistenceFilter` neither saves it nor creates a session for it.
  - The shared `securityContextPersistenceFilter` MUST stay the first filter of both REST chains, so a genuine SAML or OIDC session keeps authenticating REST requests exactly as today.
  - A session-loaded security context whose authentication is a `PatAuthenticationToken`, as the unfixed chains store it, MUST NEVER authenticate a request: `PatAuthenticationFilter` MUST clear that context before it reads the request's PAT, so a request without a valid PAT is refused with 401 and a request with a valid PAT runs with the authentication resolved from that PAT.
  - Every request that a PAT authenticates MUST pass the revocation and expiry checks of `PatValidationServiceImpl` and the account checks of `PatAuthServiceImpl`, and their result MUST NEVER be kept beyond that request.
  - Within a request that `PatAuthenticationFilter` authenticates, the `PatAuthenticationToken` MUST stay the request's authentication, so `@AdminPrivilege` and `@NotPatAuth` keep refusing it through `AuthorizationExpressions.isNotPat`.
  - The `multi` and `ad` REST chain and every non-REST chain MUST keep their current context handling.
  - When the SEC-04 directive's change is present, `PatAuthenticationFilter` MUST keep storing the `PatAuthenticationToken` it installs as a request attribute under the constant name that directive declares on the class, on every branch that installs one, including the branch that installs the PAT's authentication after the filter clears a session-loaded PAT context, and NEVER on a branch that installs none, as that directive requires. The REST chains' `CsrfFilter` exempts a PAT write only while that attribute holds the same authentication instance, compared by identity, that the `SecurityContextHolder` holds at the token check, so an install without the attribute refuses a valid PAT write with 403 in the secure state. This directive MUST NEVER change that attribute's name, that comparison or the `CsrfFilter` matcher that makes it.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: new `PatSessionPersistenceTest` (MockMvc) extends `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest` on the shared `SecurityChainTestConfiguration` context, which SEC-04 carries and this directive creates when absent, once with `user.mode=saml` and once with `user.mode=oauth2`, each with `security.csrf.enabled=false`, its default, set through `@TestPropertySource`, so every request passes the production `filterChainProxy`, that mode's REST chain, the real `PatAuthenticationFilter` over the context's Mockito `PatAuthService` and the production method security that the mode context registers through `MethodSecurityConfig` and `AuthorizationExpressions`, and no CSRF token check decides an outcome. It adds no controller or configuration to that context beyond the content the SEC-04 `Tests:` give it: every request goes to the context's write-recording test controller, whose PAT-permitted REST handler is the plain REST handler and whose `@AdminPrivilege` handler is the PAT-denied one. A session is a `MockHttpSession`, and a stored context sits under `HttpSessionSecurityContextRepository.SPRING_SECURITY_CONTEXT_KEY`. On each chain: a valid PAT request is served, with the PAT user recorded by the PAT-permitted handler, and leaves no session, and with a supplied empty session or another user's SSO session it writes no context and leaves the stored one unchanged; a follow-up request with the session that PAT request used and no PAT is refused with 401; a session from an earlier PAT request, also when it holds the stored `PatAuthenticationToken` context the unfixed chains write, whose PAT is then revoked or expired, so the mocked `PatAuthService` answers `PatAuthResolution.invalid()`, is refused with 401 without the PAT, which also removes any stored context, and with that PAT; a session holding a genuine `Saml2Authentication` or `OAuth2AuthenticationToken` context is served, with the SSO user recorded by the PAT-permitted handler; a valid PAT request of a user holding `ADMIN` to the `@AdminPrivilege` handler is refused with 403 and the controller records no write.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 8–20; Test: 261–386; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `PatSessionPersistenceTest` proves on both the `saml` and `oauth2` REST chains that a PAT request writes no session context, that a session-only follow-up and a stored PAT context with a revoked or expired PAT are refused with 401, that a genuine SSO session is served, and that the `@AdminPrivilege` handler still refuses a PAT.

CRITICAL Directive: Map local OpenL privileges on every opaque-token request instead of caching them with the userinfo claims

- Finding: [SEC-34](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-34)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-863
- Severity: Medium 6.8
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: `UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.introspect` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:63-82`, its cache value `UserCacheValue` at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:91-100`, and the userinfo service and delegate it builds itself at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:39` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:52-55`), `UserInfoClaimsConverter.convert` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoClaimsConverter.java:36-59`, user synchronization and `privilegeMapper` call at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoClaimsConverter.java:55-58`); read only: the `opaqueTokenIntrospector` bean of `OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration.java:22-36`), the `userInfoOAuth2Cache` configuration (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/cache2k.xml:28-30`), the `oauth2` privilege mapper `GetUserPrivileges` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivileges.java:27-56`) and the grant changes it reads, `GroupManagementService.updateGroup` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/GroupManagementService.java:65-69`), `GroupManagementService.deleteGroup` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/GroupManagementService.java:71-74`) and `UserManagementService.updateAuthorities` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/UserManagementService.java:192-219`); new test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospectorTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - The `userInfoOAuth2Cache` entry MUST hold only the remote identity, which is the userinfo attributes, the username and the external authorities read from the groups claim, and NEVER the result of `privilegeMapper`.
  - Every `introspect` call MUST compute the OpenL privileges by calling `privilegeMapper` once with the cached username and external authorities, so a removed group privilege, a removed group membership or a deleted group takes effect on the next request, as the JWT access-token path does at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OpenLJwtGrantedAuthoritiesConverter.java:24-27`.
  - Revocation MUST NEVER depend on evicting `userInfoOAuth2Cache`, because grants change through several `UserManagementService` and `GroupManagementService` methods and every one would need its own eviction. The cache configuration, the token-hash key and the public constructor that `OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration` calls stay unchanged.
  - Every `introspect` call MUST call the delegate introspector before it reads the cache, as today, and a token the delegate refuses MUST be refused with no userinfo call and no principal returned, even while its entry is cached.
  - The userinfo endpoint call and `syncUserData` MUST run only when the entry is absent, as today, and NEVER on a cache hit.
  - For unchanged grants the returned principal MUST equal today's: the same authorities, attributes and name attribute (`security.oauth2.attribute.username`).
  - A package-private constructor MUST accept the delegate `OpaqueTokenIntrospector` and the `OAuth2UserService`, and the public constructor MUST pass it today's `SpringOpaqueTokenIntrospector` and `DefaultOAuth2UserService`, so the test runs on mocks and contacts no remote host.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: `UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospectorTest` (new) in the `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio` test tree, with a mocked delegate `OpaqueTokenIntrospector`, a mocked `OAuth2UserService`, mocked `UserManagementService` and `GroupManagementService` behind a real `GetUserPrivileges`, and a `ConcurrentMapCache`. Grant removal: after a first `introspect` call, the mocked group loses `ADMIN`, or the mocked user loses the group membership, and a second call with the same token, before any expiry, returns authorities without it. Introspection: the delegate is called on every call, and a token it refuses with `BadOpaqueTokenException` is refused while its entry is cached, with no userinfo call. Unchanged grants: two calls return equal authorities, attributes and name. Claims caching: the mocked `OAuth2UserService` and `syncUserData` are called once while the entry is cached.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 25–50; Test: 140–216; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospectorTest` proves that a removed group `ADMIN` privilege and a removed group membership take effect on the next request before cache expiry, that every request is introspected and an inactive token is refused, that unchanged grants return today's principal, and that userinfo is loaded once per cached token.

CRITICAL Directive: Add switched security headers and a Content Security Policy to every OpenL Studio chain

- Finding: [SEC-05](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-05)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-1021
- Severity: Medium 6.5
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `SecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityConfig.java:30-58`, headers disabled at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityConfig.java:56`), `SamlSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:53-134`), `OAuth2SecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:52-117`), the `single` chain of `SingleSecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SingleSecurityConfig.java:24-32`), the `multi` and `ad` REST and default chains of `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-33`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:39-49`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; new test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityHeadersTest.java`; the SEC-04 security test context, new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioMockMvcTest.java` and new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityChainTestConfiguration.java` with its write-recording test controller (shared, counted in SEC-04; created here, with the content the SEC-04 `Tests:` give them, every mode context included, when absent because SEC-04 did not apply); `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - At `security.http-headers.all-chains.enabled=true`, the static chain MUST stop disabling headers (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityConfig.java:56`), and the SAML and OIDC chains MUST write Spring Security's default security headers, through a header writer added explicitly where a chain is assembled by hand.
  - At a non-empty `security.http-headers.content-security-policy`, every OpenL Studio chain MUST send a `Content-Security-Policy` header with that value. That includes the `HttpSecurity` chains of `SingleSecurityConfig` and `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig`, which MUST add the policy to their existing header configuration and keep Spring Security's default headers in both states.
  - At the defaults the SAML, OIDC and static chains MUST send no security headers and no chain sends a policy, exactly as today, so current embedding keeps working.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.http-headers.all-chains.enabled` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `false`; recommended secure value `true`.
  - `security.http-headers.content-security-policy` in the same file; default empty; recommended secure value `default-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'self'`.
  - `WARN` owner: `SecurityConfig` logs one line while headers are off on any chain and one line while the policy is empty, each naming SEC-05, its property and its secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the values adds Spring Security's default headers, frame and content-type headers included, to SSO and static responses, and adds the policy to the responses of every OpenL Studio chain. The policy blocks framing by other origins and loading of off-site scripts, and it also blocks OpenL Studio's own UI: the inline script at `STUDIO/studio-ui/index.html:13-25`, the inline style attributes at `STUDIO/studio-ui/index.html:29` and `STUDIO/studio-ui/api-docs.html:13`, and the style elements that antd 6.6.5 (through `@ant-design/cssinjs` 2.1.2) and antd-style 4.1.0 (`STUDIO/studio-ui/package.json:22-23`) inject at runtime, because the UI configures no CSP nonce. Operators MUST extend the policy before they set it and MUST verify the OpenL Studio UI under the extended policy: `script-src 'self'` plus the SHA-256 hash source of that static inline script; `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, because a fixed header value carries no per-response nonce and the injected styles change at runtime; and, for integrations that embed OpenL Studio, `frame-ancestors 'self'` plus each embedding origin.
- Tests: `SecurityHeadersTest`, MockMvc extending `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest` on the shared `SecurityChainTestConfiguration` context, which SEC-04 carries and this directive creates when absent, with `user.mode` and both switch values set through `@TestPropertySource`, so each of the `single`, `multi`, `ad`, `saml` and `oauth2` modes runs at the default and at the secure values and every response comes from the production `filterChainProxy`. Default: SAML, OIDC and static chain responses carry no security headers, and `single`, `multi` and `ad` chain responses carry Spring Security's default headers without a policy. Secure: SAML, OIDC, static, `single`, `multi` and `ad` chain responses carry the default headers and the policy. Partially hardened: headers on with an empty policy asserts the remaining `WARN`.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 70–120; Test: 252–324; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `SecurityHeadersTest` proves the default, secure and partially hardened states, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-05 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switched allow-list for Kafka reply and dead-letter topic headers

- Finding: [SEC-38](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-38)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-610
- Severity: Medium 5.9
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `KafkaService.getOutTopic` and `KafkaService.getDltTopic` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:152-170`), the `KafkaService` client fields (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:73-75`), `KafkaService.createService` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:84-108`) and the `KafkaService` constructor (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:110-146`), `KafkaRuleServicePublisher`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`; new test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaTopicRoutingTest.java`, which creates the Kafka startup fixture that SEC-39 and SEC-40 reuse; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - A reply-topic or dead-letter-topic header MUST be honoured only when its topic is in `ruleservice.kafka.allowed-reply-topics`. The value `*` honours every header, exactly as today; an empty list permits only the configured output and dead-letter topics.
  - A refused header MUST be ignored, and the reply MUST go to the configured output or dead-letter topic.
  - Grammar: `ruleservice.kafka.allowed-reply-topics` is a comma-separated list, each element trimmed of surrounding whitespace. `*` alone keeps today's behavior, and a value that is empty after trimming is the empty list. In any other value each element MUST be an exact, case-sensitive topic name that is valid under the rule `kafka-clients` 4.3.1 (`pom.xml:118`) enforces in `org.apache.kafka.common.internals.Topic.validate`: 1 to 249 characters from `[a-zA-Z0-9._-]`, and neither `.` nor `..`. A repeated name counts once.
  - Validation: `KafkaRuleServicePublisher` MUST parse the value before the first Kafka-published service builds a consumer or producer, and MUST refuse an empty element (`orders,,replies` or a trailing comma), an invalid topic name (`replies topic`, or a name of 250 characters) and `*` combined with any name (`*,replies`) with an error naming the property and the rejected element, so that deployment fails and no consumer subscribes.
  - Headers: outside `*`, each reply-topic and dead-letter-topic header value MUST be decoded as strict UTF-8, where malformed bytes are an error and are NEVER replaced, and compared exactly, with no trimming or case folding, with the effective allow-set: the parsed names of a non-empty list, or the configured output and dead-letter topics for the empty list. A configured topic that a non-empty list omits is outside its allow-set. An absent header or a header whose value is null counts as absent, as `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:153-157` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:161-169` check today. An empty value, malformed bytes, an invalid topic name or a name outside the allow-set refuses that header, which then also counts as absent in the existing order of `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:160-170`. At `*` header decoding and routing stay exactly as today.
  - Test seam: `KafkaService.createService`, the `KafkaService` constructor and its client fields MUST take the Kafka `Consumer` and `Producer` interfaces in place of `KafkaConsumer` and `KafkaProducer`, which already implement them, so tests drive the kafka-clients `MockConsumer` and `MockProducer` with no broker. It is a test seam that MUST NOT change runtime behavior: `KafkaRuleServicePublisher` passes the clients it builds today, and SEC-39 and SEC-40 reuse the seam unchanged.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `ruleservice.kafka.allowed-reply-topics` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside the `ruleservice.kafka.*` keys (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:26-27`); default `*`; recommended secure value an explicit topic list, where empty means the configured output and dead-letter topics only.
  - `WARN` owner: `KafkaRuleServicePublisher` logs one line when a Kafka-published service starts while the value is `*`, naming SEC-38, the property and the secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value stops replies to topics outside the list; clients that name their own reply topics get them listed.
- Tests: `KafkaTopicRoutingTest`, with mocked records and no broker, which creates the Kafka startup fixture that SEC-39 and SEC-40 reuse: the kafka-clients `MockConsumer` and `MockProducer` passed through the `createService` seam, a mocked `Environment` for the `KafkaRuleServicePublisher` startup path and log capture for its startup `WARN`. Default: a record whose reply-topic header names an unlisted topic is answered on that topic. Secure: that header is ignored and the reply goes to the configured topic, while a listed topic still works. The `WARN` is asserted at the default. Refused at deployment with the property-naming error, with mocked services and no broker: `orders,,replies`, `replies,`, `replies topic`, a 250-character name and `*,replies`; `replies , audit` lists exactly two topics, and a whitespace-only value permits only the configured topics. Outside `*`, a header with malformed UTF-8 bytes, an empty value, or a listed name with a trailing space or in another case is ignored, and the reply goes to the configured topic. With the list `replies`, a reply-topic header naming the configured dead-letter topic is ignored and the reply goes to the configured output topic. An unset property behaves as `*`. In both states a record without the header, or with a header whose value is null, is answered on the configured output topic, and a dead-letter reply without a dead-letter header falls back to an honoured reply-topic header and then to the configured dead-letter topic.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 56–96; Test: 156–264; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `KafkaTopicRoutingTest` proves both states, the refused values and headers, and the `WARN`, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-38 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add switched trusted-proxy lists for forwarded headers in both applications

- Finding: [SEC-16](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-16)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-348
- Severity: Medium 5.4
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: the `ForwardedFilter` declaration of `de.qaware.xff.filter.ForwardedHeaderFilter` from `org.openl:x-forwarded-filter` 2.0 (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:33-44`, `pom.xml:482-490`), `SpringInitializer` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java`), new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/TrustedForwardedHeaderFilter.java`, `RuleServicesFilter` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:69-70`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:99`), both `openl-default.properties` files named below; tests new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/TrustedForwardedHeaderFilterTest.java` and existing `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilterTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - OpenL Studio: `TrustedForwardedHeaderFilter` MUST take the place of the `ForwardedFilter` declaration at the same position in the filter order and delegate to the existing filter, with the same `xForwardedPrefixStrategy` `PREPEND`, only when the original peer address is in `security.forwarded-headers.trusted-proxies`; any other request passes through unwrapped.
  - OpenL Rule Services: `RuleServicesFilter.doFilter` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:99`) MUST apply its `ForwardedHeaderFilter` only when the original peer address is in `ruleservice.forwarded-headers.trusted-proxies`, and process every other request unwrapped.
  - Both checks MUST read the peer address before any forwarded header is applied. `*` trusts every peer, exactly as today.
  - `security.forwarded-headers.trusted-proxies` and `ruleservice.forwarded-headers.trusted-proxies` MUST share one list grammar: elements are separated by commas, and spaces around each element are trimmed. A value that is exactly `*` after trimming is the legacy sentinel and trusts every peer. A value that is empty or holds only spaces trusts no peer: every request passes through unwrapped and no `WARN` is logged.
  - Every other element MUST be a literal address: IPv4 as exactly four dot-separated decimal octets from 0 to 255 without leading zeros, or IPv6 in RFC 4291 text form (full, `::`-compressed, or with a dotted-quad IPv4 tail that follows the IPv4 rule). A host name, a CIDR range or prefix length, a port, square brackets, a zone index and an empty element (two adjacent commas, or a leading or trailing comma) are malformed.
  - The peer address and each element MUST be compared as binary addresses, NEVER as strings, so `::1` matches `0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1` and an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address such as `::ffff:127.0.0.1` matches `127.0.0.1`. An element or a peer address is NEVER resolved through DNS: a name-resolving lookup such as `InetAddress.getByName` NEVER receives a value that has not passed this grammar, and a peer address that fails the grammar is untrusted.
  - Each application MUST parse and validate its own property once at startup, before it serves the first request. A malformed element, or `*` combined with any other element, MUST fail startup with an explicit error naming the property and the rejected element; the application NEVER drops the element, NEVER falls back to `*` and NEVER starts with a partial list.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.forwarded-headers.trusted-proxies` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `*`; recommended secure value the proxy addresses, for example `127.0.0.1,::1`.
  - `ruleservice.forwarded-headers.trusted-proxies` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `*`; recommended secure value the proxy addresses, for example `127.0.0.1,::1`.
  - `WARN` owners: `SpringInitializer` (OpenL Studio) and `RuleServicesFilter` initialization (OpenL Rule Services) each log one line while their value is `*`, naming SEC-16, the property and the secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value ignores forwarded headers from unlisted peers, so generated links and client addresses behind an unlisted proxy fall back to the direct connection; operators list every proxy address as a literal IP address, because a host name or any other malformed entry fails startup with an error naming the property.
- Tests: `TrustedForwardedHeaderFilterTest` and `RuleServicesFilterTest`, with mocked requests. Default: `X-Forwarded-*` headers from any remote address are applied. Secure: they are applied only from listed proxies and ignored from other peers. Partially hardened, per application, because `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws` do not depend on each other: each application's test MUST assert that its own owner logs the SEC-16 `WARN` at `*` and logs none at a listed proxy value, and MUST NEVER set the other application's property, `TrustedForwardedHeaderFilterTest` for `SpringInitializer` and `RuleServicesFilterTest` for `RuleServicesFilter`. OpenL Studio hardened with OpenL Rule Services at `*` is proven by `TrustedForwardedHeaderFilterTest` at a listed proxy value logging no `WARN` together with `RuleServicesFilterTest` at `*` asserting the remaining `WARN`, and the reverse by `RuleServicesFilterTest` at a listed proxy value logging no `WARN` together with `TrustedForwardedHeaderFilterTest` at `*` asserting the remaining `WARN`. Each test also covers its own application's property with mocked requests and a mocked filter configuration in these states. Empty: forwarded headers are ignored from every peer and no `WARN` is logged. Canonical: a listed `::1` matches the peer `0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1`, a listed `127.0.0.1` matches the peer `::ffff:127.0.0.1`, and `127.0.0.1, ::1` with a space after the comma is accepted. Invalid input: `localhost`, `127.1`, `010.0.0.1`, `10.0.0.0/8`, `127.0.0.1:8080`, `[::1]`, `fe80::1%eth0`, `127.0.0.1,,::1`, a trailing comma (`127.0.0.1,`) and `*,127.0.0.1` each fail startup with the explicit error naming the property and the rejected element; the rejection of `localhost` proves that no name resolution happened.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 120–220; Test: 419–713; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, both tests prove the default, secure and partially hardened states, the partially hardened ones through that per-application pair, and, for their own property, the empty-list, canonical-match and invalid-input cases, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-16 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Refuse off-site targets in the post-login redirect

- Finding: [SEC-30](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-30)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-601
- Severity: Medium 5.4
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: the `authenticationSuccessHandler` bean of `CommonAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:71-75`, target parameter `from` at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:74`); test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfigTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - The success handler MUST follow the `from` value only when it normalizes to a same-origin relative path: a single leading `/`, no scheme, no authority and no backslash.
  - The success handler MUST refuse a decoded `from` value that holds any character below U+0021 (space, tab, CR, LF and every other C0 control character) or a percent-encoded `/` or `\` (`%2F` or `%5C`, in either letter case), because browsers strip tab, CR and LF from a `Location` value, so a `/` followed by them and a second `/` turns into a scheme-relative target, and `DefaultRedirectStrategy` only prepends the context path, which is empty at the root context path.
  - Any other value MUST fall back to the saved request or to the default target `/`, and same-site relative targets MUST keep working.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: `CommonAuthenticationConfigTest`. A same-site relative `from` value redirects there; an absolute URL naming another host, a scheme-relative value, a backslash-prefixed value, a value holding a tab, CR or LF after the leading `/`, a value holding a space or another character below U+0021, and a value holding `%2F` or `%5C` in either letter case each fall back to the default target; the saved-request fallback keeps working.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 30–50; Test: 109–188; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `CommonAuthenticationConfigTest` proves that every off-site form is refused, including a `from` value holding a control character, a space or another character below U+0021 and one holding a percent-encoded `/` or `\` (`%2F` or `%5C`, in either letter case), while same-site targets and the saved-request fallback keep working.

(`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:91-110`); unchanged read references: the static-content branch of `RuleServicesFilter` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:140-153`), which serves the bundled pages before its security check (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:155-173`), and those pages, which send no `Authorization` header: `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/index.html` reads `/admin/ui/info` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/index.html:327`) and the errors route (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/index.html:271`), and links the manifest, configuration and system-information routes (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/index.html:275`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/index.html:289`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/index.html:300`), and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/swagger-ui.html` reads `/admin/swagger-ui.json` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/static/swagger-ui.html:115`); both `openl-default.properties` files named below; tests new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/SystemInfoSecurityTest.java`, existing `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilterTest.java` and new `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidatorTest.java`, which SEC-01 adds and whose `MockEnvironment`, `@TempDir` JWKS and in-JVM signing fixture SEC-01 creates (shared, counted in SEC-01; created here, with the content the SEC-01 `Tests:` give it, when absent because SEC-01 did not apply); the SEC-04 security test context, new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/openapi/AbstractStudioMockMvcTest.java` and new `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityChainTestConfiguration.java` with its write-recording test controller (shared, counted in SEC-04; created here, with the content the SEC-04 `Tests:` give them, every mode context included, when absent because SEC-04 did not apply); `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - OpenL Studio: at `security.public-system-info.enabled=false`, `/rest/public/info/sys.json` and `/rest/public/info/http.json` MUST leave the static chain and require authentication; their paths stay unchanged.
  - OpenL Rule Services: at `ruleservice.admin.public-info.enabled=false` with authentication enabled, the only public `/admin/` paths MUST be the health checks under `/admin/healthcheck/`. Every other `/admin/` path MUST require a valid bearer token: each protected `AdminRestController` route above and any `/admin/` path without a route, so the protection denies by default and is NEVER an enumerated block list. `/admin/healthcheck/` stays public in both states.
  - At `false`, `RuleServicesFilter.skipAuthorization` MUST exempt only `/admin/healthcheck/`.
  - At `false`, `JWTValidator.authorize` MUST test whether the path info is under `/admin/` and outside `/admin/healthcheck/` right after the SEC-01 deployment check, before the `/admin/` exemption (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:83-85`) and before the OpenAPI suffix exemption (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:87-89`). Such a request MUST go straight to the bearer-token check (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:91-110`), so no protected `/admin/` path passes through either exemption, whatever suffix (`openapi.json`, `openapi.yaml`) it ends with.
  - At `false` both gates MUST classify the same `getPathInfo()` value against the same public set, so no `/admin/` path is public in one gate and protected in the other.
  - The OpenAPI suffix exemption MUST stay unchanged for every path outside `/admin/`, so the genuine service `openapi.json` and `openapi.yaml` documents stay public in both states.
  - `index.html` and `swagger-ui.html` MUST stay static content that `RuleServicesFilter` serves before its security check, and at `false` their data requests to the protected routes MUST pass the bearer-token check like every other protected request.
  - `JWTValidator` MUST read `ruleservice.admin.public-info.enabled` from its `Environment` in the constructor, and this change NEVER alters the `/admin/deploy` check that SEC-01 adds.
  - At the defaults (`true`) both applications MUST behave exactly as today.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.public-system-info.enabled` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `ruleservice.admin.public-info.enabled` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `ruleservice.authentication.enabled` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `WARN` owners: `SecurityConfig` logs one line while the OpenL Studio value is `true` outside `single` mode; `RuleServicesFilter` initialization logs one line while the OpenL Rule Services value is `true` with `ruleservice.authentication.enabled=true`. Each names SEC-11, its property and `false`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the values makes monitoring that reads the OpenL Studio information endpoints authenticate, and makes monitoring and clients that read the OpenL Rule Services information, service-list, error, manifest, Swagger UI service-list and configuration endpoints send a bearer token; the bundled `index.html` and `swagger-ui.html` pages add no token themselves, so they load that data only when a token is supplied to their requests; health checks stay anonymous.
- Tests: `SystemInfoSecurityTest` (MockMvc) extends `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest` on the shared `SecurityChainTestConfiguration` context, which SEC-04 carries and this directive creates when absent, in `multi` mode with `SysInfoController` registered, so every request passes the production `filterChainProxy`; it NEVER extends `AbstractStudioOpenApiTest`, whose inherited fixture test would load a `functionality/openapi.json` that does not exist. OpenL Studio default: `sys.json` and `http.json` answer anonymously. OpenL Studio secure: they require authentication, so an anonymous OpenL Studio request is refused with 401 and the existing session is served. `RuleServicesFilterTest` and `JWTValidatorTest` cover OpenL Rule Services, each with one parameterized test per state over the protected path set: each protected `AdminRestController` route above, the errors and `MANIFEST.MF` routes each with a single-segment and with a multi-segment deploy path, and an `/admin/` path ending in `openapi.json` and one ending in `openapi.yaml`. `JWTValidatorTest`, on the JWKS and signing fixture that SEC-01 creates and this directive creates when absent, driving `authorize` with mocked requests: at the default every protected path is authorized without a token; at `false` each protected path is refused without a token and with an invalid token, and authorized with a valid token; `/admin/healthcheck/readiness` and `/admin/healthcheck/startup` are authorized without a token in both states, and so are the genuine service `openapi.json` and `openapi.yaml` documents outside `/admin/`. `RuleServicesFilterTest`, driving the filter with mocked requests and a recording `AuthorizationChecker`: at the default the `/admin/info/` and `/admin/config/` paths skip the checker and every other protected path reaches it, as today; at `false` every protected path reaches the checker and a refusal goes to the access-denied handler; both health paths skip the checker in both states. Each state's protected path set is one parameterized test method, so the path set adds no test methods beyond those the SEC-11 LOC row sizes. Partially hardened, per application, because `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws` do not depend on each other: each application's test MUST assert that its own owner logs the SEC-11 `WARN` at the default and logs none at `false`, and MUST NEVER set the other application's property, `SystemInfoSecurityTest` in `multi` mode for `SecurityConfig` and `RuleServicesFilterTest` with `ruleservice.authentication.enabled=true` for `RuleServicesFilter`. OpenL Studio hardened with OpenL Rule Services at its default is proven by `SystemInfoSecurityTest` at `false` logging no `WARN` together with `RuleServicesFilterTest` at the default asserting the remaining `WARN`, and the reverse by `RuleServicesFilterTest` at `false` logging no `WARN` together with `SystemInfoSecurityTest` at the default asserting the remaining `WARN`.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 75–130; Test: 246–416; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `SystemInfoSecurityTest`, `RuleServicesFilterTest` and `JWTValidatorTest` together prove the default, secure and partially hardened states, the partially hardened ones through the per-application pair of `SystemInfoSecurityTest` and `RuleServicesFilterTest`, at `false` every protected OpenL Rule Services `/admin/` path is refused without a token by both gates while only the `/admin/healthcheck/` paths and the genuine service OpenAPI documents are served without one, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-11 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switched maximum of concurrent sessions per user

- Finding: [SEC-31](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-31)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-770
- Severity: Medium 5.3
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: the session management of `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.defaultFilterChain` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:45-47`, `maximumSessions(-1)`), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; new test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfigTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - `maximumSessions` MUST take its value from `security.max-sessions-per-user`; a negative value keeps unlimited sessions, exactly as today.
  - At a positive value a new login beyond the limit MUST expire the oldest session of that user and NEVER refuse the new login, and the `SessionRegistry` MUST stay consistent.
  - Validation: `security.max-sessions-per-user` is an `int`. Spring Security 6.5.11 (`pom.xml:100`) treats only `-1` as unlimited (`ConcurrentSessionControlAuthenticationStrategy.onAuthentication`) and refuses `0` without naming the property (`SessionLimit.of`), so every negative value MUST reach `maximumSessions` as `-1` and a positive value as itself. `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` MUST refuse `0` and a blank, non-integer or out-of-`int`-range value when OpenL Studio starts.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `security.max-sessions-per-user` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties`; default `-1` (negative means unlimited); recommended secure value `10`.
  - `WARN` owner: `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` logs one line while the value is negative, naming SEC-31, the property and `10`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value ends the oldest session of a user who signs in an eleventh time.
- Tests: `FormBasedAuthenticationConfigTest`. Default: an eleventh concurrent session is kept. Secure: the eleventh login expires the oldest session and the registry stays consistent. The `WARN` is asserted at the default. `-5` behaves as `-1`; `0`, a blank and a non-integer value fail startup with the property-naming error; at `1` the second login expires the first session.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 25–45; Test: 80–140; Configuration and build: 4–8; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `FormBasedAuthenticationConfigTest` proves both states, the refused values and the `WARN`, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-31 entry.
