CRITICAL Directive: Add switched payload storage and retention to the store-log database

- Finding: [SEC-12](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-12)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-359
- Severity: Medium 4.7
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: edit points `DBStoreLogDataService` (mapper field `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:37`, `setup` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:41-44`, `save` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:78-101`), `EntityManagerOperations` (factory map `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:51-52`, `getSessionFactory` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:73-92`, `onUndeploy` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:99-110`), `StoreLogDataMapper` (`MAPPING_ANNOTATIONS` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:53-62`, element collection `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:79-98`, `@Request` and `@Response` injection `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:131-134`, `processAnnotatedElement` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:240-255`), `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/resources/openl-default.properties:1`; read-only contract evidence: `getRequest` and `getResponse` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:140-180`), which read the RESTful payloads or the Kafka consumer, dead-letter and producer records, `getEntityManager` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:56-71`), which registers `@InjectEntityManager` entity classes in the factory map, `createEntities` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:103-119`), which instantiates every class listed by `StoreLogDataToDB.value` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db.annotation/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/annotation/StoreLogDataToDB.java:11`), `DefaultEntity` (entity name `openl_log_data` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DefaultEntity.java:23`, `@Id` `id` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DefaultEntity.java:25-30`, `@IncomingTime` `incomingTime` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DefaultEntity.java:32-35`, `@Lob` `request` and `response` `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DefaultEntity.java:42-52`), the custom entities `HelloEntity2` (`ITEST/itest.storelogdata/src/org/openl/itest/db/HelloEntity2.java:25-33`, a `ZonedDateTime` `@IncomingTime` field and one `Long` `@Id`) and `HelloEntity8` (`ITEST/itest.storelogdata/src/org/openl/itest/db/HelloEntity8.java:14-24`, no `@IncomingTime`); tests `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperationsTest.java` (existing), new `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataServiceTest.java`, existing `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/ws/logging/StoreLogDataMapperTest.java`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - Mapper exclusion: `StoreLogDataMapper` MUST gain a constructor that takes the mapping annotations to skip. During element collection (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:79-98`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:240-255`) an element, field or method, declared or inherited, that carries a skipped annotation MUST be left out of both collected lists, together with any `@Value` it also carries, so it receives no value: neither its value lookup (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:131-134`) nor any of its converters runs. The no-argument constructor skips nothing, so every other mapper user keeps today's mapping. The skip holds for every publisher type: for a skipped element `getRequest` and `getResponse` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:140-180`) NEVER run, so neither a RESTful payload nor a Kafka consumer, dead-letter or producer record value is read for it. The mapper NEVER alters or clears the `StoreLogData` it reads, so the Kafka records stay intact for every other mapping, `@KafkaMessageHeader` included.
  - Payload suppression: `DBStoreLogDataService.setup` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:41-44`) MUST build the mapper that replaces the field at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:37` from `ruleservice.store.logs.db.store-payloads`: at `false` it skips `@Request` and `@Response`, at `true` it skips nothing. No other mapper user changes. At `false`, for RESTful and Kafka records alike, every element mapped by `@Request` or `@Response` in `DefaultEntity` and in every custom entity class MUST receive no value and keep the entity's initial value (`null` in `DefaultEntity`), while every other column, `@Value` and `@KafkaMessageHeader` columns included, stays as today.
  - Retention contract, enforced only at a positive `ruleservice.store.logs.db.retention-days`: an entity class is purgeable only when its class hierarchy carries exactly one `@IncomingTime` element whose name (the field name, or the decapitalized property name of an annotated getter or setter) is a singular persistent attribute in the `SessionFactory` metamodel with Java type `ZonedDateTime`, `OffsetDateTime`, `Instant` or `java.util.Date`, and the entity type has a single `@Id` attribute. `DefaultEntity` meets it through `incomingTime` and `id`. `EntityManagerOperations` MUST resolve the attribute from the metamodel of the entity's factory.
  - Refusal: before the first entity of a record is saved, `DBStoreLogDataService.save` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:78-101`) MUST require the contract for every entity class of the record. An unmet contract MUST fail the save with a `StoreLogDataException` whose message names the entity class, the unmet requirement and `ruleservice.store.logs.db.retention-days`, through the same path a database save failure takes today (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:93-98`). After the record's entities are populated (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:90`) and before the first of them is saved, `save` MUST also read each entity's retention attribute value through the metamodel attribute's Java member (`Attribute.getJavaMember()`, a field or a getter), because the mapper injects `StoreLogData.getIncomingMessageTime()` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/StoreLogDataMapper.java:105-106`), which is null for a record without an incoming time, and an `@IncomingTime` converter can also return null. A null value MUST fail the save with a `StoreLogDataException` whose message names the entity class, the null retention attribute and `ruleservice.store.logs.db.retention-days`, through the same path, so no entity of that record is saved. The value is NEVER replaced by a substituted time: the record is refused, never backfilled. Together the two checks ensure a record the purge cannot delete is NEVER stored through `save`. At `0` no contract or value check runs, so every record is saved exactly as today.
  - Cutoff: each run MUST compute the cutoff once, as the current instant of an injected `java.time.Clock` (`Clock.systemUTC()` in production) minus `retention-days` days. A record is deleted only when its retention attribute is non-null and strictly before the cutoff, converted to the attribute type (`ZonedDateTime` and `OffsetDateTime` at UTC, `Instant` unchanged, `java.util.Date` through `Date.from`). A record whose value is at or after the cutoff stays, and a record whose retention attribute is null is NEVER selected or deleted; at a positive retention the refusal keeps `save` from storing a null value, so only a record stored before retention became positive, or written outside `save` through an `@InjectEntityManager` entity manager, carries one. Queries MUST use the JPA Criteria API on the metamodel attributes and NEVER a query string built from the class name, because the entity name differs from it (`openl_log_data`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DefaultEntity.java:23`).
  - Scheduler home: `DBStoreLogDataService.setup` MUST own the purge. A negative `retention-days` fails startup with an explicit error naming the property. `0` creates no executor and schedules nothing, as today. A positive value creates one single-thread `ScheduledExecutorService` on a daemon thread named `openl-store-log-retention`, injectable for tests, and registers the purge once with `scheduleWithFixedDelay`: the first run 1 minute after startup, each later run 60 minutes after the previous run ends, so runs NEVER overlap. Both delays are fixed constants and NEVER read from a property, because the switch adds only `store-payloads` and `retention-days`.
  - Failure and retry: a failure while purging one entity class MUST be logged as one `ERROR` line naming that class and the cutoff, and NEVER stops the other classes. The scheduled task MUST catch every `Exception` that escapes the purge, log one `ERROR` line naming the cutoff and return normally, so the next run retries; an exception NEVER ends the schedule.
  - Bounded transactions: `EntityManagerOperations.purge(Instant cutoff)` MUST delete per entity class in batches of at most 1000 identifiers. Each batch MUST first select the identifiers of at most 1000 records whose retention attribute is non-null and strictly before the cutoff, then delete through a JPA Criteria `CriteriaDelete` whose restriction repeats that predicate: the identifier is in the selected batch AND the retention attribute is non-null AND the retention attribute is strictly before the same bound cutoff parameter in the attribute type. The delete NEVER restricts by identifiers alone: a concurrent `save` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:54-71`) or `@InjectEntityManager` code commits in its own transaction and can move a selected record's retention value to the cutoff or later between the select and the delete, and the repeated predicate leaves that record unmatched, so it stays. The cutoff and the identifiers MUST be bound parameters, NEVER concatenated into a query. A delete count below the selected count is neither a failure nor a reason to retry. Each batch runs in its own session and transaction, commits on success, and on any `RuntimeException` rolls back while the transaction is active before the session closes, as `save` does (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:54-71`). A select that returns fewer than 1000 identifiers ends that class, whatever the delete count.
  - Factory snapshots: each run MUST purge `DefaultEntity` through `getSessionFactory` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:73-92`) and every class held by the factory-map snapshot taken at the start of the run (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:51-52`), each class once. A snapshot class that fails the retention contract, for example one registered only through `@InjectEntityManager` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DBStoreLogDataService.java:56-71`), MUST be skipped with one `WARN` line per run naming the class and the unmet requirement, and is NEVER queried; this skip is not a purge failure.
  - Close coordination: one lock MUST guard each purge batch and the swap-and-close of `onUndeploy` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/EntityManagerOperations.java:99-110`), so a factory NEVER closes during a batch. Before each batch the purge MUST confirm that its factory is still in the current map and open, and stop that class otherwise. The purge MUST stop between batches once its thread is interrupted.
  - Shutdown: a `@PreDestroy` method of `DBStoreLogDataService` MUST call `shutdownNow`, wait at most 30 seconds for termination and log one `ERROR` line when the purge thread still runs; with no executor it does nothing.
  - At the defaults `DBStoreLogDataService` MUST store the bodies and MUST purge nothing, exactly as today.
  - Validation: `ruleservice.store.logs.db.retention-days` is an `int` and MUST be 0 or above. While store-log is enabled, `DBStoreLogDataService` MUST refuse a negative, blank, non-integer or out-of-`int`-range value at startup and MUST compute the purge cutoff with `java.time` arithmetic. `0` keeps purging off and logs its `WARN`.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `ruleservice.store.logs.db.store-payloads` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `ruleservice.store.logs.db.enabled` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/resources/openl-default.properties:1`); default `true`; recommended secure value `false`.
  - `ruleservice.store.logs.db.retention-days` in the same place; default `0` (0 means no purge); recommended secure value `30`.
  - `WARN` owner: `DBStoreLogDataService`, while store-log is enabled, logs one line while payloads are stored and one line while retention is `0`, each naming SEC-12, its property and its secure value.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the values stops storing request and response bodies and deletes records after 30 days; reports that read the bodies or old records change. At a positive retention every custom entity class needs the retention contract, or its records are refused, and a record whose populated retention value is null, for example one without an incoming time, is refused as well. The purge NEVER deletes a record whose retention attribute is null, so records stored with a null value before retention became positive, at retention `0` or before the upgrade, and records written with a null value through an `@InjectEntityManager` entity manager stay; the entry MUST tell the operator that the purge NEVER deletes them by itself, and to find those rows in each entity table and either delete them or set the attribute to their actual ingestion time under the operator's own retention policy. A custom `@Value` converter that copies a body keeps storing it. A custom-entity table is purged only once its service has stored a record since the last start or redeploy, so the operator purges the tables of entity classes no service stores any more and of registered classes the purge skips for an unmet retention contract. The purge builds the `DefaultEntity` factory when it is absent, so with the module default `hibernate.hbm2ddl.auto = update` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/resources/openl-default.properties:7`) it creates the `openl_log_data` table where none exists.
- Tests: mocked collaborators only, with no database, server or real scheduler thread: Mockito mocks of `EntityManagerOperations`, `SessionFactory`, `Session`, `Transaction`, the metamodel and the Criteria objects, a mocked `ScheduledExecutorService` whose captured task the test runs, and a fixed `Clock`.
  - `DBStoreLogDataServiceTest`. Default: a RESTful record and a Kafka record each store their request and response bodies in `DefaultEntity`, nothing is scheduled, and both `WARN` lines are logged. Secure: `DefaultEntity` and a custom entity whose `@Request` and `@Response` sit on a field, a setter method and an inherited element store no body and run none of those converters, for a RESTful record and for Kafka consumer, dead-letter and producer records, while every other column, `@Value` and `@KafkaMessageHeader` included, equals its default-state value; the purge is registered once through `scheduleWithFixedDelay` with a 1-minute initial delay and a 60-minute delay; the captured task calls `purge` with the fixed clock's instant minus 30 days; no `WARN` is logged. Partially hardened: payloads off with retention `0` asserts the remaining `WARN`. Refused states: `-1`, a blank and a non-integer retention each fail `setup` with the property-naming error, and an unset retention behaves as `0`. Lifecycle: a failing purge is logged and the next run calls it again; at retention 30 an entity class without the contract, and a class-valid entity whose populated retention attribute is null for a RESTful record and for a Kafka record without an incoming time, each fail `save` with the named error and `EntityManagerOperations.save` never runs for any entity of that record, while at retention `0` the same records are saved as today; the `@PreDestroy` method calls `shutdownNow` and `awaitTermination`.
  - `EntityManagerOperationsTest`: `purge` deletes, in committed batches, only the identifiers the cutoff query returns for aged records and binds the cutoff in the attribute type, so newer records stay; each `CriteriaDelete` restriction is the conjunction of the identifier `in` predicate with the `isNotNull` and `lessThan` predicates on the retention attribute bound to the same cutoff value, so a selected record refreshed before the delete, shown by a mocked delete count below the selected count, stays while the batch commits, and the class ends only on a selection shorter than 1000 identifiers; the select and the delete both carry the `isNotNull` predicate, so a record with a null retention attribute is neither selected nor deleted; a failing batch rolls back before its session closes, logs its `ERROR` line, and the next class is still purged; `DefaultEntity` meets the contract, while a class without `@IncomingTime`, with a `String` time attribute or without a single `@Id` fails naming the class and the property; a snapshot class without the contract is skipped with its `WARN` and never queried, while the other classes are still purged; `onUndeploy` during a batch waits for it, after which the purge stops for the closed factory; an interrupted purge stops before its next batch.
  - `StoreLogDataMapperTest`: the no-argument mapper fills `@Request` and `@Response` as today; a mapper skipping them leaves them unset for RESTful data and for Kafka consumer, dead-letter and producer records, runs none of their converters, never reads the record values and leaves the `StoreLogData` unchanged, while every other annotation, `@KafkaMessageHeader` included, is mapped.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 125–230; Test: 434–640; Configuration and build: 8–16; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes; `DBStoreLogDataServiceTest`, `EntityManagerOperationsTest` and `StoreLogDataMapperTest` prove the default, secure, partially hardened and refused states and the RESTful and Kafka, custom-entity, retention-contract, null-value refusal, contract-skip, refreshed-record survival, failure-retry, overlap, undeploy and shutdown cases; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-12 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Add a switch that requests S3 server-side encryption on every write

- Finding: [SEC-22](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-22)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-311
- Severity: Medium 4.7
- Bucket: Default-changing fix
- Files and classes: `S3Repository` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:276-287` file writes, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:482-490` history copy, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:523-527` modification marker, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:91-114` initialization), `S3RepositoryFactory` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3RepositoryFactory.java:29-34`, which creates every S3 repository), `RepositoryInstatiator` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:78-90` settings resolution, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:110-123` setter mapping; read only), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/resources/openl-default.properties:16-19`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/pom.xml` (test dependencies); new test root `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/test` with `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/test/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3RepositoryEncryptionTest.java`, styled after `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/test`; `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md`.
- Required fix:
  - At `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled=true` with a non-blank `repo-aws-s3.sse-algorithm`, every `putObject` request, for saved files and for the modification marker, MUST request server-side encryption with that algorithm (`AES256` or `aws:kms`).
  - In the same state the `copyObject` request of `copyHistory` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:485-489`) MUST request server-side encryption with that algorithm, because the copy writes a new object version and today carries no encryption request. That request keeps its current metadata behavior: it NEVER sets a metadata directive, so the copy keeps the source version's metadata, including any `sseAlgorithm` entry.
  - The existing `sseAlgorithm` metadata entry MUST stay in both states, and at the default (`false`) requests MUST carry no server-side encryption, exactly as today. At `true` with a blank `sse-algorithm`, requests MUST also carry none, because no algorithm is configured.
  - `RepositoryInstatiator.setParams` maps each public one-argument setter to a flat kebab-case key (`setSseRequestEnabled` becomes `sse-request-enabled`), so it NEVER reads the dotted `sse-request.enabled`. `S3RepositoryFactory.create` MUST therefore read `sse-request.enabled` from its own settings function, after `setParams` and before `initialize()`, and hand the value to `S3Repository` through a package-private method, so the `WARN` in `S3Repository` initialization reads the effective switch. That function resolves the repository's own `<prefix>.sse-request.enabled` first and `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled` second (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:84-88`), so every S3 repository that OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services create through `RepositoryInstatiator.newRepository` receives the switch.
  - A null or blank value MUST mean `false`, matching how `setParams` skips blank values (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:117-119`). Only `true` and `false`, trimmed and case-insensitive, MUST be accepted. Any other value MUST fail repository creation with an `IllegalArgumentException` naming `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled`, the repository id from the `id` setting and the rejected value, and NEVER fall back to `false`.
  - The switch MUST NEVER be a public one-argument `set` method of `S3Repository`, because `setParams` reflects public methods only (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:112-113`) and would bind it to the undocumented flat key `sse-request-enabled`. Every existing key (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/resources/openl-default.properties:3-19`) keeps reaching its setter through the unchanged `setParams` call, the factory IDs `repo-aws-s3` and `org.openl.rules.repository.aws.S3Repository` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3RepositoryFactory.java:15-21`) stay unchanged, and `S3RepositoryFactory` keeps the public no-argument constructor that `ServiceLoader` instantiates (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository/src/org/openl/rules/repository/RepositoryInstatiator.java:50`).
  - `S3RepositoryFactory` MUST gain a package-private constructor taking the `Supplier<S3ClientBuilder>` that `S3Repository.initialize()` uses in place of `S3Client.builder()` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:92`), handed over with the switch before `initialize()`. The public constructor passes `S3Client::builder`, so production clients are built exactly as today.
  - The module has no test source root, so the directive MUST add `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/test` and the JUnit and Mockito test dependencies to the module POM.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Switch:
  - `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled` in `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/resources/openl-default.properties`, beside `repo-aws-s3.sse-algorithm` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/resources/openl-default.properties:16-19`); default `false`; recommended secure value `true`.
  - `WARN` owner: `S3Repository` initialization logs one line while `repo-aws-s3.sse-algorithm` is set and requests carry no server-side encryption, naming SEC-22, the property and `true`.
  - `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` entry: flipping the value makes S3 encrypt new objects, history copies included; with `aws:kms` the repository credentials also need permission to use the key.
- Tests: `S3RepositoryEncryptionTest` with a mocked S3 client and no remote call. Every test creates the repository through `S3RepositoryFactory.create` from a settings function holding the `repo-aws-s3` keys, passing through the package-private constructor a mocked `S3ClientBuilder` that returns the mocked client, with the bucket-versioning and object-version listing calls stubbed so that initialization and saving complete. It saves a file, applies each captured `putObject` request consumer to `PutObjectRequest.builder()` and asserts the requests for the file and for the modification marker; it NEVER sets the switch on `S3Repository` directly. Default: with `sse-algorithm` `AES256`, both requests carry the algorithm only as metadata. Secure: with `sse-request.enabled` `true`, the same requests ask for server-side encryption with `AES256`. It also copies a stored version through `copyHistory`, with `copyObject` stubbed to return a `CopyObjectResponse` holding the new version ID and the version listing holding that version, applies the captured `copyObject` request consumer to `CopyObjectRequest.builder()` and asserts the copy request in both states: at the default it carries no server-side encryption, at the secure value it asks for server-side encryption with `AES256`, and in both it sets no metadata directive. The `WARN` is asserted at the default. A value other than `true` or `false` fails creation with the error naming `repo-aws-s3.sse-request.enabled`, a blank value behaves as the default, and the flat key `sse-request-enabled` set to `true` leaves the requests without server-side encryption.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 58–113; Test: 177–255; Configuration and build: 12–24; Documentation: 8–15
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes and runs the new test root, `S3RepositoryEncryptionTest` proves both states for the file, modification-marker and history-copy requests, the `WARN` and the refused value through `S3RepositoryFactory` settings, and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` holds the SEC-22 entry.

CRITICAL Directive: Stop retaining OAuth2 access and refresh tokens after authentication while keeping password erasure and SAML and OIDC logout state

- Finding: [SEC-28](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-28)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-316
- Severity: Medium 4.7
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: the `oauth2Filter` bean of `OAuth2SecurityConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:179-191`, in-memory authorized-client service at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:185-186`), `OpenLOAuth2UserService` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OpenLOAuth2UserService.java:33-35`, ID token placed in the principal, read only), `Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler.java:24-29`, OIDC logout, read only), the `authenticationManager` bean of `CommonAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:37-49`, erasure disabled at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:47` with the SAML logout reason at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:46`, read only), which only the SAML, OAuth2 login and bearer filters receive (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:217-222`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:181-187`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:40-43`), `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-49`, read only), whose form-login and HTTP Basic chains set no manager; new test `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfigTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - The `oauth2` login MUST NEVER keep the access and refresh tokens in an application-wide store that no OpenL code reads, and MUST NEVER move them into the session instead.
  - The ID token that OIDC logout reads MUST stay in the `oauth2` principal.
  - Form login and HTTP Basic in `multi` and `ad` modes keep no password today: each `HttpSecurity` chain authenticates through the `ProviderManager` it builds over the global `ProviderManager` that Spring Security 6.5.11 (`pom.xml:100`) builds from the mode's single `AuthenticationProvider` bean, and both managers keep their default credential erasure. The fix MUST NEVER route those logins through the `CommonAuthenticationConfig` manager and MUST NEVER turn that erasure off.
  - The `authenticationManager` bean of `CommonAuthenticationConfig` MUST stay unchanged, so the SAML authentication NEVER loses the state SAML global logout needs; turning its erasure on would clear neither the SAML response nor the OAuth2 tokens, so no change to it serves this finding.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: `OAuth2SecurityConfigTest` (new), server-free: it MUST call `new OAuth2SecurityConfig().oauth2Filter(...)` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:179-191`), or the authorized-client choice the fix extracts from it, directly, with Mockito `AuthenticationManager`, `SessionAuthenticationStrategy` and `AuthenticationSuccessHandler` collaborators, a fixed `InMemoryClientRegistrationRepository` holding the mocked client registration, and a mocked token response. It MUST NEVER load `OAuth2SecurityConfig` into a Spring context, because its import of `OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:46`) reaches `OAuth2ImportSelector` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:29`), which builds `OAuth2Configuration` and so fetches the issuer metadata over HTTP while the context is parsed (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:20-22`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:31-35`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:48-53`). It MUST NEVER use `LazyClientRegistrationRepository`, whose first lookup fetches the issuer metadata as well (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/LazyClientRegistrationRepository.java:32-33`). It MUST exercise OIDC logout through `new Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler(...)` over the same fixed repository (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler.java:24-29`). It covers these states: an `oauth2` login retains no authorized client holding its access or refresh token, and OIDC logout still receives the ID token from the principal.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 15–25; Test: 51–78; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `OAuth2SecurityConfigTest` proves that an `oauth2` login retains no authorized client holding its access or refresh token while OIDC logout still receives the ID token, `OAuth2SecurityConfigTest` loads no Spring context holding `OAuth2SecurityConfig` and makes no network request, and the directive's own diff changes neither `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` nor the `authenticationManager` bean of `CommonAuthenticationConfig`.

CRITICAL Directive: Remove request payloads from the Kafka dead-letter failure logs

- Finding: [SEC-40](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-40)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-532
- Severity: Medium 4.7
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: `KafkaService.sendErrorToDlt` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:454-499`, synchronous failure line at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:491-496`) and `KafkaService.onDltSent` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:501-522`, callback failure line at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:510-514`, store-log handling at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:506-509` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:515-521`); `KafkaService.processRecord`, which resolves the request ID and puts it under the `requestId` MDC key (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:246-249`), and `KafkaService.onResultSent`, which calls `sendErrorToDlt` from the result producer's callback (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:349-380`); `RequestMessage.asText` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.kafka/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/RequestMessage.java:58-60`, read only); `RuleServicesFilter.REQUEST_ID_KEY` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:53`, read only) and the two layouts that render it (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:10`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:18`, read only); new test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaDltLoggingTest.java`; the `KafkaService.createService` test seam (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:84-108`) that takes the Kafka `Consumer` and `Producer` interfaces, and the Kafka startup fixture, both shared, counted in SEC-38: the SEC-38 directive creates them, and this directive reuses them and creates the seam itself only when it is absent.
- Required fix:
  - `sendErrorToDlt` and `onDltSent` MUST NEVER put the record value, its raw data, `RequestMessage.asText()` output or text derived from them into a log line, so neither dead-letter failure line carries any part of the request payload.
  - Each failure line MUST name the dead-letter topic and the input record's topic, partition and offset, and MUST pass the exception that failed the send as the logged throwable, as today, so the failed record stays traceable without its content.
  - Request ID: when `log.request-id.header` is set (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaRuleServicePublisher.java:288`), each failure line MUST carry the request ID that `processRecord` resolved, through the `requestId` MDC entry that both layouts render and NEVER as message text, so the request ID keeps the one logged form its MDC entry gives it. Both methods also run on producer callback threads, which hold no such entry, so `onDltSent` MUST receive the request ID as `sendErrorToDlt` does, and each method MUST set the entry to that request ID for its log call and restore the thread's previous entry afterwards. With the property unset, neither method sets an entry.
  - The two failure lines MUST be identical with store-log disabled and enabled, and the store-log handling of `onDltSent` stays unchanged.
  - A dead-letter send that succeeds MUST NOT write a log line it does not write today, and the dead-letter record, its headers and its topic routing stay unchanged.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: new `KafkaDltLoggingTest` in the module's test tree, built through the `KafkaService.createService` seam with the kafka-clients `MockConsumer` and `MockProducer`, mocked records whose raw value carries a unique payload marker, a mocked `StoreLogDataManager`, a capture of the `KafkaService` log output and no broker. The module's test logging binding `slf4j-simple` (`pom.xml:1192-1196`) keeps no MDC, so `MDC` is statically mocked on the test thread, and every failure line under test is written on that thread from a callback that `MockProducer.errorNext` or `MockProducer.completeNext` runs.
  - Synchronous failure: a result send that the result `MockProducer` fails through `errorNext` reaches `sendErrorToDlt`, and with `sendException` set on the dead-letter `MockProducer` the captured dead-letter failure line names the dead-letter topic and the record's topic, partition and offset, carries the thrown exception and holds neither the payload marker nor any other part of `asText()`. The line is identical with store-log disabled and enabled.
  - Asynchronous failure: with the dead-letter `MockProducer` not completing sends automatically, `errorNext` fails the dead-letter send, and the captured line meets the same assertions with store-log disabled and enabled.
  - Request ID: with a request-ID header key passed to `createService`, as `log.request-id.header` supplies it, `MDC` holds the record's request ID under `requestId` while each failure line is written, and the thread's previous entry is restored after it; without a key, no entry is set.
  - Successful send: `completeNext` on the dead-letter `MockProducer` adds no line to the capture, with store-log disabled and enabled.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 15–30; Test: 180–248; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `KafkaDltLoggingTest` proves that neither dead-letter failure line holds payload text with store-log disabled or enabled, that each line keeps the topics, partition, offset and exception and, when configured, the request ID entry, and that a successful dead-letter send logs nothing new.

CRITICAL Directive: Log a categorized, secret-free reason for every refused OpenL Rule Services JWT

- Finding: [SEC-43](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-43)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-532
- Severity: Medium 4.7
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: the bearer-token check of `JWTValidator.authorize` in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:91-110`, with its refusal catch at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:105-106`; unchanged read references: the jose4j 0.9.7 version (`pom.xml:109`), whose `org.jose4j.jwt.consumer.InvalidJwtException` carries the `org.jose4j.jwt.consumer.ErrorCodes` codes, and the console and rolling-file layouts (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:10`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:18`); test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidatorTest.java` (created by SEC-01; created here when absent).
- Required fix:
  - Every refusal by the bearer-token check, on every JWT-protected path with deployment paths included, MUST log exactly one `WARN` line holding one fixed category and the request path info (`getPathInfo()`) with every control character removed.
  - The category MUST come only from the error codes of the caught `InvalidJwtException` and the type of its cause, NEVER from message text, and MUST be the first that matches in this order: `expired` (`EXPIRED`), `invalid audience` (`AUDIENCE_INVALID`, `AUDIENCE_MISSING`), `invalid issuer` (`ISSUER_INVALID`, `ISSUER_MISSING`), `invalid signature` (`SIGNATURE_INVALID`), `disallowed algorithm` (`SIGNATURE_MISSING`, or a cause of type `org.jose4j.lang.InvalidAlgorithmException`), `unresolvable key` (a cause of type `org.jose4j.lang.UnresolvableKeyException`), `malformed` (`JSON_INVALID`, `MALFORMED_CLAIM` or any other `MISCELLANEOUS` failure), and `other` for every remaining code and every exception that is not an `InvalidJwtException`.
  - The line MUST NEVER hold the token, a claim value, the original exception, its message (`getMessage`, `getOriginalMessage`), the messages of its error details or its cause, and the exception MUST NOT reach the logger as a throwable or as a format argument. The `Authorization` header value is NEVER logged.
  - `authorize` MUST refuse every token it refuses today and accept every token it accepts today, and the absent-header and non-bearer lines (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:93`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:97`) MUST stay unchanged.
  - The SEC-01 directive makes this catch edit first, and its LOC row counts it. This directive MUST keep that edit, and MUST make it itself whenever `JWTValidator` still logs the original exception, its message or its cause.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: `JWTValidatorTest`, with the JWKS and signing fixture and the log-capture helper that SEC-01 adds (created here when absent), drives `authorize` with mocked `HttpServletRequest` objects on an ordinary service path outside `/admin/`; no server starts and no remote JWKS location is opened. A malformed, an expired, a wrong-audience, a wrong-issuer, a bad-signature, an unknown-key-ID and an unsigned or disallowed-algorithm token are each refused, and each captured refusal line holds its category and the path and holds neither the compact token, any claim value nor the jose4j message text. A refused request whose path info holds a line feed yields exactly one line, and an accepted token logs no refusal line.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 0–0; Test: 93–207; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `JWTValidatorTest` proves that every refused token is logged as one line holding its category and the path and none of the token, claim values or jose4j message text.

`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityEventAuditTest.java` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/service/SecurityEventAuditTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - Events: `SecurityEventLogger` MUST write exactly one event through one dedicated SLF4J logger, `org.openl.studio.security.audit`, for each failed login, access denial, PAT creation and deletion, ACL change, and user or group membership change, naming the event type, the acting user, the subject and the outcome. It receives that logger through its constructor, because the test classpath routes logging to `slf4j-simple` (`pom.xml:1192-1196`) and has no capturing appender.
  - Registration: `SecurityEventAuditConfig` MUST carry the condition of `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:29` and declare `SecurityEventLogger`, the shared `AccessDeniedHandler` and `SecurityEventPostProcessor` as explicit `@Bean`s, the post-processor through a `static` method, so no hook depends on component scanning and the post-processor never initializes its configuration early. The auditing failure handlers and entry-point wrappers below come from methods of the `SecurityEventLogger` bean, which each filter configuration injects. `ApiExceptionControllerAdvice` obtains the bean through an `ObjectProvider`, because `single` mode declares none.
  - Failed logins: every refused login MUST write one event at the filter that received it, because a refusal raised before an authentication manager is called never reaches that manager, and every caller of a manager is one of the filters below. The SAML, OAuth2 login and bearer filters call the `CommonAuthenticationConfig` `ProviderManager` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:45`), which each of them receives explicitly (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:217-222`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:181-187`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:40-43`). Form login and HTTP Basic set no manager (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-49`), so each `HttpSecurity` chain calls the `ProviderManager` it builds, whose parent is the global `ProviderManager` that Spring Security 6.5.11 (`pom.xml:100`) builds from the mode's single `AuthenticationProvider` bean through `InitializeAuthenticationProviderBeanManagerConfigurer`. `Saml2WebSsoAuthenticationFilter`, `OAuth2LoginAuthenticationFilter` and the form-login filter MUST each get through `setAuthenticationFailureHandler` an auditing handler that writes the event and then delegates to the handler the filter uses today: a new `SimpleUrlAuthenticationFailureHandler` for SAML and OAuth2, and one for `/login?error` for form login. `AbstractAuthenticationProcessingFilter` passes every refusal from `attemptAuthentication` to that handler once, so it records refusals raised before the manager, such as an unknown relying-party registration and the OAuth2 errors `invalid_request`, `authorization_request_not_found` and `client_registration_not_found`, as well as those the manager throws. Form login takes the handler through `withObjectPostProcessor` and NEVER through `failureHandler`, because `failureHandler` clears the default failure URL and `permitAll` then stops permitting `/login?error`. The settings check at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/model/constraint/ADConnectionConstraintValidator.java:31` tests a candidate configuration, not a login, and writes no event.
  - Failed REST credentials: the HTTP Basic `HttpStatusEntryPoint` MUST be replaced by an auditing wrapper of it that writes the event only when the request's `Authorization` header starts with `Basic`, compared without case as `BasicAuthenticationConverter` does, because `BasicAuthenticationFilter` calls that entry point once for each refused attempt, an undecodable header included, and stops the chain, while exception translation calls it for requests without Basic credentials. `BearerTokenAuthenticationFilter` MUST get through `setAuthenticationEntryPoint` an auditing wrapper of the entry-point bean, which the filter calls only when `DefaultBearerTokenResolver` refuses a malformed token before the manager, and its failure handler MUST write the event before its existing branches and keep calling the unwrapped bean. `bearerExceptionTranslationFilter` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:67-70`) keeps the unwrapped bean, so a request without a token writes nothing. `PatAuthenticationFilter` refuses tokens without the manager, so it MUST write the event at both refusals (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:77-82`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:84-89`), naming the token's public ID only when the token parses.
  - Single writer: a security event MUST NEVER come from an `AuthenticationEventPublisher` or a listener for authentication-failure events, so a refusal the manager throws is written once, by the filter that called it.
  - Failed-login identity: the `actor` field MUST carry the name of the refusal's `getAuthenticationRequest()` only when it is a `UsernamePasswordAuthenticationToken`, which form login and HTTP Basic build from the request (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:43`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:30`) and which the global `ProviderManager` above attaches to every refusal it throws, the chain's own `ProviderManager` rethrowing that refusal unchanged, and MUST stay empty for every other token type and for a refusal raised before the manager, because a bearer token's principal is the token itself. The rule MUST hold even when the request already carries an authenticated session, so a failed-login `actor` is NEVER read from the current authentication.
  - Access denials: one shared `AccessDeniedHandler` bean MUST write the event and then delegate to `AccessDeniedHandlerImpl`, so every response stays as today. It MUST be set through `setAccessDeniedHandler` on each hand-assembled `ExceptionTranslationFilter` bean above and through `exceptionHandling` on both `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` chains. `ApiExceptionControllerAdvice` answers method-security and ACL refusals before any filter sees them, so it MUST write the event in `handleSecurityErrors` and for every `RestRuntimeException` it answers with 403. An exception the advice handles never reaches a filter, so each denial writes one event. The subject is the HTTP method and the request URI with every `;` path parameter removed.
  - PAT, ACL and membership changes: `SecurityEventPostProcessor` MUST advise, with class-based proxies so every injection point typed by class keeps resolving, `save` (creation, reached from `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatGeneratorServiceImpl.java:88`), `deleteByPublicId` (single-token revocation, reached from `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/users/rest/controller/PersonalAccessTokenController.java:103`) and `deleteAllByUser` (per-user revocation) of `PersonalAccessTokenServiceImpl`; every `addPermissions`, `addRootPermissions`, `removePermissions`, `removeRootPermissions`, `createAcl`, `deleteAcl`, `move` and `updateOwner` overload of both repository ACL service beans, the `RepositoryAclServiceImpl` design bean and the `SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl` production bean; `deleteSid` and `updateSid` of `JdbcMutableAclService`; `addUser`, both `updateAuthorities` overloads and `deleteUser` of `UserManagementService`; `addGroup`, both `updateGroup` overloads and `deleteGroup` of `GroupManagementService`; and `process` of `BulkAclOverwriteServiceImpl`. The advised ACL beans therefore record the project edits of `AclProjectsController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/AclProjectsController.java:131-154`), the repository and repository-root edits of `AclRepositoriesController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/AclRepositoriesController.java:90-150`) and the edits of every other caller, and a controller NEVER writes an ACL event of its own.
  - No duplicate events: an advised call made while another advised call runs on the same thread MUST write no event of its own. A bulk overwrite therefore writes one event carrying the counts of groups, users and resources in its request, and `UserManagementService.deleteUser` writes one event, NEVER one per inner change. The SID removal that `UsersController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/UsersController.java:303-306`) and `ManagementController` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/ManagementController.java:112-127`) make as a separate call after a deletion is its own ACL change and writes its own event.
  - Committed outcomes: when a transaction synchronization is active, `SecurityEventInterceptor` MUST register a `TransactionSynchronization` and write the event in `afterCompletion`, as `evictCacheOnCommit` does (`STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:84-95`); without one it writes the event when the call returns or throws. The outcome is `success` only when the call returned and its transaction, if any, committed, and `failure` otherwise. A `BulkAclOverwriteServiceImpl.process` call that returns with its thread's interrupt status set, read without clearing it, records `failure`, because the method returns normally without changing anything when interrupted (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/service/BulkAclOverwriteServiceImpl.java:55-57`). The call's own result and exception pass through unchanged.
  - Field contract: each event MUST be one line holding one JSON object written by the Jackson `ObjectMapper` the module already uses (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/settings/rest/controller/SystemSettingsController.java:4`), with the fields `event`, `actor`, `subject`, `resource`, `permissions`, `outcome`, `reason` and `truncated`. `event` and `outcome` take values from fixed sets; `actor` of a failed-login event, a refused REST credential included, follows the Failed-login identity rule above, carrying the attempted username of a username-password refusal or staying empty, and NEVER takes the current authentication's name or `system`, while the `actor` of every access denial, PAT creation and deletion, ACL change, and user or group membership change is the current authentication's name, or `system` when there is none; `subject` is the user, group, SID or token public ID acted on; `resource` is the repository and path, project or request; `permissions` lists the integer masks of an ACL change's `Permission` arguments and is empty for every other event; `reason` is the simple class name of the refusing exception and NEVER its message.
  - Bounded and escaped values: before serialization every free-text value MUST be normalized to Unicode NFC and cut to its first 256 characters without splitting a surrogate pair, with `truncated` set to `true` when any value was cut. The serializer MUST write every character below U+0020, from U+007F to U+009F, U+2028 and U+2029 as a `\uXXXX` escape besides the JSON escapes of `"` and `\`, so an event NEVER spans two lines, NEVER adds a field or a record, and NEVER exceeds 8192 characters.
  - Safety: events MUST NEVER contain a password, PAT secret or hash, bearer token, session identifier, query string, request body or exception message. Writing an event MUST NEVER change an authentication, authorization or mutation outcome: a serialization failure writes the event type and outcome alone, and no logger exception reaches the caller.
  - Successful-login recording (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/LastLoginRecorder.java:23-31`) and every existing behavior MUST stay unchanged.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests:
  - `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityEventAuditTest.java` (new) passes a Mockito mock of `org.slf4j.Logger` to `SecurityEventLogger` and captures every line. A `Saml2WebSsoAuthenticationFilter` and an `OAuth2LoginAuthenticationFilter` built as their configurations build them, with a mocked manager, each write exactly one event and answer the status they answer without auditing, both for a refusal raised before the manager (an unknown relying-party registration; `invalid_request`, `authorization_request_not_found` and `client_registration_not_found`) and for a manager refusal. Both `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` chains in a MockMvc context over a Mockito `AuthenticationProvider` bean, which the global `ProviderManager` holds as its provider so that each refusal carries its authentication request, write one event for a refused form login, which still redirects to `/login?error` while that page stays reachable without authentication, one each for a refused and an undecodable Basic header, which still answer 401, and none for a REST request without credentials. The bearer filter writes one event for a malformed token and one for a token the manager refuses, each answering as today, and none for a request without a token. Each `PatAuthenticationFilter` refusal writes one event and still answers 401. A refused form login and a refused Basic header carry the attempted username as `actor`, also when the request's session already holds a different authenticated user; the SAML, OAuth2, bearer and PAT refusals and every refusal raised before the manager (the undecodable Basic header, the unknown relying-party registration, the three OAuth2 errors and the malformed bearer token) carry an empty `actor`, NEVER `system`; and the access-denial events carry the authenticated user's name. Each listed `ExceptionTranslationFilter` bean and both `FormBasedAuthenticationConfig` chains in a MockMvc context, reached by an authenticated request whose handler throws `AccessDeniedException`, write one event and still answer 403, and `ApiExceptionControllerAdvice` writes one event for an `AccessDeniedException` and for a `ForbiddenException`.
  - The same class covers hostile input: an identity and a subject holding CR, LF, NUL, DEL, U+0085 and U+2028, and a 10000-character identity, each yield exactly one captured line with no raw line terminator, the escaped values, the 256-character cut with `truncated` `true` and at most 8192 characters, while the thrown exception, the status and the authentication result equal those of the same attempt with auditing removed. Every password, PAT secret, bearer token, session ID, query string and request body a test sets is absent from every line, and a serializer that throws leaves every outcome unchanged.
  - `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/test/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/service/SecurityEventAuditTest.java` (new) applies `SecurityEventPostProcessor` to mocked targets: each advised PAT, ACL and membership method writes one event, `deleteByPublicId` naming the token public ID and `deleteAllByUser` the user, and every ACL event lists its permission masks; inside a `TransactionTemplate` the event appears only after commit with `success`, after a rollback with `failure`, and at once without a transaction. The project edits of `AclProjectsController` through the `RepositoryAclServiceImpl` bean and the repository and repository-root edits of `AclRepositoriesController` through the `SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl` bean write their events, `BulkAclOverwriteServiceImpl.process` writes exactly one event with the request counts and no inner event, and records `failure` when interrupted, `UserManagementService.deleteUser` writes one event, the separate SID removal of `UsersController` writes one ACL event, and the advised `JdbcMutableAclService` still resolves by class.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 225–390; Test: 598–867; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and both `SecurityEventAuditTest` classes prove that every listed event is written once with its committed outcome, single-token and per-user PAT revocation and the project, repository and repository-root ACL edits included, that hostile values stay escaped and bounded, that a failed-login event's `actor` names only the attempted username of a username-password refusal and is otherwise empty, that no authentication, authorization or mutation outcome changes, and that no secret appears in any event.

CRITICAL Directive: Evict ACL cache entries after commit on every revocation path

- Finding: [SEC-25](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-25)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-613
- Severity: Medium 4.2
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: `SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl` (`STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:244-255`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:277-298` and `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:307-331` revocation paths, existing `evictCacheOnCommit` at `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:79-95`), the two-minute ACL cache (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/cache2k.xml:17-18`, read only); new test `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/test/org/openl/security/acl/repository/AclRevocationCacheTest.java`.
- Required fix:
  - Every `removePermissions` overload MUST evict the affected identity, and its affected descendants, through the existing `evictCacheOnCommit`, in addition to the eviction before the write, so a read during the uncommitted transaction cannot leave a stale grant cached.
  - The cache configuration MUST stay unchanged.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: `AclRevocationCacheTest` with an active transaction synchronization: a cache read between the revocation and the commit repopulates the entry, and after completion the entry is gone, for every revocation overload.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 40–80; Test: 100–200; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, and `AclRevocationCacheTest` proves post-commit eviction on every revocation overload.

CRITICAL Directive: Escape and bound the Kafka and HTTP request IDs in log output while the HTTP response, Kafka reply and dead-letter headers keep them unchanged

- Finding: [SEC-39](SECURITY_FINDINGS.md#sec-39)
- CVE/CWE: CWE-117
- Severity: Low 3.7
- Bucket: Code fix
- Files and classes: `KafkaService.processRecord` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:240-286`, MDC write at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:246-248`), `KafkaService.resolveRequestIdHeader` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:291-300`, read only), the reply request-ID header in `KafkaService.sendResult` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:328-330`, read only) and the dead-letter request-ID header in `KafkaService.sendErrorToDlt` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:464-466`, read only); the `requestId` MDC writes that the SEC-40 directive adds to `KafkaService.sendErrorToDlt` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:454-499`) and `KafkaService.onDltSent` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:501-522`) for their dead-letter failure lines; `RuleServicesFilter.REQUEST_ID_KEY` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:53`), beside which the new `requestIdForLog` method goes, and `RuleServicesFilter.processForwarded` (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:102-123`, MDC write at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:114`, response header at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:113` read only); the plain patterns `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:10` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:18` and the image's plain pattern `Dockerfile:227` (read only); new test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaRequestIdLoggingTest.java`; existing test `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/test/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilterTest.java`; the `KafkaService.createService` test seam that takes the Kafka `Consumer` and `Producer` interfaces and the Kafka startup fixture (shared, counted in SEC-38).
- Required fix:
  - Logging representation: `RuleServicesFilter` MUST compute the value of the MDC `requestId` entry in one `public static` method, `requestIdForLog`, declared beside `REQUEST_ID_KEY`, and every `MDC.put` of the `requestId` key in `RuleServicesFilter` and `KafkaService` MUST pass only that method's result for the request ID it sets: the write in `RuleServicesFilter.processForwarded`, the write in `KafkaService.processRecord` and every write the SEC-40 directive adds to `sendErrorToDlt` and `onDltSent` for their dead-letter failure lines. Those two `KafkaService` methods MUST keep receiving the decoded request ID and encode it only at the `MDC.put`, so the dead-letter header built from the same value stays unchanged. When the SEC-40 writes are absent, this rule covers the `processForwarded` and `processRecord` writes alone and this directive adds no write to `sendErrorToDlt` or `onDltSent`. The restore of a thread's previous entry that the SEC-40 directive requires MUST put that entry back unchanged and NEVER pass it through `requestIdForLog` again, so an encoded ID is NEVER encoded twice. The `requestId` entry, and every log line rendered from it, MUST NEVER contain a raw character of the HTTP or Kafka request-ID header below U+0020, from U+007F to U+009F, U+2028 or U+2029.
  - Encoding: `requestIdForLog` MUST write each of those characters as a `\uXXXX` escape with four uppercase hexadecimal digits and each backslash as two backslashes, and MUST copy every other character unchanged, so a UUID-style ID renders exactly as received.
  - Bound: when the encoded text exceeds 128 characters, `requestIdForLog` MUST keep its first 128 characters, cutting before any escape sequence or surrogate pair that crosses the bound and NEVER inside one, and MUST append the marker `[truncated]`, so the entry NEVER exceeds 139 characters.
  - Placement: `requestIdForLog` MUST exist only in `RuleServicesFilter` and NEVER be duplicated in `KafkaService`, which already reads `RuleServicesFilter.REQUEST_ID_KEY` from another package of the same module (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:248`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:283`), so both channels share one encoding and no module boundary or dependency changes.
  - HTTP business value: `processForwarded` MUST keep setting the response header to the raw request-header value or the generated ID exactly as today (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:113`) and MUST apply `requestIdForLog` only at the `MDC.put`, so an HTTP client receives the ID it sent. A blank or absent header MUST still yield one generated random ID for both the response header and the MDC entry, an unset `log.request-id.header` MUST still write no MDC entry and no response header, and the `MDC.remove` of the key after the request (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:118`) MUST stay.
  - Kafka business value: `resolveRequestIdHeader` and the reply and dead-letter request-ID headers (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:328-330`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:464-466`) MUST keep the value they carry today, the decoded header text or the generated ID, so a UTF-8 header reaches the reply and dead-letter records with its original bytes and correlation is unchanged. A blank or absent header MUST still yield a generated random ID, and an unset `log.request-id.header` MUST still write no MDC entry and no request-ID header.
  - Test seam: `KafkaRequestIdLoggingTest` MUST reuse the `KafkaService.createService` seam that takes the Kafka `Consumer` and `Producer` interfaces and the Kafka startup fixture, both counted in SEC-38, and MUST create the seam only when it is absent.
  - Any code comment this fix adds MUST explain WHY, not WHAT.
- Tests: new `KafkaRequestIdLoggingTest` and the HTTP cases of the existing `RuleServicesFilterTest`, both server-free. `KafkaRequestIdLoggingTest` runs with the kafka-clients `MockConsumer` and `MockProducer` and no broker. Unit tests serve SLF4J through slf4j-simple, whose MDC adapter stores nothing (`pom.xml:1192-1207`), so `KafkaRequestIdLoggingTest` MUST assert rendering with log4j-core `PatternLayout` instances built from the patterns at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:10` and `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:18`, formatting events whose `requestId` context entry holds the `requestIdForLog` result. log4j-core reaches the test classpath through the module's runtime `log4j-slf4j2-impl` dependency (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/pom.xml:62-67`).
  - Encoding: a header holding CR, LF, CR LF, NUL, ESC, DEL, U+0085 and U+2028 renders through both patterns as exactly one physical line, with each of those characters escaped.
  - Bound: a 10000-character header yields its first 128 encoded characters followed by `[truncated]`, and a header whose cut falls inside an escape sequence or a surrogate pair is cut before it.
  - Normal ID: a UUID-style header and a 128-character header of printable characters render unchanged.
  - Reply and dead letter: a record whose request-ID header holds CR and LF gets a reply on the `MockProducer` whose request-ID header bytes equal the original bytes, and with a service bean that throws, every request-ID header of the dead-letter record equals the original bytes.
  - Dead-letter failure lines: with a request-ID header holding CR and LF and a service bean that answers, `errorNext` on the non-completing result `MockProducer` runs `sendErrorToDlt` on the test thread, as the SEC-40 test drives it, and the dead-letter send of one record fails through `sendException` and that of a second through `errorNext`. `MDC` is statically mocked on the test thread, because slf4j-simple keeps no MDC, and for each dead-letter failure line the mock records a `requestId` write equal to the `requestIdForLog` result and no `requestId` write holding the raw header text; with the SEC-40 writes absent, the case asserts only the absence of the raw text.
  - Absent header: a record without the header gets a reply whose request-ID header holds a generated ID that `UUID.fromString` parses and `requestIdForLog` returns unchanged.
  - `RuleServicesFilterTest` HTTP fixture: the existing class MUST gain its own fixture and NEVER depend on a fixture another directive adds to it: a `RuleServicesFilter` initialized through `init` over a mocked `FilterConfig` and `ServletContext`, with `SpringInitializer.getApplicationContext` statically mocked to return a mocked application context that holds a `MockEnvironment` for `log.request-id.header`, no `AuthorizationChecker` bean and one `AccessDeniedHandler` bean (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:75-86`), requests driven through `doFilter` into a recording `FilterChain`, and `MDC` statically mocked on the test thread, because slf4j-simple keeps no MDC.
  - HTTP hostile header: with `log.request-id.header` set, a request whose request-ID header holds CR, LF, a tab and U+0085, and a request whose header is 10000 characters long, each reach the recording chain while the `MDC` mock records exactly one `requestId` write, equal to the `requestIdForLog` result for that header, and no write holding the raw header text; the response header equals the original value, and the `MDC.remove` of the key runs after the chain returns.
  - HTTP blank or absent header: a request with a blank request-ID header and a request without it each get a response header holding a generated ID that `UUID.fromString` parses, and the `MDC` mock records a `requestId` write of that same ID.
  - HTTP unset property: with `log.request-id.header` unset, the request reaches the recording chain, the `MDC` mock records no `requestId` write and the response carries no request-ID header.
- Estimated LOC: Production: 18–34; Test: 231–366; Configuration and build: 0–0; Documentation: 0–0
- Pass/fail: the validation command passes, `KafkaRequestIdLoggingTest` proves that control characters render escaped on one physical line through both plain patterns, that an overlong header is cut at 128 encoded characters with the marker, that a normal ID renders unchanged, that the reply and dead-letter request-ID headers keep the original bytes, that each dead-letter failure line's `requestId` entry is the `requestIdForLog` result and never the raw header text, and that an absent header still yields a generated ID, and `RuleServicesFilterTest` proves that the HTTP filter's `requestId` MDC write is the `requestIdForLog` result and never the raw header text while the response header keeps the original value, that a blank or absent header yields one generated ID in both, and that an unset `log.request-id.header` writes neither.
